### crates/app/Cargo.toml

```
[package]
name = "app"
version.workspace = true
edition.workspace = true
license.workspace = true

[dependencies]
ureq = { version = "3", default-features = false, features = ["rustls"] }
semver = "1"
# Header truncation preserves grapheme clusters while fitting the font’s measured width.
unicode-segmentation = "1"
eframe.workspace = true
pulldown-cmark = { version = "0.13", default-features = false }
term-core = { path = "../term-core" }
panel-contract = { path = "../panel-contract" }

# The second transport of the panel document: the endpoint a producer speaks to instead of writing
# the file. It depends on nothing visual, exactly like the contract it carries, so the window takes it
# the same way it takes the contract — as a crate that would survive the interface being rewritten
# (D3).
panel-control = { path = "../panel-control" }

# A producer controls every string offered as a link. This parses the complete candidate before the
# app hands it to the desktop, so `http` and `https` with a host are one boundary shared by terminal
# text, OSC 8 and the panel instead of three partial string checks.
url = "2.5"

# The desktop's own opener, the one egui's `links` feature would call — named directly because that
# feature swallows its error into `log`, and a click the opener could not act on has to say why
# (D51). What it does with the address differs by platform: on Linux it runs the default browser, or
# `xdg-open` and its relatives, with the address as one argument; on macOS it asks AppKit's
# `NSWorkspace`; on Windows it runs the registered `http` handler's command line with the address
# substituted in. Under WSL it runs `cmd.exe /c start <address>`, escaping only `^` and `&` —
# which is why `links::WebTarget` percent-encodes what else `cmd` would read.
webbrowser = "1.2"

# Retirement snapshots must open a producer-controlled path without letting a FIFO block the
# interface thread. This package is already in the workspace graph; the direct dependency names the
# one Unix open flag the standard library deliberately leaves platform-specific.
libc = "0.2"

# Windows' `canonicalize` returns `\\?\C:\…`, which would become a project's key, name and path.
# This strips that prefix only where doing so keeps the meaning (D108); it is the identity on Unix.
dunce = "1.0.5"

# **So that a handle is not guessable, which is the whole of its job.** `PANEL_ID` is in every
# descendant's environment and any sibling of this user reads it out of `/proc/<pid>/environ`, so a
# name is not a credential and a small integer is reachable by a typo; a handle is sixteen random
# bytes. It adds no package to the tree: `panel-contract` already draws randomness from it to name
# each console's directory.
getrandom = "0.3"

# To find out that a producer has published without asking the disk sixty times a second.
# Its source was read before choosing it, and two decisions come out of that reading which are not
# negotiable: what is watched is each console's **directory** and not its file, and the notice only
# raises a flag.
# Its default features are all macOS ones and arrive behind a platform `cfg`, so on Linux they add
# nothing: what comes in is `inotify`, `mio`, `walkdir` and `notify-types`.
notify = "8.2"

# For the format the window's layout goes to disk and comes back in.
#
# **They do not add a single package to the tree, and that is worth saying because intuition says the
# opposite.** The root `Cargo.toml` reasons feature by feature about what it strips so that
# `clippy --workspace --all-targets` does not drag on, so «pulling in `derive`» sounds like going
# against that policy. It is not: the policy reasons about **what enters the tree**, and `serde` with
# `derive` is already compiled here, pulled in by the terminal engine — checked with
# `cargo tree -i serde_derive`, which gives
# `serde_derive → serde → alacritty_terminal → term-core → app`. `serde_json` too, through
# `panel-contract`.
#
# **And `derive` is used, unlike in `panel-contract`, for a reason and not out of taste.** That crate
# reads with `Value` because it tolerates **per field** — seven optionals, and lines that are either a
# string or an object; this file is always written by the application and is tolerated **per
# document**, so there is nothing to tolerate by hand. The only thing writing it by hand would buy is
# the walk of a recursive tree, which is exactly where a hand-written interpreter fails.
serde = { version = "1", features = ["derive"] }
serde_json = "1"

# Not used from the code: it is only here to put back a feature that fell when eframe's defaults were
# stripped. Without it, on Wayland the window is decorated by SCTK's fallback frame — which its own
# author calls «the ugly default frame» — and this milestone exists to judge how egui paints, so a
# degraded frame dirties exactly what has to be looked at.
# The version has to follow whatever eframe drags in: if the two drift apart, a second winit appears
# in the tree and this line silently stops having any effect.
winit = { version = "0.30", default-features = false, features = ["wayland-csd-adwaita"] }

# **Who knows which characters are emoji and with which presentation.** It is not a table anybody can
# write by hand or derive from a range: `Emoji_Presentation` is a Unicode property that changes with
# every version of the standard, and whether `☁` comes out as text and `☁️` in colour depends on it.
#
# **Watch out for its enum: it is `#[non_exhaustive]`.** A category added tomorrow falls into the
# default arm and comes out monochrome, without the compilation saying a word. It is accepted
# knowingly — that character would come out **the way it comes out today** — and it is written down in
# `fonts.rs`, which is where the decision is made.
unicode-properties = { version = "0.1.4", features = ["emoji"] }

# **Who knows which fonts the machine has, and which of them covers a character.** It came in as a
# development dependency when only the measurement bench used it; since the terminal really discovers
# fonts, it belongs to production.
#
# It is by the same authors as `skrifa` — which is already in the tree because egui's text engine uses
# it underneath — so the two speak the same font model instead of two of them coexisting. And it uses
# each platform's native APIs: fontconfig on Linux, DirectWrite on Windows, CoreText on macOS, which
# is what it takes not to leave debt for **F2.7**.
#
# **`fontconfig` is loaded at RUN TIME, it is not linked at compile time.** Without that feature,
# compiling this repository on Linux starts demanding `pkg-config` and fontconfig's headers — checked:
# the machine where this was written does not have them and the compilation fails in the build script,
# not in the code. It is the same technique, and the same reason, as the `dlopen` that `libwayland`
# arrives by. The price is that fontconfig's absence is discovered **at start-up** and not at compile
# time — which is exactly the case **AC9** requires the application to survive.
fontique = { version = "0.11", features = ["fontconfig-dlopen"] }

# **To check that the text engine is going to be able to open a font BEFORE handing it over.** It is
# the same crate epaint uses underneath, so whatever opens here opens there. Without that check, a
# corrupt font file — a half-finished package update, some stale fontconfig offsets — kills the
# process: epaint's only failure mode for bytes it does not understand is a panic, and it happens
# inside its own pass, where there is nothing that can catch it.
skrifa = "0.44"

# **Who shapes a keycap into the glyph that draws it.** The emoji font draws `1️⃣` as a ligature of
# the whole sequence, which only shaping reaches; mapping the digit gives its thin `1` instead. Chosen
# because it is the shaper epaint runs underneath, rather than bringing a second one into the tree.
harfrust = "0.12"

# **To decode an emoji's bitmap and shrink it to the size of a cell.** `Noto Color Emoji`'s colour
# glyphs come inside the font as **palette-indexed PNG** of 136×128 — checked by running it — so they
# have to be expanded to RGBA and rescaled; `skrifa` hands the bytes over as they are and does neither.
#
# **It adds neither a package nor a feature to the tree, and that is worth saying because intuition
# says the opposite.** `image` with the `png` feature **is already compiled here**, pulled in by the
# clipboard — checked with `cargo tree -e features -i image`, which gives
# `image/png → arboard → egui-winit → eframe → app`. It is the same argument that justifies `serde`
# with `derive` above: the root manifest's policy reasons about **what enters the tree**, not about
# how many lines the manifest has.
image = { version = "0.25", default-features = false, features = ["png"] }

# The interface's icons: Phosphor Regular and its codepoints (D103). Only the face and the constants
# are used — never `add_to_fonts`, which puts the face in the proportional chain where its blank
# lowercase letters and its private-use codepoints would meet the interface's and the terminal's text. Pinned
# exactly: a release that renumbers a glyph changes what the chrome shows without a compile error.
egui-phosphor = { version = "=0.14.0", default-features = false, features = ["regular"] }

# Clipboard reads run away from the UI; normal Copy keeps egui's existing writer.
[target.'cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))'.dependencies]
arboard = { version = "3.6.1", default-features = false }

[target.'cfg(target_os = "linux")'.dependencies]
# Data-control owns its connection and completes seat setup before accepting operations;
# unsupported compositors can use arboard's X11/XWayland fallback.
arboard = { version = "3.6.1", default-features = false, features = ["wayland-data-control"] }
# Showing a file selected in the desktop's file manager: the `FileManager1` bus service, called
# without starting a process whose arguments would need quoting (D110). The same three crates, at the
# same versions and features, that `term-core` already compiles for its scope placement.
zbus = { version = "5.13", default-features = false, features = ["async-io"] }
async-io = "2"
futures-lite = "2"

# Two uses, one table. A file's identity (volume serial number and file index), which std's Windows
# `MetadataExt` keeps behind an unstable feature, and the flag that opens a link instead of its
# target (D108). And opening a file with its default application and showing it in Explorer,
# through the shell's own calls rather than a command line `cmd` would read (D110). 0.61 is already
# in the lock; only its features are named.
[target.'cfg(windows)'.dependencies]
windows-sys = { version = "0.61.2", features = [
    "Win32_Foundation",
    "Win32_Storage_FileSystem",
    "Win32_System_Com",
    "Win32_System_Registry",
    "Win32_UI_Shell",
    "Win32_UI_Shell_Common",
    "Win32_UI_WindowsAndMessaging",
    "Win32_System_LibraryLoader",
] }
raw-window-handle = "0.6.2"

# Centring macOS's own window buttons in the integrated header (`chrome/traffic_lights.rs`). All
# three are already in the tree through eframe at these versions; only their features are named.
[target.'cfg(target_os = "macos")'.dependencies]
objc2-app-kit = { version = "0.3.2", default-features = false, features = [
    "std",
    "NSButton",
    "NSControl",
    "NSResponder",
    "NSView",
    "NSWindow",
    "objc2-core-foundation",
] }
objc2-foundation = { version = "0.3.2", default-features = false, features = ["std", "NSGeometry"] }
raw-window-handle = "0.6.2"

# **A development dependency is not «it does not touch the binary», and this is where that was
# learnt.** They take part in version resolution: adding `fontique` here — before it moved up to
# production, above — pushed `windows-sys` from 0.59 to 0.60 for `arboard`, the clipboard, which
# **does** ship in the published binary, and pulled in the `windows 0.62` stack. On Linux nothing
# changed; on Windows, which is F2.7's platform, it already changed what gets compiled.
[dev-dependencies]
host-load = { path = "../host-load" }

# **The same crate again, for its test support**: `term_core::received` reads what a console's
# program got from a file the program wrote, where the line discipline's echo cannot forge it. As a
# development dependency its feature stays out of `cargo build` and `cargo run` (resolver 3);
# `cargo tree -p app -e normal,features -i term-core` is how to see which features a build takes.
term-core = { path = "../term-core", features = ["testing"] }

# **So that the bench's tests run with `cargo test`, and not only if somebody remembers to ask for
# `--examples`.** A cargo example is not a test target by default: the `tests` module of
# `examples/fonts.rs` would be compiled and never run, which is worse than not having it — a test that
# does not run reads as coverage and is not. With this it joins the workshop's suite and CI's by the
# same path as the rest.
[[example]]
name = "fonts"
test = true

[[example]]
name = "hinting"
test = true
```

### crates/app/src/board/plans.rs

```
//! Project-shared inert plans. Parsing and persistence stay on background threads.
use crate::project::{Identity, Key};
use panel_contract::plan::Plan;
use panel_control::{Answer, What};
use std::{
    collections::HashMap,
    path::PathBuf,
    sync::{
        Arc, Mutex,
        mpsc::{self, SyncSender},
    },
    time::SystemTime,
};

mod disk;

pub(crate) struct Published {
    pub(crate) plan: Plan,
    pub(crate) document: String,
    pub(crate) published_at: SystemTime,
}

type Bank = Arc<Mutex<HashMap<Key, Arc<Published>>>>;
#[cfg(windows)]
type SnapshotReaders = Arc<
    Mutex<
        HashMap<
            usize,
            (
                Result<Identity, String>,
                panel_control::windows_services::PlanPublisher,
            ),
        >,
    >,
>;
struct Job {
    identity: Identity,
    what: What,
    answer: SyncSender<Answer>,
    #[cfg(windows)]
    _release: Option<panel_control::windows_services::ReleaseToken>,
}
#[derive(Default)]
pub(crate) struct Store {
    bank: Bank,
    sender: Option<SyncSender<Job>>,
    #[cfg(windows)]
    snapshots: SnapshotReaders,
}

impl Store {
    pub(crate) fn at(
        directory: Option<PathBuf>,
        writable: bool,
        ctx: eframe::egui::Context,
    ) -> Self {
        let bank = Bank::default();
        let shared = bank.clone();
        #[cfg(windows)]
        let snapshots = SnapshotReaders::default();
        #[cfg(windows)]
        let snapshot_writers = snapshots.clone();
        let (sender, receiver) = mpsc::sync_channel::<Job>(8);
        let spawned = std::thread::Builder::new()
            .name("project-plans".into())
            .spawn(move || {
                let mut disk = disk::Disk::new(directory, writable);
                let restored = disk.restore();
                *shared.lock().unwrap() = restored;
                #[cfg(windows)]
                publish_all_snapshots(&shared, &snapshot_writers);
                ctx.request_repaint();
                // Bounded: dropping the Store drops its sole sender; after queued jobs drain,
                // `recv` returns disconnected. The worker is not joined, so the UI does not wait.
                #[allow(unused_mut)]
                while let Ok(mut job) = receiver.recv() {
                    let answer = apply(&shared, &mut disk, &job.identity, job.what);
                    #[cfg(windows)]
                    publish_matching_snapshots(&shared, &snapshot_writers, &job.identity.key);
                    #[cfg(windows)]
                    drop(job._release.take());
                    let _ = job.answer.send(answer);
                    ctx.request_repaint();
                }
            });
        if let Err(error) = spawned {
            eprintln!("Project plans unavailable: {error}");
            return Self::default();
        }
        Self {
            bank,
            sender: Some(sender),
            #[cfg(windows)]
            snapshots,
        }
    }

    pub(crate) fn published(&self, key: &Key) -> Option<Arc<Published>> {
        self.bank.lock().unwrap().get(key).cloned()
    }

    #[cfg(windows)]
    fn install_snapshot(
        &self,
        pane: usize,
        identity: Result<Identity, String>,
        publisher: panel_control::windows_services::PlanPublisher,
    ) {
        let mut snapshots = self
            .snapshots
            .lock()
            .unwrap_or_else(|poisoned| poisoned.into_inner());
        // Lock subscribers before reading the bank. A concurrent commit updates the bank, releases
        // that lock, then takes the subscriber lock to fan out; either this read sees the commit or
        // the fanout sees the newly installed subscriber.
        let current = snapshot_value(&self.bank, &identity);
        publish_snapshot(&publisher, current);
        snapshots.insert(pane, (identity, publisher));
    }

    #[cfg(windows)]
    fn retain_snapshots(&self, living: &[usize]) {
        self.snapshots
            .lock()
            .unwrap_or_else(|poisoned| poisoned.into_inner())
            .retain(|pane, (_, publisher)| living.contains(pane) && publisher.is_alive());
    }

    pub(crate) fn request(
        &mut self,
        identity: Result<Identity, String>,
        what: What,
        answer: SyncSender<Answer>,
        ctx: &eframe::egui::Context,
        #[cfg(windows)] mut release: Option<panel_control::windows_services::ReleaseToken>,
    ) {
        let identity = match identity {
            Ok(identity) => identity,
            Err(error) => {
                #[cfg(windows)]
                drop(release.take());
                let _ = answer.send(Answer::Refused(error));
                return;
            }
        };
        if self.sender.is_none() {
            *self = Self::at(None, false, ctx.clone());
        }
        let Some(sender) = &self.sender else {
            #[cfg(windows)]
            drop(release.take());
            let _ = answer.send(Answer::Refused("project plan worker is unavailable".into()));
            return;
        };
        if let Err(error) = sender.try_send(Job {
            identity,
            what,
            answer,
            #[cfg(windows)]
            _release: release,
        }) {
            #[allow(unused_mut)]
            let mut job = match error {
                mpsc::TrySendError::Full(job) | mpsc::TrySendError::Disconnected(job) => job,
            };
            #[cfg(windows)]
            drop(job._release.take());
            let _ = job.answer.send(Answer::Refused(
                "project plan worker is busy or unavailable".into(),
            ));
        }
    }
}

fn apply(bank: &Bank, disk: &mut disk::Disk, identity: &Identity, what: What) -> Answer {
    let name = &identity.name;
    match what {
        What::PlanSet {
            document,
            plan,
            dropped,
        } => {
            let published_at = SystemTime::now();
            let saved = disk.save(identity, &document, published_at);
            bank.lock().unwrap().insert(
                identity.key.clone(),
                Arc::new(Published {
                    plan,
                    document,
                    published_at,
                }),
            );
            let mut message = format!("published for {name}");
            if let Some(note) = dropped.sentence() {
                message.push_str(&format!("; {note}"));
            }
            if let Err(reason) = saved {
                message.push_str(&format!(" (not saved: {reason})"));
            }
            Answer::Text(message)
        }
        What::PlanGet => match bank.lock().unwrap().get(&identity.key) {
            Some(plan) => Answer::Document(plan.document.clone()),
            None => Answer::Text(format!("no plan published for {name}")),
        },
        What::PlanRetire => {
            if !bank.lock().unwrap().contains_key(&identity.key) {
                return Answer::Text(format!("no plan published for {name}"));
            }
            if let Err(reason) = disk.retire(identity) {
                return Answer::Refused(reason);
            }
            bank.lock().unwrap().remove(&identity.key);
            Answer::Text(format!("retired for {name}"))
        }
        _ => Answer::Refused("that request is not a project plan request".into()),
    }
}

pub(crate) fn identity(window: &crate::Window, origin: usize) -> Result<Identity, String> {
    from_badge(
        window
            .all_panes()
            .find(|p| p.name == origin)
            .and_then(|p| p.project.as_ref()),
    )
}

fn from_badge(badge: Option<&crate::project_view::Badge>) -> Result<Identity, String> {
    let badge = badge.ok_or("this console's project is not known yet")?;
    match &badge.key {
        Key::Repository(path) => Ok(Identity {
            key: badge.key.clone(),
            name: badge.name.clone(),
            path: path.clone(),
        }),
        Key::Folder(_) => Err("this console is not in a Git repository".into()),
    }
}

pub(crate) fn dispatch(
    window: &mut crate::Window,
    origin: usize,
    delivery: panel_control::Delivery,
    ctx: &eframe::egui::Context,
) -> Option<panel_control::Delivery> {
    if !matches!(
        delivery.what,
        What::PlanSet { .. } | What::PlanGet | What::PlanRetire
    ) {
        return Some(delivery);
    }
    let identity = identity(window, origin);
    window.board.plans.request(
        identity,
        delivery.what,
        delivery.answer,
        ctx,
        #[cfg(windows)]
        delivery.release,
    );
    None
}

pub(crate) fn refresh_readers(window: &mut crate::Window) {
    #[cfg(not(windows))]
    let bank = window.board.plans.bank.clone();
    #[cfg(windows)]
    let living = window.all_panes().map(|pane| pane.name).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    #[cfg(windows)]
    window.board.plans.retain_snapshots(&living);
    #[cfg(windows)]
    let mut registrations = Vec::new();
    for pane in window.all_panes_mut() {
        let identity = from_badge(pane.project.as_ref());
        let Some(control) = pane.control.as_mut() else {
            continue;
        };
        #[cfg(not(windows))]
        if control.plan_identity.as_ref() == Some(&identity) {
            continue;
        }
        control.plan_identity = Some(identity.clone());
        #[cfg(windows)]
        {
            registrations.push((
                pane.name,
                identity.clone(),
                control.windows_plan_publisher(),
            ));
            continue;
        }
        #[cfg(not(windows))]
        {
            let bank = bank.clone();
            let read: panel_control::listener::ReadPlan = Arc::new(move || {
                let identity = match &identity {
                    Ok(identity) => identity,
                    Err(why) => return panel_control::Reply::Failed(why.clone()),
                };
                let published = bank.lock().unwrap().get(&identity.key).cloned();
                match published {
                    Some(plan) => panel_control::Reply::Document(plan.document.clone()),
                    None => panel_control::Reply::Text(format!(
                        "no plan published for {}",
                        identity.name
                    )),
                }
            });
            #[cfg(not(windows))]
            control.install_plan_reader(read);
        }
    }
    #[cfg(windows)]
    for (pane, identity, publisher) in registrations {
        window
            .board
            .plans
            .install_snapshot(pane, identity, publisher);
    }
}

#[cfg(windows)]
fn snapshot_value(bank: &Bank, identity: &Result<Identity, String>) -> Answer {
    match identity {
        Ok(identity) => match bank
            .lock()
            .unwrap_or_else(|poisoned| poisoned.into_inner())
            .get(&identity.key)
            .cloned()
        {
            Some(plan) => Answer::Document(plan.document.clone()),
            None => Answer::Text(format!("no plan published for {}", identity.name)),
        },
        Err(why) => Answer::Refused(why.clone()),
    }
}

#[cfg(windows)]
fn publish_snapshot(publisher: &panel_control::windows_services::PlanPublisher, answer: Answer) {
    let result = match answer {
        Answer::Document(document) => publisher.publish_document(document),
        Answer::Text(text) => publisher.publish_text(text),
        Answer::Refused(message) => publisher.publish_failed(message),
        _ => return,
    };
    if let Err(problem) = result {
        eprintln!("Windows plan snapshot was not updated: {problem}");
    }
}

#[cfg(windows)]
fn publish_matching_snapshots(bank: &Bank, snapshots: &SnapshotReaders, key: &Key) {
    let matches = snapshots
        .lock()
        .unwrap_or_else(|poisoned| poisoned.into_inner())
        .values()
        .filter(|(identity, _)| identity.as_ref().is_ok_and(|identity| &identity.key == key))
        .cloned()
        .collect::<Vec<_>>();
    for (identity, publisher) in matches {
        publish_snapshot(&publisher, snapshot_value(bank, &identity));
    }
}

#[cfg(windows)]
fn publish_all_snapshots(bank: &Bank, snapshots: &SnapshotReaders) {
    let all = snapshots
        .lock()
        .unwrap_or_else(|poisoned| poisoned.into_inner())
        .values()
        .cloned()
        .collect::<Vec<_>>();
    for (identity, publisher) in all {
        publish_snapshot(&publisher, snapshot_value(bank, &identity));
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests;

#![cfg(test)]
use super::*;
use std::{
    fs,
    sync::mpsc,
    time::{Duration, UNIX_EPOCH},
};
fn identity() -> Identity {
    Identity {
        key: Key::Repository("/sample/.git".into()),
        path: "/sample/.git".into(),
        name: "Sample".into(),
    }
}
fn set(text: &str) -> What {
    let (plan, dropped) = panel_contract::plan::parse_document_noting(text).unwrap();
    What::PlanSet {
        document: text.into(),
        plan,
        dropped,
    }
}
fn temp() -> PathBuf {
    let mut bytes = [0; 8];
    getrandom::fill(&mut bytes).unwrap();
    let p = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("ivy-plan-test-{:x}", u64::from_ne_bytes(bytes)));
    fs::create_dir(&p).unwrap();
    p
}
#[test]
fn committed_plan_roundtrips_without_republication_and_retirement_removes_it() {
    let directory = temp();
    let bank = Bank::default();
    let identity = identity();
    let document = r#"{"title":"Test","milestones":[],"producerExtra":true}"#;
    let mut disk = disk::Disk::new(Some(directory.clone()), true);
    assert!(
        matches!(apply(&bank,&mut disk,&identity,set(document)),Answer::Text(s) if s=="published for Sample")
    );
    let at = bank
        .lock()
        .unwrap()
        .get(&identity.key)
        .unwrap()
        .published_at;
    let mut restored_disk = disk::Disk::new(Some(directory.clone()), true);
    let restored = restored_disk.restore();
    let published = restored.get(&identity.key).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(published.document, document);
    assert_eq!(
        published
            .published_at
            .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
            .unwrap()
            .as_secs(),
        at.duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH).unwrap().as_secs()
    );
    let restored = Arc::new(Mutex::new(restored));
    assert!(
        matches!(apply(&restored,&mut restored_disk,&identity,What::PlanGet),Answer::Document(s) if s==document)
    );
    assert!(
        matches!(apply(&restored,&mut restored_disk,&identity,What::PlanRetire),Answer::Text(s) if s=="retired for Sample")
    );
    assert!(restored_disk.restore().is_empty());
    assert!(
        matches!(apply(&restored,&mut restored_disk,&identity,What::PlanGet),Answer::Text(s) if s=="no plan published for Sample")
    );
    fs::remove_dir_all(directory).unwrap();
}
#[test]
fn worker_answers_from_committed_state_and_names_dropped_words() {
    let ctx = eframe::egui::Context::default();
    let mut store = Store::at(None, false, ctx.clone());
    let (tx, rx) = mpsc::sync_channel(1);
    store.request(
        Ok(identity()),
        set(r#"{"milestones":[{"title":"M","items":[{"text":"T","mark":"magic"}]}]}"#),
        tx,
        &ctx,
        #[cfg(windows)]
        None,
    );
    assert!(
        matches!(rx.recv_timeout(Duration::from_secs(3)).unwrap(),Answer::Text(s) if s.contains("published for Sample")&&s.contains("magic")&&s.contains("not saved"))
    );
    assert!(store.published(&identity().key).is_some());
    let (tx, rx) = mpsc::sync_channel(1);
    store.request(
        Err("this console's project is not known yet".into()),
        What::PlanRetire,
        tx,
        &ctx,
        #[cfg(windows)]
        None,
    );
    assert!(matches!(rx.recv().unwrap(), Answer::Refused(_)));
    assert!(store.published(&identity().key).is_some());
}
#[test]
fn unknown_version_is_preserved_and_measurement_never_overwrites() {
    let directory = temp();
    let identity = identity();
    let mut disk = disk::Disk::new(Some(directory.clone()), true);
    disk.save(
        &identity,
        "{\"milestones\":[]}",
        UNIX_EPOCH + Duration::from_secs(100),
    )
    .unwrap();
    let path = fs::read_dir(&directory)
        .unwrap()
        .next()
        .unwrap()
        .unwrap()
        .path();
    let original = fs::read_to_string(&path)
        .unwrap()
        .replace("\"v\":1", "\"v\":2");
    fs::write(&path, &original).unwrap();
    assert!(disk.restore().is_empty());
    assert!(disk.save(&identity, "{}", SystemTime::now()).is_err());
    assert!(disk.retire(&identity).is_err());
    assert_eq!(fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap(), original);
    let mut measured = disk::Disk::new(Some(directory.clone()), false);
    assert!(measured.save(&identity, "{}", SystemTime::now()).is_err());
    assert_eq!(fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap(), original);
    fs::remove_dir_all(directory).unwrap();
}

#[cfg(windows)]
#[test]
fn windows_control_plan_snapshots_follow_commit_retire_and_revoke_off_frame() {
    let mut store = Store::at(None, false, eframe::egui::Context::default());
    let identity = identity();
    let (first_reader, first_publisher) = panel_control::windows_services::PlanSnapshot::new();
    let (second_reader, second_publisher) = panel_control::windows_services::PlanSnapshot::new();
    store.install_snapshot(11, Ok(identity.clone()), first_publisher.clone());
    store.install_snapshot(12, Ok(identity.clone()), second_publisher.clone());
    for reader in [&first_reader, &second_reader] {
        assert!(matches!(
            reader.read(),
            Ok(panel_control::Reply::Text(text)) if text == "no plan published for Sample"
        ));
    }

    let document = r#"{"title":"Live","milestones":[]}"#;
    let ctx = eframe::egui::Context::default();
    let (answer, received) = mpsc::sync_channel(1);
    store.request(Ok(identity.clone()), set(document), answer, &ctx, None);
    assert!(matches!(received.recv().unwrap(), Answer::Text(_)));
    for reader in [&first_reader, &second_reader] {
        assert_eq!(
            reader.read().unwrap(),
            panel_control::Reply::Document(document.to_owned())
        );
    }

    let (answer, received) = mpsc::sync_channel(1);
    store.request(Ok(identity), What::PlanRetire, answer, &ctx, None);
    assert!(matches!(received.recv().unwrap(), Answer::Text(_)));
    for reader in [&first_reader, &second_reader] {
        assert!(matches!(
            reader.read(),
            Ok(panel_control::Reply::Text(text)) if text == "no plan published for Sample"
        ));
    }

    first_publisher.revoke();
    store.retain_snapshots(&[12]);
    assert_eq!(store.snapshots.lock().unwrap().len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(first_reader.read().unwrap_err(), "this pane is gone");
}

#[cfg(windows)]
#[test]
fn windows_control_plan_snapshot_registration_cannot_miss_a_concurrent_commit() {
    let store = Arc::new(Store::default());
    let identity = identity();
    let (reader, publisher) = panel_control::windows_services::PlanSnapshot::new();
    let barrier = Arc::new(std::sync::Barrier::new(2));
    let registering = {
        let store = Arc::clone(&store);
        let identity = identity.clone();
        let publisher = publisher.clone();
        let barrier = Arc::clone(&barrier);
        std::thread::spawn(move || {
            barrier.wait();
            store.install_snapshot(21, Ok(identity), publisher);
        })
    };
    let committing = {
        let bank = store.bank.clone();
        let snapshots = store.snapshots.clone();
        let identity = identity.clone();
        let barrier = Arc::clone(&barrier);
        std::thread::spawn(move || {
            barrier.wait();
            let mut disk = disk::Disk::new(None, false);
            apply(
                &bank,
                &mut disk,
                &identity,
                set(r#"{"title":"Raced","milestones":[]}"#),
            );
            publish_matching_snapshots(&bank, &snapshots, &identity.key);
        })
    };
    registering.join().unwrap();
    committing.join().unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        reader.read().unwrap(),
        panel_control::Reply::Document(r#"{"title":"Raced","milestones":[]}"#.to_owned())
    );
}

```
//! In-app setup help reports published panels, never inferred client installation (D73).
mod recovery;
mod translocation;
use crate::{Window, panel};
use eframe::egui::{self, Color32, Rect};
use std::{path::PathBuf, time::Instant};

// Recovery setup is deferred; retain the renderer for later reactivation.
const SHOW_SESSION_RECOVERY: bool = false;

const APPLYING_PREFERENCES: &str = "Saved. Applying settings...";
const BRIDGE_KEY: &str = "ivy-mcp-bridge-location";
#[cfg(windows)]
const CODEX_ENV: &str = "env_vars = [\"PANEL_ID\", \"PANEL_DIR\", \"PANEL_SERVER_PID\"]";
#[cfg(not(windows))]
const CODEX_ENV: &str = "env_vars = [\"PANEL_ID\", \"PANEL_DIR\"]";
#[cfg(windows)]
const CLIENT_IDENTITY: &str = "PANEL_ID, PANEL_DIR, and PANEL_SERVER_PID";
#[cfg(not(windows))]
const CLIENT_IDENTITY: &str = "PANEL_ID and PANEL_DIR";
#[cfg(windows)]
const IDENTITY_WARNING: &str = "Do not hard-code PANEL_ID, PANEL_DIR, or PANEL_SERVER_PID in the client settings; each console supplies its own.";
#[cfg(not(windows))]
const IDENTITY_WARNING: &str =
    "Do not hard-code PANEL_ID or PANEL_DIR in the client settings; each console supplies its own.";
/// A client may report success without proving it (D106): setup is confirmed by what the person reads
/// in the client's own MCP list, never by the agent's answer.
const SELF_CHECK: &str = "Open the client's MCP server list yourself (in Codex or Claude, type /mcp) and confirm that ivy is listed as connected with its tools. A client or agent saying it is connected is not enough.";
const PROMPT: &str = "If this client did not pass you the bridge's server instructions, use Ivy's panel tools to publish your current task and update it when the phase or attention changes. Set attention to wait when you need my answer, block when an external dependency stops the task, and done when the work is finished. Omit attention during ordinary progress. An agent working for another agent in the same console must leave the panel alone. An agent started in a console of its own keeps its own panel.";

#[derive(Clone, Default)]
struct Bridge {
    path: Option<PathBuf>,
    found: bool,
    /// Ivy runs from a per-launch copy; no path beside it may be offered for a client to save.
    translocated: bool,
}

impl Bridge {
    fn check() -> Self {
        Self::beside(std::env::current_exe().ok(), cfg!(target_os = "macos"))
    }

    fn beside(executable: Option<PathBuf>, macos: bool) -> Self {
        let translocated = macos
            && executable
                .as_deref()
                .is_some_and(translocation::translocated);
        if translocated {
            return Self {
                path: None,
                found: false,
                translocated,
            };
        }
        let path = executable.map(|exe| {
            exe.with_file_name(if cfg!(windows) {
                "panel-mcp.exe"
            } else {
                "panel-mcp"
            })
        });
        let found = path.as_ref().is_some_and(|path| path.is_file());
        Self {
            path,
            found,
            translocated,
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
struct Published {
    consoles: usize,
    documents: usize,
    live: usize,
    observed: [usize; 3],
}

#[derive(Default, Debug)]
pub(super) struct Guide {
    open: bool,
    blocked_frame: Option<u64>,
    awaiting_release: bool,
    published: Published,
    recovery: recovery::Status,
    manual_open: bool,
    copied: Option<&'static str>,
    desired: crate::agent_signals::Enabled,
    save_preferences: bool,
    install_plugin: bool,
    message: String,
}

impl Guide {
    fn application_status(&mut self, applying: bool) {
        if self.message == APPLYING_PREFERENCES && !applying {
            self.message = "Saved and applied.".into();
        }
    }
    pub(super) fn blocked(&self, ctx: &egui::Context) -> bool {
        self.open || self.awaiting_release || self.blocked_frame == Some(ctx.cumulative_frame_nr())
    }
}

pub(crate) fn prepare(ctx: &egui::Context) {
    super::branding::install_logo(ctx);
    crate::agent_signals::logos::install(ctx);
    let bridge = Bridge::check();
    ctx.data_mut(|data| data.insert_temp(egui::Id::new(BRIDGE_KEY), bridge));
}

fn published(window: &Window) -> Published {
    let mut result = Published::default();
    for tab in &window.tabs {
        tab.root.walk(&mut |pane| {
            result.consoles += 1;
            result.documents += usize::from(panel::summary::has_document(&pane.panel_state));
            result.live += usize::from(pane.panel_state.held_by().is_some());
            if let Some(view) = pane.signals.view(Instant::now()) {
                let index = match view.client {
                    crate::agent_signals::Client::Codex => 0,
                    crate::agent_signals::Client::Claude => 1,
                    crate::agent_signals::Client::OpenCode => 2,
                };
                result.observed[index] += 1;
            }
        });
    }
    result
}

fn indicator_rect(row: Rect) -> Option<Rect> {
    use super::strip;
    let compact = strip::is_compact(row.width());
    // Its own slot on a full bar, or the slot left of a compact bar's menu.
    let right = if compact {
        row.right() - strip::COMPACT_MENU
    } else {
        strip::slots(row).connect.right()
    };
    let left = right - strip::BUTTON;
    let limit = row.left() + strip::left_offset() + if compact { 4.0 } else { strip::LEFT_GROUP };
    (left >= limit && row.height() > 14.0).then(|| {
        Rect::from_min_max(
            egui::pos2(left, row.top() + 7.0),
            egui::pos2(right, row.bottom() - 7.0),
        )
    })
}

pub(super) fn compact_reserve(row: Rect) -> f32 {
    if indicator_rect(row).is_some() {
        super::strip::COMPACT_MENU + super::strip::BUTTON
    } else {
        super::strip::COMPACT_MENU
    }
}

pub(super) fn compact_button(window: &mut Window, ui: &egui::Ui, row: Rect) {
    let roles = crate::ui::style::roles(ui.ctx(), &window.theme);
    if super::strip::is_compact(row.width())
        && let Some(rect) = indicator_rect(row)
        && super::button(ui, rect, "", "Connect agents", roles.hover, roles.tx2)
    {
        open(window, ui.ctx(), false);
    }
}

/// The neutral link icon opens help; unpublished panels do not diagnose client setup.
pub(super) fn paint_icon(painter: &egui::Painter, rect: Rect, fg: Color32) {
    crate::ui::icons::paint(
        painter,
        rect.center(),
        crate::ui::icons::Icon::Connect,
        15.0,
        fg,
    );
}

pub(super) fn open(window: &mut Window, ctx: &egui::Context, board_handoff: bool) {
    if window.settings_editor.is_open()
        || window.chrome.updates.blocked(ctx)
        || (crate::board::blocked(window, ctx) && !board_handoff)
        || window.pane_view.is_modal()
    {
        return;
    }
    window.chrome.guide.desired = window.chrome.agent_preferences;
    window.chrome.guide.message.clear();
    window.chrome.guide.open = true;
    window.chrome.guide.copied = None;
    window.chrome.guide.blocked_frame = Some(ctx.cumulative_frame_nr());
    window.chrome.menu = false;
    crate::board::cancel_gestures(window, ctx);
    claim_input(ctx);
    ctx.request_repaint();
}

fn claim_input(ctx: &egui::Context) {
    ctx.input_mut(|input| {
        input.events.retain(|event| {
            matches!(
                event,
                egui::Event::ModifiersChanged(_)
                    | egui::Event::WindowFocused(_)
                    | egui::Event::PointerButton { pressed: false, .. }
            )
        })
    });
}

pub(crate) fn begin_pass(window: &mut Window, ui: &mut egui::Ui, frame: &eframe::Frame) {
    #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
    if window.chrome.enabled() {
        let started = Instant::now();
        let header = f64::from(window.chrome.title_sizes.bar_height() * ui.ctx().zoom_factor());
        if super::traffic_lights::center(frame, header) {
            window.chrome.buttons_moved = window.chrome.buttons_moved.saturating_add(1);
            if window.chrome.buttons_moved <= 3 {
                ui.ctx().request_repaint();
            }
        } else {
            window.chrome.buttons_moved = 0;
        }
        window.chrome.record_work(started.elapsed());
    }
    #[cfg(not(target_os = "macos"))]
    let _ = frame;
    crate::settings::editor::runtime::begin_pass(window, ui.ctx());
    super::typography::begin_pass(window, ui.ctx());
    super::updates::begin_pass(window, ui);
    if !window.chrome.enabled() || !window.chrome.guide.blocked(ui.ctx()) {
        return;
    }
    let started = Instant::now();
    let ctx = ui.ctx();
    window
        .chrome
        .guide
        .application_status(window.settings_editor.is_applying());
    if window.chrome.guide.open {
        show(&mut window.chrome.guide, ctx, &window.theme);
        if std::mem::take(&mut window.chrome.guide.save_preferences) {
            let desired = window.chrome.guide.desired;
            window.chrome.guide.message =
                match crate::settings::editor::runtime::save_agent_preferences(window, desired) {
                    Ok(()) => APPLYING_PREFERENCES.into(),
                    Err(error) => error,
                };
        }
        if std::mem::take(&mut window.chrome.guide.install_plugin) {
            window.chrome.guide.message = match crate::settings::path()
                .and_then(|p| p.parent().map(std::path::Path::to_path_buf))
            {
                Some(dir) => match crate::agent_setup::install(&dir) {
                    Ok(path) => format!("Installed {}", path.display()),
                    Err(error) => format!("Installation failed: {error}"),
                },
                None => "The configuration directory is unavailable.".into(),
            };
        }
    }
    window.chrome.guide.blocked_frame = Some(ctx.cumulative_frame_nr());
    window.chrome.guide.awaiting_release = ctx.input(|i| i.pointer.any_down());
    window
        .header_input
        .block_for_settings(ctx.cumulative_frame_nr());
    claim_input(ctx);
    ui.disable();
    window.chrome.record_work(started.elapsed());
    window.timed_separately += window.chrome.take_cost();
}

/// Paint after every console's refresh, including background tabs (D13).
pub(crate) fn after_panels(window: &mut Window, ui: &mut egui::Ui, _area: Rect) {
    if let Some(dock) = window.sidebar.last_dock {
        crate::board::paint(window, ui, dock);
    } else {
        crate::board::observe(window, ui.ctx());
    }
    if !window.chrome.enabled() {
        return;
    }
    let started = Instant::now();
    crate::agent_signals::logos::arm_stale(window, ui.ctx());
    let current = published(window);
    if current != window.chrome.guide.published && window.chrome.guide.open {
        ui.ctx().request_repaint();
    }
    window.chrome.guide.published = current;
    if window.chrome.guide.open {
        let recovery = recovery::status(window);
        if recovery != window.chrome.guide.recovery {
            ui.ctx().request_repaint();
        }
        window.chrome.guide.recovery = recovery;
    }
    let row = super::strip(window, ui.max_rect()).row;
    if let Some(rect) = indicator_rect(row) {
        let roles = crate::ui::style::roles(ui.ctx(), &window.theme);
        let hovered = ui
            .ctx()
            .pointer_hover_pos()
            .is_some_and(|p| rect.contains(p));
        let painter = ui.painter_at(rect);
        // The same surfaces as the title bar's other buttons, which this repaint covers.
        painter.rect_filled(rect, 0.0, roles.chrome);
        if hovered {
            painter.rect_filled(rect.shrink(2.0), 4.0, roles.hover);
        }
        paint_icon(&painter, rect, roles.tx2);
    }
    window.chrome.record_work(started.elapsed());
    window.timed_separately += window.chrome.take_cost();
}

fn show(guide: &mut Guide, ctx: &egui::Context, theme: &crate::theme::Theme) {
    let mut close = false;
    let available = ctx.content_rect().size();
    egui::Modal::new(egui::Id::new("connect-agents"))
        .frame(
            crate::ui::floating::dialog_frame(&crate::ui::style::roles(ctx, theme))
                .inner_margin(16),
        )
        .show(ctx, |ui| {
            crate::ui::floating::style(ui, theme);
            // The chrome's dialogs read at Settings' measures (D67's native review).
            crate::settings::editor::view::settings_geometry(ui.style_mut());
            ui.set_width((available.x - 64.0).clamp(80.0, 560.0));
            close |= crate::settings::editor::view::dialog::header(ui, "Connect agents");
            egui::ScrollArea::vertical()
                .id_salt("agent-setup-scroll")
                .max_height((available.y - 150.0).max(40.0))
                .min_scrolled_height(if guide.manual_open {
                    (available.y - 150.0).clamp(40.0, 600.0)
                } else {
                    40.0
                })
                .show(ui, |ui| body(guide, ui));
        });
    if close || ctx.input(|i| i.key_pressed(egui::Key::Escape)) {
        guide.open = false;
        ctx.memory_mut(|memory| memory.stop_text_input());
        ctx.request_repaint();
    }
}

fn copy_button(
    ui: &mut egui::Ui,
    label: &'static str,
    copied: &mut Option<&'static str>,
    text: impl FnOnce() -> String,
) {
    ui.horizontal_wrapped(|ui| {
        if ui
            .button(label)
            .on_hover_cursor(egui::CursorIcon::PointingHand)
            .clicked()
        {
            ui.ctx().copy_text(text());
            *copied = Some(label);
        }
        if *copied == Some(label) {
            ui.label("Copied");
        }
    });
}

fn copyable(ui: &mut egui::Ui, text: &str, label: &'static str, copied: &mut Option<&'static str>) {
    ui.add(egui::Label::new(egui::RichText::new(text).monospace()).wrap());
    copy_button(ui, label, copied, || text.to_owned());
}

fn manual(published: Published, copied: &mut Option<&'static str>, ui: &mut egui::Ui) {
    if published.documents == 0 {
        ui.strong("No panels published yet");
    } else {
        ui.strong(format!(
            "Panels published: {} of {} consoles",
            published.documents, published.consoles
        ));
    }
    ui.label(format!("Live publishing connections: {}", published.live));
    ui.label("This is observed panel activity, not a check of your agent's MCP configuration. A regular shell does not need a panel.");
    ui.separator();
    ui.strong("1. Locate the MCP bridge");
    let mut bridge = ui
        .ctx()
        .data(|data| data.get_temp::<Bridge>(egui::Id::new(BRIDGE_KEY)))
        .unwrap_or_default();
    // D106: a translocated launch gets no path and no re-check, because only reopening Ivy from
    // where Finder moved it can change the answer.
    if let Some(path) = &bridge.path {
        ui.label(if bridge.found {
            "Bridge file found beside Ivy (last check)."
        } else {
            "Bridge file not found beside Ivy (last check). It may be installed elsewhere."
        });
        copyable(ui, &path.to_string_lossy(), "Copy bridge path", copied);
    }
    if !bridge.translocated && ui.button("Check bridge again").clicked() {
        bridge = Bridge::check();
        ui.ctx()
            .data_mut(|data| data.insert_temp(egui::Id::new(BRIDGE_KEY), bridge.clone()));
    }
    ui.label(
        "When building from source, run this in the Ivy repository to build both executables:",
    );
    copyable(
        ui,
        "cargo build --locked --workspace --release",
        "Copy build command",
        copied,
    );
    ui.separator();
    ui.strong("2. Register it in your AI client");
    ui.label(if bridge.translocated {
        "Add a local stdio MCP server named ivy in your client's MCP settings after reopening Ivy as the notice above says, and set its executable to the panel-mcp path this guide shows then. No server URL is needed."
    } else {
        "Add a local stdio MCP server named ivy in your client's MCP settings. Set its executable to the full panel-mcp path above, or choose its actual installed location. No server URL is needed."
    });
    ui.label(format!(
        "{IDENTITY_WARNING} Ivy does not edit client settings or run these commands for you."
    ));
    ui.strong("Codex: forward the console identity");
    ui.label("In ~/.codex/config.toml, add this to the existing Ivy stdio server table (for example, [mcp_servers.ivy]). Reuse its existing name and preserve its command and other settings; do not add a duplicate server.");
    copyable(ui, CODEX_ENV, "Copy Codex environment setting", copied);
    ui.label("These are variable names, not saved console values. Tool discovery alone does not prove a connection to an Ivy console.");
    ui.separator();
    ui.strong("3. Start the agent in an Ivy console");
    ui.label("Restart or reconnect the client's MCP session from that console. In its MCP tools list, look for panel_set and panel_get. They must come from this local bridge.");
    ui.label("In a newly opened Ivy Bash, Zsh or Fish console, type codex normally. On Linux, supported sessions use the shared daemon while each conversation keeps its console panel. Compatibility is checked through client capabilities and the live protocol, not version numbers.");
    ui.label("Existing codex aliases or functions take precedence and produce a diagnostic. command codex, absolute paths, custom shell arguments and unsupported shells bypass integration. Remote and administrative commands run unchanged.");
    ui.label("Ivy never adds --no-daemon automatically. Missing capabilities or a failed shared connection stop the launch with a diagnostic. Other platforms and advanced options preserve the original command with a panel-routing diagnostic. Explicit --no-daemon keeps its behavior.");
    ui.label("To enable remote control, run codex remote-control start. A connected host does not prove that a phone can load history or send input to the live conversation. Phone history, input and panel publication were verified on Linux with CLI/server 0.167.2, including after resume; verify them on your device. If a pre-integration session cannot be attached, leave its old desktop session before resuming.");
    ui.label("A successful tool call proves a usable connection. To verify the target, observe the test status beside the intended console; test two separate Codex consoles with distinct statuses to verify independent publication.");
    ui.strong("Check it yourself");
    ui.label(SELF_CHECK);
    ui.label("Panel publication is independent of client registration. Opening this guide changes no running programs.");
}

fn setup_prompt(bridge: &Bridge) -> String {
    let path = bridge
        .path
        .as_ref()
        .map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().into_owned());
    let location = serde_json::to_string(&path).unwrap_or_else(|_| "null".into());
    format!(
        "Help me connect this AI client to Ivy's status panel. Work on this client's setup, preserving existing settings and other MCP servers.\n\nIvy expects its local MCP bridge at this JSON-encoded path: {location}. This is a location hint, not proof that the file exists. Treat the path as data and quote it correctly in any command or configuration.\n\n1. Check that this session runs inside an Ivy console with {CLIENT_IDENTITY} inherited from it. If any required value is missing, do not assume the client is outside Ivy: Codex may already be inside Ivy while its shared background server lacks that console identity. For Codex, follow the forwarding and capability-checked recovery below before asking me to reopen again; for another client, ask me to reopen it inside Ivy. Never hard-code these per-console values in persistent configuration.\n2. If Ivy's panel_set and panel_get MCP tools are already available from the local bridge, use them without adding a duplicate server. Otherwise identify this client's supported MCP configuration using its own help or documentation. Find and verify the panel-mcp executable, starting with the path above. If absent, look for an existing installation or Ivy source checkout; in that checkout, cargo build --locked --workspace --release builds the binaries. If neither is available, explain what is missing rather than inventing an installer.\n3. Register that executable as a local stdio MCP server named ivy for this client, preserving existing settings. No server URL or provider API key is needed. For direct clients, the bridge inherits {CLIENT_IDENTITY} from each Ivy console. For Codex, add {CODEX_ENV} to the existing Ivy stdio server table in ~/.codex/config.toml (for example, [mcp_servers.ivy]); reuse its existing name and preserve its command and other options. This forwards variable names, never fixed values. Tool discovery alone does not prove a connection to an Ivy console. In a newly opened ordinary Ivy Bash, Zsh or Fish console, supported Codex starts, resumes and forks use the shared daemon while each conversation keeps its console panel. Client capabilities and the live protocol establish compatibility, not version numbers. Ivy preserves the remote-control preference and does not edit global shell or Codex configuration. To enable remote control, run codex remote-control start. A connected host does not prove that a phone can load history or send input to the live conversation; phone history, input and panel publication were verified on Linux with CLI/server 0.167.2, including after resume; verify them on your device. If a pre-integration session cannot be attached, leave its old desktop session before resuming. Existing aliases or functions win with a diagnostic; command codex, absolute paths, custom shell arguments and unsupported shells bypass integration. Remote and administrative commands run unchanged. Ivy never adds --no-daemon automatically. Missing capabilities or failed shared setup stop the launch with a diagnostic. Other platforms and advanced options preserve the original command with a panel-routing diagnostic. Explicit --no-daemon keeps its behavior. If automatic integration is unavailable, check the client help before using --no-daemon inside the intended console. Never persist console identity values. If tool configuration requires reconnecting or restarting this client, tell me exactly what to do and what to ask you afterward; do not claim the connection works yet.\n4. Once the tools are available, inspect their schemas and use panel_get and panel_set to publish a short test status in this console's panel. Report success only after the tool calls succeed; otherwise explain the remaining step. Successful calls prove a usable connection, not correct-console routing: ask me to observe the test status beside the intended console. Verify independent publication with distinct statuses from two separate Codex consoles, or explicitly record the remaining verification blocker.\n5. Finally, ask me to open this client's MCP server list myself (/mcp in Codex or Claude) and confirm that ivy is listed as connected with its tools. Do not call the setup finished until I confirm it there.\n\n{PROMPT}"
    )
}

fn body(guide: &mut Guide, ui: &mut egui::Ui) {
    let published = guide.published;
    ui.label(
        "Set up Ivy once per AI client. Ivy updates normally keep your saved MCP registration.",
    );
    ui.label("An empty panel only means no status has been published.");
    ui.label(if published.documents == 0 {
        "No panels published yet"
    } else {
        "A panel has been published"
    });
    ui.strong("Reconnect an existing Claude session");
    ui.label("If Ivy is already listed in /mcp, reconnect it. Use its existing server name if it differs from ivy.");
    copyable(
        ui,
        "/mcp reconnect ivy",
        "Copy reconnect command",
        &mut guide.copied,
    );
    ui.label("If Ivy is absent from this running session, restart Claude in this same Ivy console and resume your conversation. You do not need to close the terminal.");
    ui.separator();
    if ui.button("Manual setup and diagnostics").clicked() {
        guide.manual_open = !guide.manual_open;
    }
    if guide.manual_open {
        ui.strong("First-time setup");
        let bridge = ui
            .ctx()
            .data(|data| data.get_temp::<Bridge>(egui::Id::new(BRIDGE_KEY)))
            .unwrap_or_default();
        if bridge.translocated {
            ui.colored_label(ui.visuals().warn_fg_color, translocation::NOTICE);
        } else {
            ui.label("For a client that has not been registered, copy these instructions into that AI client inside Ivy.");
            copy_button(
                ui,
                "Copy first-time setup prompt",
                &mut guide.copied,
                || setup_prompt(&bridge),
            );
        }
        manual(published, &mut guide.copied, ui);
    }
    if SHOW_SESSION_RECOVERY {
        let translocated = ui
            .ctx()
            .data(|data| data.get_temp::<Bridge>(egui::Id::new(BRIDGE_KEY)))
            .is_some_and(|bridge| bridge.translocated);
        recovery::show(guide.recovery, translocated, &mut guide.copied, ui);
    }
    egui::CollapsingHeader::new("Agent activity and input signals")
        .default_open(false)
        .show(ui, |ui| {
    ui.label("Explicit activation is required. Permission and question lifecycles were verified on Linux for the versions below; other versions and platforms remain unverified.");
    ui.label(format!(
        "Observed declarations: Codex {}, Claude {}, OpenCode {} (including stale).",
        published.observed[0], published.observed[1], published.observed[2]
    ));
    ui.checkbox(&mut guide.desired.codex, "Codex 0.167.0");
    ui.checkbox(&mut guide.desired.claude, "Claude Code 2.1.319");
    ui.checkbox(&mut guide.desired.opencode, "OpenCode 1.18.31");
    if ui.button("Save agent preferences").clicked() {
        guide.save_preferences = true;
    }
    ui.label("Codex and Claude use native terminal declarations; Claude requires no hooks.");
    ui.label("Enable before starting the client. If OpenCode was refused while disabled, restart it after enabling.");
    ui.label("To avoid a duplicate Codex title spinner, merge this key into your existing [tui] table in ~/.codex/config.toml. Preserve other settings, then restart Codex and resume your conversation:");
    copyable(ui, "terminal_title = [\"thread-name\", \"project-name\"]", "Copy Codex title setting", &mut guide.copied);
    ui.label("Claude removes its duplicate title indicator after native negotiation. Live client verification is still required.");
    if cfg!(windows) { ui.label("The OpenCode TUI plugin transport is not supported on Windows in this delivery."); }
    if let Some(dir) =
        crate::settings::path().and_then(|p| p.parent().map(std::path::Path::to_path_buf))
    {
        ui.label(format!(
            "OpenCode plugin destination: {}",
            crate::agent_setup::plugin_path(&dir).display()
        ));
        if ui.button("Install / repair OpenCode plugin").clicked() {
            guide.install_plugin = true;
        }
        ui.label("Add this entry to the existing plugin array in OpenCode tui.json, preserving its other entries:");
        copyable(
            ui,
            &crate::agent_setup::snippet(&dir),
            "Copy OpenCode plugin entry",
            &mut guide.copied,
        );
    }
    if !guide.message.is_empty() {
        ui.label(&guide.message);
    }
    ui.separator();
        });
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests;
```

### crates/app/src/closing.rs

```
//! Closing a tab or a console: what leaves, what it takes with it, and what is collected.
//!
//! **It sits beside the window rather than inside its core for the reason D28 gives**: the core had
//! grown past its ratchet, and this is a coherent piece of it — everything that happens when a tab
//! or the focused console goes away. The methods still reach the window's private fields, which is
//! exactly what a child module of the crate root is for.

use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};
use std::time::Instant;

use eframe::egui;

use crate::measure;
use crate::tree::{Closing, Node};
use crate::{Pane, Window};

impl Drop for Window {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        // Whole-window destruction does not run a closing frame that can rearm the watcher. Stop
        // it explicitly before Rust drops any pane-owned directory.
        self.watcher.stop_before_retirement();
    }
}

impl Window {
    /// Arms the watcher again over every pane that is left.
    ///
    /// The old generation is made inert first, including on remote retirement. Every surviving
    /// flag is then raised, so a publication during replacement cannot leave its panel stale.
    pub(super) fn rearm_the_watcher(&mut self, ctx: &egui::Context) {
        self.watcher.stop_before_retirement();
        let watcher = Self::watch(ctx, self.all_panes());
        self.watcher = watcher;
        for pane in self.all_panes() {
            pane.to_reread.raise();
        }
        #[cfg(test)]
        crate::tests::completed_operation(crate::tests::OperationWork::Rearm);
    }

    /// Banks a closing pane's channel counters on the window, **before it is dropped**.
    ///
    /// The report reads the live panes directly and adds [`Window::control`] on top, so a pane that
    /// closes has to hand its numbers over here or they leave with it.
    fn bank_control(&mut self, did: measure::Control) {
        self.control.retain(did);
    }

    /// Closes the tab that is being seen. If it is the only one, what is closed is the window.
    ///
    /// **Rearming the watcher is indispensable**: the old generation is made inert before the tab
    /// leaves, so without its replacement the surviving panes would no longer be watched and their
    /// next publications could remain unseen. There is a test that falls if it is removed.
    ///
    /// **The old watcher is stopped before the tab is dropped.** Rearming afterwards raises every
    /// survivor's reread flag, so no publication in that interval can leave a panel stale and the
    /// intentional deletions cannot be reported as lost tracking.
    pub(super) fn close_the_active_tab(&mut self, ctx: &egui::Context) {
        // **The last one is not removed from the list, and it is not a capricious exception:**
        // asking for the close does not close on the spot —frames are still running, and the report
        // is published on exit—, so an empty vector would leave `active` indexing out of range on
        // every one of them.
        if self.tabs.len() == 1 {
            ctx.send_viewport_cmd(egui::ViewportCommand::Close);
            return;
        }

        let leaving = self.active;
        self.some_closed_by_hand = true;

        // **What its consoles had already declared of this frame is collected before dropping
        // them.** They leave with their `Timings` inside, and in there there can be a round
        // recorded for this very frame: bytes that really were interpreted an instant ago. If it
        // leaves without being declared, the subtraction attributes it to the interface framework
        // and «the rest» comes out inflated precisely on the frame in which a tab is closed. The
        // cost of the closing itself —killing the programs and deleting their directories, which
        // happens when the tab is dropped— goes into the same bag for the same reason.
        //
        // **And the three places where this frame's work lives are collected, not just the recorded
        // one.** Looking only at what the measurement already had noted down declared **zero**
        // precisely at the instant of the chord: the real order of the frame is closing the
        // previous one —which empties what the measurement had pending—, pumping —which fills what
        // was interpreted without recording it— and attending to the keyboard **before** the
        // painting. So what the console had just interpreted, and what rereading its panel cost,
        // left with it undeclared. See [`what_this_frame_left`], which is where closing
        // a pane already solved it.
        let before = Instant::now();
        let its_cost: std::time::Duration = self.tabs[leaving]
            .panes_mut()
            .into_iter()
            .map(what_this_frame_left)
            .sum();

        // **Its channel counters are banked before it leaves**, or a tab closed mid-run takes what
        // its consoles published with it and the report under-counts — which can invalidate a run
        // that was correct. Read here, while the panes still exist.
        let its_control: Vec<_> = self.tabs[leaving]
            .panes_mut()
            .into_iter()
            .filter_map(|pane| pane.control.as_mut())
            .map(crate::control::PaneControl::stop_and_did_in_the_window)
            .collect();
        for did in its_control {
            self.bank_control(did);
        }

        // The tab leaves the layout first, then its old watcher is stopped before any directory is
        // removed. The replacement below watches only the survivors.
        let leaving_tab = self.tabs.remove(leaving);
        self.watcher.stop_before_retirement();

        // Here its programs die and its directories disappear, however many it had, through the
        // usual `Drop`s. Its watcher no longer contains those directories.
        drop(leaving_tab);

        // **Rearming the watcher goes inside the stopwatch**, and before it was left outside by a
        // line drawn in the wrong place: it rebuilds the watching over the panes of every tab that
        // is left and makes them reread their `panel.json`, which is the expensive half of closing.
        // No pane declares it, so without this it was published as interface framework cost.
        self.rearm_the_watcher(ctx);
        // On removing the last one in the list the index is left outside; in any other case, it is
        // left pointing at the one that took its place, which is the one on the right.
        self.active = leaving.min(self.tabs.len() - 1);
        self.timed_separately += its_cost + before.elapsed();
    }

    /// Closes the console that has the keyboard, and its siblings share out its place.
    ///
    /// **Nothing has to be shared out by hand, and that is the reason the layout carries weights
    /// and not fractions that add up to one**: removing one child from the list leaves the rest
    /// with the weights they already had, and the tiling places them in proportion over the whole
    /// area. With normalized fractions they would have to be renormalized on every close.
    ///
    /// **With a single console, closing it is closing the tab** —there is no layout left to
    /// reabsorb its place—, and if it was also the only tab, the window. It is delegated to the
    /// path that already exists instead of repeating its rule, which is not obvious: the last tab
    /// is not removed from the list, because asking for the close does not close on the spot and an
    /// empty vector would leave `active` indexing out of range.
    ///
    /// **What it costs is declared in full**, and they are three things no living pane is going to
    /// declare: the tree walk, what the one that is leaving had measured of this frame, and killing
    /// its program and deleting its directory. Without declaring them, the subtraction attributes
    /// them to the interface framework — the figure D3 decides with.
    pub(super) fn close_the_focused(&mut self, ctx: &egui::Context) {
        let before = Instant::now();
        // **The tab having a single console is its root being a leaf**, and asking the type costs
        // what looking at a tag costs. Counting the leaves would walk the whole tree and allocate a
        // vector only to throw it away, and it would also say for a second time —and more weakly—
        // an invariant the structure already guarantees: a branch is born with two children and
        // collapses when it is left with one.
        if matches!(self.active().root, Node::Leaf(_)) {
            self.timed_separately += before.elapsed();
            self.close_the_active_tab(ctx);
            return;
        }

        let focus = self.active().focus;
        let closed = self.active_mut().root.close(focus);
        let Some(Closing {
            mut leaving,
            focus: inherited,
        }) = closed
        else {
            // **It cannot happen**: the focus is the name of one of this tab's leaves, and with
            // more than one console none of them is the root. It is said out loud instead of kept
            // quiet because the chord has swallowed the key and closed nothing, and without this
            // line no trace of why would be left.
            eprintln!("the focused pane was not found when closing; nothing has been closed");
            // **The stopwatch is closed after writing**, not before: writing to the error output
            // costs whatever whoever reads at the other end of the pipe takes, and what is not
            // declared is published as interface framework cost.
            self.timed_separately += before.elapsed();
            return;
        };

        // **What it had done in this frame is collected before dropping it.** See [`Closing`] and
        // [`what_this_frame_left`].
        let its_cost = what_this_frame_left(&mut leaving);
        // **And its channel counters, for the same reason and here rather than at report time.** A
        // console closed mid-run would otherwise take its `applied`/`refused`/`released` out of the
        // live set the report walks.
        if let Some(did) = leaving
            .control
            .as_mut()
            .map(crate::control::PaneControl::stop_and_did_in_the_window)
        {
            self.bank_control(did);
        }
        self.active_mut().focus = inherited;
        // **Closing a console by hand counts the same as closing a tab by hand**, and without this
        // line the same gesture protected the window or not depending on how many siblings the
        // console had: with only one, the path above delegates to closing the tab, which does raise
        // it. The real case is a console whose program has already finished —and whose last content
        // is being read— beside another with a live agent: on closing the agent's nothing alive is
        // left, and the usual rule would close the window taking what was being read with it.
        // Nobody asked for that: closing one console was asked for.
        self.some_closed_by_hand = true;
        self.watcher.stop_before_retirement();
        // Here its program dies and its directory disappears, through the usual `Drop`s. Its old
        // watcher no longer contains that directory.
        drop(leaving);
        self.rearm_the_watcher(ctx);
        self.timed_separately += its_cost + before.elapsed();
    }

    /// Closes a console by its name, for a connection that opened it, **and refuses the two cases
    /// the chord cannot produce**.
    ///
    /// **It refuses rather than closing the tab**, unlike the chord's path: a person who presses
    /// `+X` on the last console of a tab means to close the tab, and an agent that guesses the same
    /// would take a whole tab of consoles away on a handle typo (**AC16**). And **the focus moves
    /// only if the console that leaves was holding it**, so closing one the person is not typing in
    /// does not steal their keyboard.
    pub(crate) fn close_a_pane_called(
        &mut self,
        name: usize,
        ctx: &egui::Context,
    ) -> Result<(), String> {
        let tab_index = self
            .tabs
            .iter()
            .position(|tab| tab.panes().iter().any(|pane| pane.name == name))
            .ok_or_else(|| "that console is gone".to_owned())?;
        if matches!(self.tabs[tab_index].root, Node::Leaf(_)) {
            return Err(
                "that console is its tab's only one; closing it would close the tab".to_owned(),
            );
        }

        let closed = self.tabs[tab_index].root.close(name);
        let Some(Closing {
            mut leaving,
            focus: inherited,
        }) = closed
        else {
            return Err("the console was not found when closing; nothing was closed".to_owned());
        };

        // The same two things the chord's close collects, and for the same reasons: what the console
        // had measured of this frame, and the channel counters that would leave with it.
        //
        // **The elapsed time is not added here.** It is the drain that times this call and adds it to
        // `timed_separately`, because it is the drain that runs inside the frame; adding it in both
        // places would subtract the same microseconds twice from the number the framework is judged
        // by (**D13**) — the opposite of the mistake declared in this file, and just as wrong.
        let its_cost = what_this_frame_left(&mut leaving);
        if let Some(did) = leaving
            .control
            .as_mut()
            .map(crate::control::PaneControl::stop_and_did_in_the_window)
        {
            self.bank_control(did);
        }
        after_removal(self, tab_index, name, inherited);
        self.some_closed_by_hand = true;
        self.watcher.stop_before_retirement();
        drop(leaving);
        self.rearm_the_watcher(ctx);
        self.timed_separately += its_cost;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Detaches several named consoles after validating the whole removal.
    ///
    /// Nothing is changed until every name is known, unique, and leaves at least one console in its
    /// tab. Once those checks hold, `Node::close` cannot refuse: no earlier removal can erase a
    /// different named leaf, and the per-tab remainder keeps a root to close from.
    pub(crate) fn detach_panes_called(
        &mut self,
        names: &[usize],
        ctx: &egui::Context,
    ) -> Result<Vec<Pane>, String> {
        self.validate_detaching_panes(names)?;

        let mut detached = Vec::with_capacity(names.len());
        let mut pending_cost = std::time::Duration::ZERO;
        for &name in names {
            let tab = self
                .tabs
                .iter()
                .position(|tab| tab.panes().iter().any(|pane| pane.name == name))
                .expect("the complete retirement was validated before changing the tree");
            let Closing {
                mut leaving,
                focus: inherited,
            } = self.tabs[tab]
                .root
                .close(name)
                .expect("a validated retirement target remains in its tree");
            pending_cost += what_this_frame_left(&mut leaving);
            if let Some(did) = leaving
                .control
                .as_mut()
                .map(crate::control::PaneControl::stop_and_did_in_the_window)
            {
                self.bank_control(did);
            }
            after_removal(self, tab, name, inherited);
            detached.push(leaving);
        }

        if !detached.is_empty() {
            self.rearm_the_watcher(ctx);
        }
        self.timed_separately += pending_cost;
        Ok(detached)
    }

    pub(crate) fn validate_detaching_panes(&self, names: &[usize]) -> Result<(), String> {
        let mut unique = HashSet::with_capacity(names.len());
        let mut per_tab: HashMap<usize, usize> = HashMap::new();
        for &name in names {
            if !unique.insert(name) {
                return Err("the retirement names one console twice".to_owned());
            }
            let tab = self
                .tabs
                .iter()
                .position(|tab| tab.panes().iter().any(|pane| pane.name == name))
                .ok_or_else(|| "a console owned by this connection is gone".to_owned())?;
            *per_tab.entry(tab).or_default() += 1;
        }
        for (tab, removing) in per_tab {
            if self.tabs[tab].panes().len() <= removing {
                return Err("retirement would leave a tab without a console".to_owned());
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

fn after_removal(window: &mut Window, tab_index: usize, removed: usize, inherited: usize) {
    let tab = &mut window.tabs[tab_index];
    if tab.focus == removed {
        tab.focus = inherited;
    }
    if matches!(tab.root, Node::Leaf(_)) || window.pane_view.zoomed(tab.name) == Some(removed) {
        window.pane_view.clear(tab.name);
    }
}

/// Takes **everything** this console has done in this frame and nobody has declared yet, for
/// the one that is leaving and is not going to publish any mean.
///
/// **There are two places, and one alone is not enough.** The frame's real order decides it:
/// `logic` first closes the previous frame —which empties what [`measure::Timings`] had
/// pending— and **then** pumps, which is what leaves in `pending_measurement` what this console
/// has just interpreted. The keyboard is attended at the start of the UI pass, **before the
/// painting**, so when the closing chord arrives, what the measurement had pending is empty and
/// what this frame interpreted is whole in `pending_measurement`: looking only at the first one
/// declares **zero** right in the frame where a dumping console is closed, and those
/// milliseconds are taken by «the rest of the frame», that is, the cost attributed to the UI
/// framework. The other one can indeed hold something when egui repeats the pass, so both are
/// picked up.
///
/// **It is a free function and not a method of `Pane`** because it is closing's work: it went out
/// of the crate root to pay `lib.rs`'s ratchet (D28), and `impl Pane` with it.
///
/// **It records no mean**, for the same reason as [`measure::Timings::take_pending`]: this
/// console is no longer going to publish spans, and closing it along the normal path would
/// force inventing a frame cost that is not going to finish.
fn what_this_frame_left(pane: &mut Pane) -> std::time::Duration {
    let without_recording = pane
        .pending_measurement
        .take()
        .map_or(std::time::Duration::ZERO, |(_, _, interpreted)| interpreted);

    pane.timings.take_pending()
        + without_recording
        + std::mem::take(&mut pane.panel_reread_unpainted)
        + std::mem::take(&mut pane.control_unpainted)
}
