```
//! Agent metadata reaches pane/tab attention without acquiring producer authority.
use super::*;
use crate::agent_signals::{Client, Enabled};
use panel_contract::Attention;

mod native_transport;

fn enabled() -> Enabled {
    Enabled {
        claude: true,
        codex: true,
        opencode: true,
    }
}

fn native(pane: &mut Pane, payload: &str, now: Instant) {
    pane.screen
        .advance(format!("\x1b]7501;{payload}\x1b\\").as_bytes());
    pane.signals.sync_native(&mut pane.screen, now);
}

fn configure(pane: &mut Pane) {
    pane.signals.configure(enabled(), &mut pane.screen);
}

fn publish_flow(pane: &mut Pane, attention: &str) -> String {
    let document = format!(
        r#"{{"attention":"{attention}","phase":"build","lines":["Flow task"],"actions":[{{"label":"Inspect","send":"git status"}}]}}"#
    );
    assert_eq!(
        panel_contract::parse_document(&document)
            .unwrap()
            .actions
            .len(),
        1,
        "the authority witness needs a real producer action"
    );
    pane.panel_state
        .deliver(
            71,
            "flow",
            panel_contract::parse_document(&document).unwrap(),
            SystemTime::now(),
        )
        .unwrap();
    pane.panel_state.document().unwrap()
}

fn still_flow_owned(pane: &mut Pane, document: &str) {
    assert_eq!(
        pane.panel_state.document().unwrap(),
        document,
        "agent metadata rewrote the producer document or actions"
    );
    let competing = panel_contract::parse_document(r#"{"lines":["intruder"]}"#).unwrap();
    assert!(
        pane.panel_state
            .deliver(72, "other", competing, SystemTime::now())
            .is_err(),
        "agent metadata released Flow's ownership"
    );
}

fn snapshot(permission: bool, question: bool) -> panel_control::AgentSnapshot {
    panel_control::AgentSnapshot {
        stale: false,
        session: "ses_root".into(),
        activity: panel_control::Activity::Busy,
        permission,
        question,
    }
}

fn delivered(pane: &mut Pane, connection: u64, what: panel_control::What) -> panel_control::Answer {
    let (answer, reply) = std::sync::mpsc::sync_channel(1);
    pane.control
        .as_ref()
        .unwrap()
        .enqueue_for_tests(panel_control::Delivery {
            origin: panel_control::PublicationOrigin::Unknown,
            connection,
            what,
            answer,
            #[cfg(windows)]
            release: None,
        });
    pane.refresh_its_panel(&egui::Context::default());
    reply
        .recv_timeout(Duration::from_secs(2))
        .expect("the pane must answer the signal request")
}

#[test]
fn native_waits_reach_the_background_tab_without_changing_flow() {
    let mut window = window_of_tabs([["sleep 8", "sleep 8"]; 2]);
    let now = Instant::now();
    let document = {
        let pane = window.tabs[1].panes_mut().into_iter().next().unwrap();
        configure(pane);
        publish_flow(pane, "block")
    };
    for payload in [
        "state=working:app=codex",
        "state=blocked:app=codex:kind=permission",
        "state=blocked:app=codex:kind=question",
        "state=idle:app=codex",
        "state=clear",
    ] {
        let expected = if payload.contains("blocked") {
            Attention::Wait
        } else {
            Attention::Block
        };
        let pane = window.tabs[1].panes_mut().into_iter().next().unwrap();
        native(pane, payload, now);
        assert_eq!(
            pane.effective_attention(SystemTime::now()).unwrap().class,
            expected
        );
        still_flow_owned(pane, &document);
        assert_eq!(
            window.tabs[1].attention(SystemTime::now()).unwrap().class,
            expected,
            "background tab lost its console's input requirement"
        );
        assert_eq!(window.active, 0);
        assert!(
            window.tabs[0].attention(SystemTime::now()).is_none(),
            "signal escaped its pane"
        );
    }
}

#[test]
fn native_resolution_and_disable_preserve_an_existing_panel_wait() {
    let mut pane = pane_with("sleep 8", 0);
    configure(&mut pane);
    let document = publish_flow(&mut pane, "wait");
    let now = Instant::now();
    for payload in [
        "state=blocked:app=claude-code:kind=auth",
        "state=done:app=claude-code",
        "state=error:app=claude-code",
        "state=clear",
    ] {
        native(&mut pane, payload, now);
        assert_eq!(
            pane.effective_attention(SystemTime::now()).unwrap().class,
            Attention::Wait
        );
        still_flow_owned(&mut pane, &document);
    }
    native(&mut pane, "state=blocked:app=codex:kind=question", now);
    pane.signals.configure(Enabled::default(), &mut pane.screen);
    assert!(pane.signals.view(now).is_none());
    assert_eq!(
        pane.effective_attention(SystemTime::now()).unwrap().class,
        Attention::Wait
    );
    still_flow_owned(&mut pane, &document);
}

#[test]
fn disabled_malformed_unknown_and_child_signals_cannot_replace_a_root_wait() {
    let mut pane = pane_with("sleep 8", 0);
    let now = Instant::now();
    native(&mut pane, "state=blocked:app=codex:kind=question", now);
    assert!(pane.effective_attention(SystemTime::now()).is_none());
    configure(&mut pane);
    native(
        &mut pane,
        "state=blocked:app=claude-code:kind=permission",
        now,
    );
    for payload in [
        "state=idle:app=claude-code:id=child",
        "state=clear:id=child",
        "state=working:app=unknown",
        "state=blocked:app=codex:kind=made-up",
        "state=working:app=codex:state=idle",
    ] {
        native(&mut pane, payload, now);
        let view = pane.signals.view(now).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            view.client,
            Client::Claude,
            "invalid or child frame stole root identity: {payload}"
        );
        assert!(
            view.waiting,
            "invalid or child frame resolved the root wait: {payload}"
        );
    }
}

#[test]
fn opencode_socket_takeover_is_ordered_and_does_not_acquire_flow_authority() {
    let mut window = window_with("sleep 8", "sleep 8");
    attach_a_channel(&mut window, 0);
    let pane = window.active_mut().panes_mut().into_iter().next().unwrap();
    configure(pane);
    let document = publish_flow(pane, "block");
    let now = Instant::now();
    native(pane, "state=blocked:app=codex:kind=question", now);
    assert!(matches!(
        delivered(pane, 81, panel_control::What::AgentSignal(None)),
        panel_control::Answer::Taken
    ));
    assert_eq!(
        pane.signals.view(now).unwrap().client,
        Client::Codex,
        "a connection without a route snapshot took presentation authority"
    );
    assert!(matches!(
        delivered(
            pane,
            81,
            panel_control::What::AgentSignal(Some(snapshot(true, false)))
        ),
        panel_control::Answer::Taken
    ));
    assert_eq!(
        pane.signals.view(Instant::now()).unwrap().client,
        Client::OpenCode
    );
    assert!(
        matches!(
            delivered(
                pane,
                82,
                panel_control::What::AgentSignal(Some(snapshot(false, false)))
            ),
            panel_control::Answer::Refused(_)
        ),
        "a competing socket replaced the live owner"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        pane.effective_attention(SystemTime::now()).unwrap().class,
        Attention::Wait
    );
    still_flow_owned(pane, &document);
    assert!(matches!(
        delivered(
            pane,
            81,
            panel_control::What::AgentSignal(Some(snapshot(false, false)))
        ),
        panel_control::Answer::Taken
    ));
    assert_eq!(
        pane.effective_attention(SystemTime::now()).unwrap().class,
        Attention::Block
    );
    native(
        pane,
        "state=blocked:app=claude-code:kind=question",
        Instant::now(),
    );
    assert!(matches!(
        delivered(pane, 81, panel_control::What::AgentSignal(None)),
        panel_control::Answer::Taken
    ));
    assert!(
        pane.signals.view(Instant::now()).is_none(),
        "withdrawal resurrected a suppressed native declaration"
    );
    still_flow_owned(pane, &document);
    native(
        pane,
        "state=blocked:app=claude-code:kind=question",
        Instant::now(),
    );
    assert_eq!(
        pane.signals.view(Instant::now()).unwrap().client,
        Client::Claude
    );
}

#[test]
fn unexpected_opencode_close_retains_unconfirmed_wait_until_complete_replacement() {
    let mut window = window_with("sleep 8", "sleep 8");
    attach_a_channel(&mut window, 0);
    let pane = window.active_mut().panes_mut().into_iter().next().unwrap();
    configure(pane);
    let document = publish_flow(pane, "block");
    assert!(matches!(
        delivered(
            pane,
            81,
            panel_control::What::AgentSignal(Some(snapshot(false, true)))
        ),
        panel_control::Answer::Taken
    ));
    let (answer, _reply) = std::sync::mpsc::sync_channel(1);
    pane.control
        .as_ref()
        .unwrap()
        .enqueue_for_tests(panel_control::Delivery {
            origin: panel_control::PublicationOrigin::Unknown,
            connection: 81,
            what: panel_control::What::Closed,
            answer,
            #[cfg(windows)]
            release: None,
        });
    pane.refresh_its_panel(&egui::Context::default());
    let view = pane.signals.view(Instant::now()).unwrap();
    assert!(view.waiting);
    assert!(
        !view.backed,
        "socket loss was reported as a confirmed current wait"
    );
    assert!(!view.busy, "disconnected metadata still animates");
    let attention = pane.effective_attention(SystemTime::now()).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(attention.class, Attention::Wait);
    assert!(!attention.backed, "socket loss was treated as an answer");
    delivered(
        pane,
        82,
        panel_control::What::AgentSignal(Some(snapshot(false, false))),
    );
    assert_eq!(
        pane.effective_attention(SystemTime::now()).unwrap().class,
        Attention::Block
    );
    still_flow_owned(pane, &document);
}

#[test]
fn native_reset_and_silence_have_different_attention_effects() {
    let mut pane = pane_with("sleep 8", 0);
    configure(&mut pane);
    let now = Instant::now();
    native(&mut pane, "state=blocked:app=codex:kind=question", now);
    let stale = now + Duration::from_secs(30 * 60 + 1);
    let attention = pane.signals.attention(stale).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(attention.class, Attention::Wait);
    assert!(
        !attention.backed,
        "silence must retain an unconfirmed request"
    );
    pane.screen.advance(b"\x1bc");
    pane.signals.sync_native(&mut pane.screen, stale);
    assert!(
        pane.signals.view(stale).is_none(),
        "RIS failed to withdraw terminal metadata"
    );
}

#[test]
fn claude_negotiation_is_explicit_and_cannot_rewrite_titles_or_panel_documents() {
    let mut pane = pane_with("sleep 8", 0);
    let now = Instant::now();
    pane.screen.advance(b"\x1b]7501;?\x07");
    assert!(
        !pane.signals.sync_native(&mut pane.screen, now),
        "disabled Claude answered capability negotiation"
    );
    configure(&mut pane);
    let document = publish_flow(&mut pane, "wait");
    pane.screen
        .advance(b"\x1b]0;Keep my title\x07\x1b]7501;?\x07\x1b]7501;?\x1b\\");
    assert!(
        pane.signals.sync_native(&mut pane.screen, now),
        "enabled Claude probe was not routed to the PTY reply path"
    );
    assert!(
        !pane.signals.sync_native(&mut pane.screen, now),
        "one parsed batch replayed capability replies"
    );
    native(&mut pane, "state=working:app=claude-code", now);
    assert_eq!(pane.screen.terminal_title(), Some("Keep my title"));
    still_flow_owned(&mut pane, &document);
}

#[test]
fn live_settings_and_window_pump_apply_and_withdraw_hidden_pane_signals() {
    let mut window = window_of_tabs([["sleep 8", "sleep 8"]; 2]);
    let (preferences, warnings) = settings::interpret("agent.codex.enabled=true").unwrap();
    assert!(warnings.kept().is_empty());
    preferences.apply_to(&mut window);
    window.pump_them_all();
    window.tabs[1].panes_mut()[1]
        .screen
        .advance(b"\x1b]7501;state=blocked:app=codex:kind=question\x1b\\");
    window.pump_them_all();
    assert_eq!(
        window.tabs[1].attention(SystemTime::now()).unwrap().class,
        Attention::Wait,
        "the app pump failed to collect native metadata from a hidden pane"
    );
    assert!(window.tabs[0].attention(SystemTime::now()).is_none());
    let (preferences, warnings) = settings::interpret("agent.codex.enabled=false").unwrap();
    assert!(warnings.kept().is_empty());
    preferences.apply_to(&mut window);
    window.pump_them_all();
    assert!(
        window.tabs[1].attention(SystemTime::now()).is_none(),
        "live disable left background attention behind"
    );
}

#[test]
fn actual_headers_paint_only_declared_logos_preserve_manual_names_and_gate_animation() {
    use crate::agent_signals::{View, logos};
    let ctx = egui::Context::default();
    chrome::prepare(&ctx);
    logos::install(&ctx);
    let mut window = window_with("sleep 8", "sleep 8");
    window.chrome.enable();
    window.chrome.agent_preferences = enabled();
    window.headers = window.headers.integrated(true);
    window.pump_them_all();
    window.active_mut().panes_mut()[0].label = Some("My review console".into());
    window.active_mut().panes_mut()[0]
        .screen
        .advance(b"\x1b]0;Arbitrary \xe2\xa0\xb8 title\x07");
    let area = egui::Rect::from_min_size(egui::Pos2::ZERO, egui::vec2(1400.0, 1000.0));
    let textures: Vec<_> = [Client::Claude, Client::Codex, Client::OpenCode]
        .into_iter()
        .map(|client| {
            ctx.data(|data| {
                data.get_temp::<egui::TextureHandle>(egui::Id::new(("agent-logo", client as u8)))
                    .unwrap()
                    .id()
            })
        })
        .collect();
    let painted = |window: &mut Window| {
        let shapes = ui_pass(window, &ctx, area, egui::RawInput::default());
        assert!(
            the_text_queued_up(&shapes, "My review console"),
            "a declared agent replaced the person's console name"
        );
        shapes
            .into_iter()
            .filter_map(|shape| match shape.shape {
                egui::Shape::Mesh(mesh) if textures.contains(&mesh.texture_id) => {
                    Some(mesh.texture_id)
                }
                _ => None,
            })
            .collect::<Vec<_>>()
    };
    assert!(
        painted(&mut window).is_empty(),
        "a title alone was used as agent identity"
    );
    for (index, app) in ["claude-code", "codex"].into_iter().enumerate() {
        native(
            window.active_mut().panes_mut().into_iter().next().unwrap(),
            &format!("state=working:app={app}"),
            Instant::now(),
        );
        assert_eq!(
            painted(&mut window),
            vec![textures[index]],
            "the real pane header omitted or misidentified a native client"
        );
    }
    window.active_mut().panes_mut()[0]
        .signals
        .receive(81, Some(snapshot(false, false)), Instant::now())
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        painted(&mut window),
        vec![textures[2]],
        "the real pane header omitted the OpenCode reporter"
    );
    window.chrome.agent_preferences = Enabled::default();
    window.pump_them_all();
    assert!(
        painted(&mut window).is_empty(),
        "disabled client identity remained painted"
    );
    let busy = View {
        client: Client::Codex,
        busy: true,
        done: false,
        waiting: false,
        backed: true,
    };
    assert_eq!(
        logos::animation_delay(busy, true, true),
        Some(Duration::from_millis(100))
    );
    for (view, visible, focused) in [
        (busy, false, true),
        (busy, true, false),
        (
            View {
                busy: false,
                done: false,
                ..busy
            },
            true,
            true,
        ),
        (
            View {
                backed: false,
                ..busy
            },
            true,
            true,
        ),
        (
            View {
                waiting: true,
                busy: false,
                done: false,
                ..busy
            },
            true,
            true,
        ),
    ] {
        assert!(
            logos::animation_delay(view, visible, focused).is_none(),
            "hidden, unfocused, idle, waiting or unconfirmed source armed animation"
        );
    }
}

#[test]
fn incomplete_opencode_refresh_cannot_take_over_or_resolve_a_known_wait() {
    let mut window = window_with("sleep 8", "sleep 8");
    attach_a_channel(&mut window, 0);
    let pane = window.active_mut().panes_mut().into_iter().next().unwrap();
    configure(pane);
    let document = publish_flow(pane, "block");
    native(
        pane,
        "state=blocked:app=codex:kind=permission",
        Instant::now(),
    );
    let mut stale = snapshot(false, false);
    stale.stale = true;
    assert!(matches!(
        delivered(
            pane,
            81,
            panel_control::What::AgentSignal(Some(stale.clone()))
        ),
        panel_control::Answer::Refused(_)
    ));
    assert_eq!(
        pane.signals.view(Instant::now()).unwrap().client,
        Client::Codex
    );
    assert!(matches!(
        delivered(
            pane,
            81,
            panel_control::What::AgentSignal(Some(snapshot(false, true)))
        ),
        panel_control::Answer::Taken
    ));
    assert!(matches!(
        delivered(pane, 81, panel_control::What::AgentSignal(Some(stale))),
        panel_control::Answer::Taken
    ));
    let view = pane.signals.view(Instant::now()).unwrap();
    assert!(
        view.waiting,
        "incomplete refresh replaced a known question with false flags"
    );
    assert!(
        !view.backed && !view.busy,
        "incomplete refresh must stop animation and dim the retained wait"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        pane.effective_attention(SystemTime::now()).unwrap().class,
        Attention::Wait
    );
    assert!(!pane.effective_attention(SystemTime::now()).unwrap().backed);
    still_flow_owned(pane, &document);
    assert!(matches!(
        delivered(
            pane,
            81,
            panel_control::What::AgentSignal(Some(snapshot(false, false)))
        ),
        panel_control::Answer::Taken
    ));
    assert_eq!(
        pane.effective_attention(SystemTime::now()).unwrap().class,
        Attention::Block
    );
    still_flow_owned(pane, &document);
}
```

### crates/panel-contract/Cargo.toml

```
[package]
name = "panel-contract"
version.workspace = true
edition.workspace = true
license.workspace = true

[dependencies]
# Already compiled in the tree as a knock-on from egui (through `ahash`), and **with these very
# features**: asking it for one more would force it to recompile. The only one that would be of any
# use is the one that turns its error into an `io::Error`, and that is done by hand in two lines.
getrandom = "0.3"

# The contract has seven optional fields and lines that are a string **or** an object, and whatever
# is not understood has to be tolerated field by field: writing that by hand would mean rewriting a
# JSON parser with more bugs in it.
# **It is not free, and that is worth knowing**: although `serde` was already in the tree as a
# knock-on from the terminal engine, this adds four packages that were not — `serde_json`,
# `serde_core`, `itoa` and `zmij`. It is paid for because reading someone else's file tolerantly is
# exactly what this crate exists to do.
serde_json = "1"

[target.'cfg(windows)'.dependencies]
windows-sys = { version = "0.61.2", features = [
    "Win32_Foundation",
    "Win32_Security",
    "Win32_Security_Authorization",
    "Win32_Storage_FileSystem",
    "Win32_System_Memory",
    "Win32_System_Threading",
] }
```

### crates/panel-contract/src/lib.rs

```
#![deny(rustdoc::broken_intra_doc_links)]
// **A broken rustdoc link is invisible to the compiler, and half the repository is rustdoc.** When
// this was put in, the tree already had one pointing at a constant that an earlier rename had left
// behind: nobody noticed because `cargo doc` warns and does not fail. With the `deny` it fails, and
// the milestone that renames 46,458 lines is going to be grateful for it.
//
// It fires on `cargo doc`, **not** on `cargo test` — this repository has no continuous integration,
// so it still has to be invoked. What changes is that now it breaks instead of warning.
//! Types for `panel.json`, reading and validation, and **the directory where that file lives**.
//!
//! It depends on nothing visual: if the painting layer is rewritten, this crate keeps serving.
//!
//! The directory is here and not in whoever launches the consoles for the reason the line above
//! gives: **nothing about generating a name, creating a directory and deleting it has anything to do
//! with painting**, so putting it in the crate that is going to be rewritten the day the interface
//! framework changes would force it to be rewritten along with it for no reason at all. And *where*
//! the state is published is part of the contract, just as much as *what* is published.

use std::ffi::OsStr;
#[cfg(not(windows))]
use std::fs::DirBuilder;
use std::io;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

mod dropped;
mod panel;
mod patch;
pub mod plan;
#[cfg(windows)]
pub mod windows_directory;

pub use dropped::Dropped;
pub use panel::{
    Action, Attention, Line, MAX_ACTIONS, MAX_BYTES, MAX_INDENT, MAX_SEND, Mark, Panel, Problem,
    STALE_AFTER, Style, is_shown_as_itself, parse_document, parse_document_noting, read,
    read_if_newer, serialize,
};
pub use patch::{MAX_OPS, Op, Patch, Scalar, parse_ops, parse_ops_noting};

/// The only file the contract puts inside the directory. It is written by whoever does the work,
/// not by this crate.
///
/// **Public because it is needed outside.** The watcher that arrives with F1.6 runs in the
/// application and has to discard the notifications from any other path, so it needs to name this
/// file; without exporting it, the string would be written out by hand in every place and the
/// contract would stop living in a single one.
pub const PANEL_FILE: &str = "panel.json";

/// The other thing the contract puts inside the directory: the endpoint a producer can speak to
/// instead of writing the file.
///
/// **It is here, beside [`PANEL_FILE`], for the reason the crate's header gives**: *where* the state
/// is published is part of the contract, so the two transports' names live in the same place. It is
/// also what [`remove`] has to know about — see that function for what a second name in this
/// directory costs if it is forgotten.
///
/// **This is the POSIX half of the endpoint's name, and only that.** A pipe lives in the machine's
/// namespace rather than in a directory, so on Windows there is nothing here to join a path to and
/// whoever binds builds the name from the pane's identifier instead; nothing on that platform reads
/// this constant. Which of the two does the separating is the transport's business, and the
/// `panel-control` crate is where it is decided.
pub const CONTROL_SOCKET: &str = "control.sock";

/// The names of the contract's two variables, in a single place.
///
/// They are here, and not in every place that writes them, because they are needed **also when there
/// is no directory**: then they have to be removed from the program's environment, and to remove
/// them you have to know what they are called.
pub const ENV_NAMES: [&str; 2] = ["PANEL_ID", "PANEL_DIR"];

/// The directory of a split: its identifier and the path where its producer publishes the state.
///
/// It is born with the directory already created and **deletes it when it dies**. That is the reason
/// it is a type and not two loose functions: the `Drop` covers paths that an explicit cleanup would
/// miss —the half-built error of whoever uses it, and the panic, where the interface framework does
/// not run its shutdown hook but destructors do run.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct PanelDir {
    id: String,
    path: PathBuf,
}

impl PanelDir {
    /// Generates the identifier, creates its directory and leaves it ready for the producer to write.
    ///
    /// **Creation is the uniqueness check**: the directory is created without recursion, so if the
    /// name already existed this fails instead of sharing the directory with another split —and
    /// sharing it would silently break the per-identifier scoping the rest depends on. That is also
    /// why the path adds **a single component**: there is no intermediate segment that would have to
    /// be created first, nor that a third party could leave prepared in a shared temporary.
    pub fn create() -> io::Result<Self> {
        let id = generate_id()?;
        let path = base_dir().join(format!("ivy-{id}"));
        create_dir(&path)?;

        Ok(Self { id, path })
    }

    pub fn id(&self) -> &str {
        &self.id
    }

    pub fn path(&self) -> &Path {
        &self.path
    }

    /// The two variables the split's process inherits.
    ///
    /// **The array is of two and not a list, because that number is what gets reviewed.** Whoever
    /// launches the program accepts an open list of pairs —it has to accept it, because it does not
    /// know what a panel is—, so the ceiling is not there: it is in that the only thing passed to it
    /// comes from here. Adding a third variable forces a change to this type, in the contract crate;
    /// with a list built at the call site, injecting one more —a credential, for example— would be
    /// data and not a change that someone would have to approve.
    pub fn env(&self) -> [(&'static str, &OsStr); 2] {
        [
            (ENV_NAMES[0], OsStr::new(&self.id)),
            (ENV_NAMES[1], self.path.as_os_str()),
        ]
    }
}

impl Drop for PanelDir {
    /// Deletes what the contract puts in the directory, and then the directory.
    ///
    /// **Without recursion, on purpose.** The directory belongs to this crate, but its content
    /// belongs to the producer: a recursive delete would wipe out whatever it had left there, and
    /// would turn any badly built path into data loss instead of an error. If something that is not
    /// the contract's remains, the directory survives and it is said where.
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        // A notice is emitted because the fallback to a system temporary is **not** emptied when the
        // session closes, unlike the user's runtime directory: without this line, a leftover there
        // would be invisible and would pile up as soon as there are producers writing for real.
        if let Some(failure) = remove(&self.path) {
            eprintln!(
                "the panel directory {} remains ({failure})",
                self.path.display()
            );
        }
    }
}

/// Deletes the contract file and the directory, and returns **what deserves a notice**.
///
/// That the directory is no longer there does not deserve one: the user has a shell inside their
/// console and may have deleted it themselves. That something not belonging to the contract remains,
/// yes.
///
/// It is separate from the destructor so it can be tested: a destructor returns nothing, and the
/// decision of what is kept quiet and what is said is exactly what has to be assertable.
///
/// **Everything the contract puts here is deleted here, and that is why the list is two names
/// rather than one.** The deletion stays non-recursive for the reason above, so anything of this
/// crate's own left behind makes `remove_dir` fail and prints the notice on **every** console close.
fn remove(path: &Path) -> Option<io::Error> {
    // That it is not there is the normal case: until someone publishes, the directory is empty.
    let _ = std::fs::remove_file(path.join(PANEL_FILE));
    // The endpoint is only there on the platforms where it is a file at all, and only once
    // something bound it: the same «normal case» as the line above, and for the same reason its
    // failure is dropped.
    let _ = std::fs::remove_file(path.join(CONTROL_SOCKET));

    match std::fs::remove_dir(path) {
        Ok(()) => None,
        Err(failure) if failure.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => None,
        Err(failure) => Some(failure),
    }
}

/// Creates the directory, and **fails if it already exists**.
///
/// Without recursion for the two reasons: there is no intermediate segment to create, and the
/// creation mode that does accept intermediate segments also does not complain when the path is
/// already there — it would adopt a directory this crate did not make.
fn create_dir(path: &Path) -> io::Result<()> {
    #[cfg(windows)]
    {
        windows_directory::create(path)
    }
    #[cfg(not(windows))]
    {
        let mut builder = DirBuilder::new();
        // Without this the mode comes from the session's umask, and a file with user state is
        // going to live here. The fallback may be a temporary shared by everyone.
        #[cfg(unix)]
        {
            use std::os::unix::fs::DirBuilderExt;
            builder.mode(0o700);
        }
        builder.create(path)
    }
}

/// Eight hexadecimal digits.
///
/// The four in the example of the decision that sets this are 65,536 names, and with a creation that
/// fails if the name already exists a collision leaves a split without an identity. Thirty-two bits
/// cost the same.
fn generate_id() -> io::Result<String> {
    // The error is converted by hand and not with `?` so as not to have to enable for `getrandom`
    // the feature that brings that conversion: it is already compiled in the tree without it, and
    // asking for it would recompile it.
    let random = getrandom::u32().map_err(|failure| io::Error::other(failure.to_string()))?;
    Ok(format_id(random))
}

/// The formatting, apart from the draw, so it can be tested with given values.
///
/// The zero-padding is what makes them **always** eight digits: without it, a small number would
/// give a shorter name, and a test that looks at the length of a randomly drawn identifier would
/// almost never notice.
fn format_id(random: u32) -> String {
    format!("{random:08x}")
}

/// Where the splits' directories live.
///
/// The user's runtime directory is the first choice for what it is: theirs and theirs alone, in
/// memory, and the system empties it when the session closes. When it is unset or not absolute
/// —macOS, a Linux session without a manager, a mistyped value— it falls back to the system
/// temporary, held to the same rule on Unix ([`base_dir_from`]).
pub fn base_dir() -> PathBuf {
    let base = base_dir_from(
        std::env::var_os("XDG_RUNTIME_DIR").as_deref(),
        std::env::temp_dir(),
    );
    // Said once per process, because every console created asks again. The XDG specification
    // only asks for a warning when the variable is unset; a value that was set and had to be
    // ignored is what somebody would otherwise never find out about.
    if let Some(warning) = warning(&base) {
        static WARNED: std::sync::Once = std::sync::Once::new();
        WARNED.call_once(|| eprintln!("{warning}"));
    }
    base.path
}

/// Where consoles' directories go, and which variables were set but could not be used.
#[derive(Debug, PartialEq)]
struct Base {
    path: PathBuf,
    ignored: Vec<&'static str>,
}

/// What a person is told about a [`Base`] that had to ignore something, or nothing.
fn warning(base: &Base) -> Option<String> {
    (!base.ignored.is_empty()).then(|| {
        format!(
            "ivy: {} set but not an absolute path; consoles use {} instead",
            base.ignored.join(" and "),
            base.path.display()
        )
    })
}

/// The decision, separated from reading the environment so it can be tested without touching it.
///
/// **Only an absolute path is a runtime directory**, which is what the XDG specification asks of
/// the variable: an empty or relative value would create the directory inside the application's
/// working directory — without failing, which is the worst thing it could do — and hand every
/// console a `PANEL_DIR` its own MCP bridge refuses as not a whole identity (D88). **The fallback
/// is held to the same rule**: the system temporary is TMPDIR as written on Unix, relative or
/// empty included, and then `/tmp` is the one directory every Unix has. Elsewhere the platform's
/// temporary is taken as it comes: Windows resolves it to a full path itself. An empty value is
/// unset, not ignored: only a non-empty one somebody wrote is reported.
fn base_dir_from(value: Option<&OsStr>, temporary: PathBuf) -> Base {
    let mut ignored = Vec::new();
    let runtime = value.filter(|value| !value.is_empty()).map(Path::new);
    if let Some(runtime) = runtime {
        if runtime.is_absolute() {
            return Base {
                path: runtime.to_path_buf(),
                ignored,
            };
        }
        ignored.push("XDG_RUNTIME_DIR");
    }
    if temporary.is_absolute() || cfg!(not(unix)) {
        return Base {
            path: temporary,
            ignored,
        };
    }
    if !temporary.as_os_str().is_empty() {
        ignored.push("TMPDIR");
    }
    Base {
        path: PathBuf::from("/tmp"),
        ignored,
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn the_directory_is_born_created_and_can_be_written_inside() {
        let panel = PanelDir::create().expect("create the panel directory");

        assert!(panel.path().is_dir(), "not a directory: {panel:?}");
        std::fs::write(panel.path().join(PANEL_FILE), b"{}")
            .expect("the producer has to be able to publish there");
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_path_ends_in_the_identifier() {
        let panel = PanelDir::create().expect("create the panel directory");

        let last = panel
            .path()
            .file_name()
            .expect("the path has a last segment")
            .to_string_lossy()
            .into_owned();

        assert_eq!(last, format!("ivy-{}", panel.id()));
    }

    #[test]
    fn when_dropped_the_directory_disappears() {
        let path = {
            let panel = PanelDir::create().expect("create the panel directory");
            panel.path().to_path_buf()
        };

        assert!(!path.exists(), "the directory remained: {}", path.display());
    }

    /// The contract file is written by the producer, not by this crate — and even so it takes it
    /// away on closing. Without this test, the deletion would pass the rest of the suite all the same
    /// and would leave a directory per split as soon as someone published for real.
    #[test]
    fn when_dropped_it_takes_the_contract_file_with_it() {
        let path = {
            let panel = PanelDir::create().expect("create the panel directory");
            std::fs::write(panel.path().join(PANEL_FILE), b"{\"lines\":[]}")
                .expect("publish a panel");
            panel.path().to_path_buf()
        };

        assert!(!path.exists(), "the directory remained: {}", path.display());
    }

    /// The endpoint is the contract's, exactly like the file, so closing a console takes it away and
    /// nobody is told the directory remains.
    ///
    /// **Without this the notice comes out on every console close**, which is what a second name in
    /// this directory costs: `remove_dir` is non-recursive on purpose, so one leftover of this
    /// crate's own is enough. The socket is written here as a plain file because what is being
    /// asserted is the deletion, not the binding — a bound socket is a directory entry all the same,
    /// and building one would drag the transport into the contract crate.
    #[test]
    fn when_dropped_it_takes_the_control_endpoint_with_it() {
        let path = {
            let panel = PanelDir::create().expect("create the panel directory");
            std::fs::write(panel.path().join(PANEL_FILE), b"{\"lines\":[]}")
                .expect("publish a panel");
            std::fs::write(panel.path().join(CONTROL_SOCKET), b"").expect("leave an endpoint");
            panel.path().to_path_buf()
        };

        assert!(!path.exists(), "the directory remained: {}", path.display());
    }

    /// The flip side of deletion without recursion: what does not belong to the contract is not
    /// touched, and that is why the directory is still there. It is the property that is wanted, not
    /// an oversight.
    #[test]
    fn a_file_that_is_not_the_contracts_leaves_the_directory_standing() {
        let path = {
            let panel = PanelDir::create().expect("create the panel directory");
            std::fs::write(panel.path().join("record.log"), b"producer's work")
                .expect("write something foreign to the contract");
            panel.path().to_path_buf()
        };

        // The fact is captured and cleanup happens **before** asserting: with the cleanup behind the
        // `assert`, a test failure would leave the directory there forever.
        let survived = path.join("record.log").is_file();
        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&path).expect("clean up what the test left on purpose");

        assert!(
            survived,
            "something that was not the contract's was deleted"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn two_directories_share_neither_identifier_nor_path() {
        let first = PanelDir::create().expect("create the first");
        let second = PanelDir::create().expect("create the second");

        assert_ne!(first.id(), second.id());
        assert_ne!(first.path(), second.path());
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_two_variables_are_the_contracts_and_point_to_the_directory() {
        let panel = PanelDir::create().expect("create the panel directory");

        let [(id_name, id_value), (dir_name, dir_value)] = panel.env();

        assert_eq!(id_name, "PANEL_ID");
        assert_eq!(dir_name, "PANEL_DIR");
        assert_eq!(id_value, OsStr::new(panel.id()));
        assert_eq!(dir_value, panel.path().as_os_str());
    }

    /// Uniqueness is not checked: it is obtained from creation failing. If this stopped failing, two
    /// splits could end up sharing a directory and nothing else in the crate would notice.
    #[test]
    fn creating_over_a_directory_that_already_exists_fails() {
        let panel = PanelDir::create().expect("create the panel directory");

        let again = create_dir(panel.path());

        assert!(
            again.is_err(),
            "creating over what already exists has to fail"
        );
    }

    #[cfg(unix)]
    #[test]
    fn the_directory_cannot_be_read_by_anyone_else() {
        use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;

        let panel = PanelDir::create().expect("create the panel directory");

        let mode = panel
            .path()
            .metadata()
            .expect("read the permissions")
            .permissions()
            .mode()
            & 0o777;

        assert_eq!(
            mode, 0o700,
            "mode {mode:o}, and the panel is going to live there"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_real_identifier_has_eight_hexadecimal_digits() {
        let id = generate_id().expect("generate an identifier");

        assert_eq!(id.len(), 8, "not eight digits: {id}");
        assert!(
            id.chars()
                .all(|d| d.is_ascii_hexdigit() && !d.is_uppercase()),
            "not lowercase hexadecimal: {id}"
        );
    }

    /// With given values, and not just with a drawn one: looking at the length of a random identifier
    /// lets the missing zero-padding through, because it only shows with small numbers — one in
    /// sixteen.
    #[test]
    fn the_format_pads_with_zeros_up_to_eight() {
        assert_eq!(format_id(0), "00000000");
        assert_eq!(format_id(0xff), "000000ff");
        assert_eq!(format_id(0x0abcdef1), "0abcdef1");
        assert_eq!(format_id(u32::MAX), "ffffffff");
    }

    /// An absolute directory written the way this platform writes one: `/run/user/1000` is not
    /// absolute on Windows, which wants a drive.
    fn absolute(unix: &'static str, windows: &'static str) -> PathBuf {
        PathBuf::from(if cfg!(windows) { windows } else { unix })
    }

    /// The fallback the tests hand in, absolute and distinct from any runtime directory, so a row
    /// that falls back is told apart from one that does not.
    fn temporary() -> PathBuf {
        absolute("/var/tmp-for-test", r"C:\Temp\for-test")
    }

    fn base(path: PathBuf, ignored: &[&'static str]) -> Base {
        Base {
            path,
            ignored: ignored.to_vec(),
        }
    }

    /// The case that does not fail and does the wrong thing: with the variable **defined and empty**
    /// the path would come out relative, and the panel directory would end up inside the working
    /// directory. Empty is unset, so nothing is reported.
    #[test]
    fn an_empty_environment_variable_does_not_count_as_a_path() {
        assert_eq!(
            base_dir_from(Some(OsStr::new("")), temporary()),
            base(temporary(), &[])
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn without_a_runtime_directory_it_falls_back_to_the_system_temporary() {
        assert_eq!(base_dir_from(None, temporary()), base(temporary(), &[]));
    }

    #[test]
    fn with_a_runtime_directory_that_one_is_used() {
        let runtime = absolute("/run/user/1000", r"C:\run\user\1000");
        assert_eq!(
            base_dir_from(Some(runtime.as_os_str()), temporary()),
            base(runtime, &[])
        );
    }

    /// **A relative runtime directory is not one**, as the XDG specification says: it would put
    /// every console's directory under the application's working directory, and the MCP bridge
    /// refuses a relative `PANEL_DIR` as not a whole identity. Somebody wrote it, so it is reported.
    #[test]
    fn a_relative_runtime_directory_does_not_count_as_one() {
        assert_eq!(
            base_dir_from(Some(OsStr::new("run")), temporary()),
            base(temporary(), &["XDG_RUNTIME_DIR"])
        );
        #[cfg(windows)]
        assert_eq!(
            base_dir_from(Some(OsStr::new(r"\run")), temporary()),
            base(temporary(), &["XDG_RUNTIME_DIR"]),
            "a rooted path with no drive is not absolute either"
        );
    }

    /// **The fallback is held to the same rule.** TMPDIR reaches `temp_dir` as written on Unix,
    /// and a relative or empty one would land every console in the working directory all the same
    /// — whether the runtime variable was absent, empty or relative.
    #[cfg(unix)]
    #[test]
    fn a_relative_system_temporary_falls_back_to_tmp() {
        let tmp = PathBuf::from("/tmp");
        assert_eq!(base_dir_from(None, PathBuf::new()), base(tmp.clone(), &[]));
        assert_eq!(
            base_dir_from(None, PathBuf::from("tmp")),
            base(tmp.clone(), &["TMPDIR"])
        );
        assert_eq!(
            base_dir_from(Some(OsStr::new("")), PathBuf::from("tmp")),
            base(tmp.clone(), &["TMPDIR"])
        );
        assert_eq!(
            base_dir_from(Some(OsStr::new("run")), PathBuf::from("tmp")),
            base(tmp, &["XDG_RUNTIME_DIR", "TMPDIR"])
        );
    }

    /// **A person is told only when a variable they wrote was ignored**, and is told which one and
    /// where the consoles went instead.
    #[test]
    fn the_warning_names_what_was_ignored_and_what_was_used() {
        assert_eq!(warning(&base(temporary(), &[])), None);
        let told = warning(&base(PathBuf::from("/tmp"), &["XDG_RUNTIME_DIR", "TMPDIR"]))
            .expect("an ignored variable is said");
        assert!(told.contains("XDG_RUNTIME_DIR and TMPDIR"), "{told}");
        assert!(told.contains("/tmp"), "{told}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn deleting_what_is_no_longer_there_does_not_deserve_a_notice() {
        let path = {
            let panel = PanelDir::create().expect("create the panel directory");
            panel.path().to_path_buf()
        };

        assert!(
            remove(&path).is_none(),
            "it warned about a directory that was no longer there"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn what_is_left_undeleted_does_deserve_a_notice() {
        let panel = PanelDir::create().expect("create the panel directory");
        std::fs::write(panel.path().join("record.log"), b"from the producer")
            .expect("write something foreign to the contract");

        let warning = remove(panel.path());
        std::fs::remove_dir_all(panel.path()).expect("clean up what the test left on purpose");

        assert!(
            warning.is_some(),
            "the directory survived with something inside and nobody said so"
        );
    }
}
```

### crates/panel-contract/src/windows_directory.rs

```
//! Windows creation and validation for private pane directories.
//!
//! The ACL is applied in `CreateDirectoryW`, so no permissive directory is visible between
//! creation and tightening. Inherited paths are opened without following reparse points and their
//! owner, protected DACL, type, and exact allow entries are checked through that handle.

use std::ffi::OsStr;
use std::ffi::c_void;
use std::io;
use std::os::windows::ffi::OsStrExt;
use std::path::Path;
use std::ptr::{null, null_mut};

use windows_sys::Win32::Foundation::*;
use windows_sys::Win32::Security::Authorization::*;
use windows_sys::Win32::Security::*;
use windows_sys::Win32::Storage::FileSystem::*;
use windows_sys::Win32::System::Threading::*;

fn error() -> io::Error {
    io::Error::last_os_error()
}
fn wide(value: &OsStr) -> Vec<u16> {
    value.encode_wide().chain(Some(0)).collect()
}

struct OwnedHandle(HANDLE);
impl Drop for OwnedHandle {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        unsafe {
            CloseHandle(self.0);
        }
    }
}
struct OwnedDescriptor(PSECURITY_DESCRIPTOR);
impl Drop for OwnedDescriptor {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        unsafe {
            LocalFree(self.0);
        }
    }
}

fn token_user() -> io::Result<(OwnedHandle, Vec<usize>)> {
    let mut token = null_mut();
    if unsafe { OpenProcessToken(GetCurrentProcess(), TOKEN_QUERY, &mut token) } == 0 {
        return Err(error());
    }
    let token = OwnedHandle(token);
    let mut needed = 0;
    unsafe {
        GetTokenInformation(token.0, TokenUser, null_mut(), 0, &mut needed);
    }
    let mut bytes = vec![0usize; (needed as usize).div_ceil(std::mem::size_of::<usize>())];
    if unsafe {
        GetTokenInformation(
            token.0,
            TokenUser,
            bytes.as_mut_ptr().cast(),
            needed,
            &mut needed,
        )
    } == 0
    {
        return Err(error());
    }
    Ok((token, bytes))
}

fn descriptor(children: bool) -> io::Result<OwnedDescriptor> {
    let (_token, user) = token_user()?;
    let token_user = unsafe { &*(user.as_ptr().cast::<TOKEN_USER>()) };
    let mut sid = null_mut();
    if unsafe { ConvertSidToStringSidW(token_user.User.Sid, &mut sid) } == 0 {
        return Err(error());
    }
    let mut length = 0;
    unsafe {
        while *sid.add(length) != 0 {
            length += 1;
        }
    }
    let user_sid = String::from_utf16_lossy(unsafe { std::slice::from_raw_parts(sid, length) });
    unsafe {
        LocalFree(sid.cast());
    }
    let inherit = if children { "OICI" } else { "" };
    let system_ace = if user_sid == "S-1-5-18" {
        String::new()
    } else {
        format!("(A;{inherit};FA;;;SY)")
    };
    let sddl: Vec<u16> = format!("O:{user_sid}D:P(A;{inherit};FA;;;{user_sid}){system_ace}")
        .encode_utf16()
        .chain(Some(0))
        .collect();
    let mut output = null_mut();
    let mut size = 0;
    if unsafe {
        ConvertStringSecurityDescriptorToSecurityDescriptorW(
            sddl.as_ptr(),
            SDDL_REVISION_1,
            &mut output,
            &mut size,
        )
    } == 0
    {
        return Err(error());
    }
    Ok(OwnedDescriptor(output))
}

pub fn create(path: &Path) -> io::Result<()> {
    let security = descriptor(true)?;
    let attributes = SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES {
        nLength: std::mem::size_of::<SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES>() as u32,
        lpSecurityDescriptor: security.0,
        bInheritHandle: 0,
    };
    let path = wide(path.as_os_str());
    if unsafe { CreateDirectoryW(path.as_ptr(), &attributes) } == 0 {
        return Err(error());
    }
    Ok(())
}

pub fn validate(path: &Path) -> io::Result<()> {
    let path_wide = no_follow_path(path.as_os_str());
    let handle = unsafe {
        CreateFileW(
            path_wide.as_ptr(),
            READ_CONTROL | FILE_READ_ATTRIBUTES,
            FILE_SHARE_READ | FILE_SHARE_WRITE | FILE_SHARE_DELETE,
            null(),
            OPEN_EXISTING,
            FILE_FLAG_BACKUP_SEMANTICS | FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT,
            null_mut(),
        )
    };
    if handle == INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE {
        return Err(error());
    }
    let handle = OwnedHandle(handle);
    let mut info = unsafe { std::mem::zeroed::<BY_HANDLE_FILE_INFORMATION>() };
    if unsafe { GetFileInformationByHandle(handle.0, &mut info) } == 0 {
        return Err(error());
    }
    if info.dwFileAttributes & FILE_ATTRIBUTE_DIRECTORY == 0
        || info.dwFileAttributes & FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT != 0
    {
        return Err(io::Error::new(
            io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied,
            format!("{} is not a private directory of this user", path.display()),
        ));
    }
    let (token, token_bytes) = token_user()?;
    let user = unsafe { &*(token_bytes.as_ptr().cast::<TOKEN_USER>()) };
    let mut owner = null_mut();
    let mut dacl = null_mut();
    let mut descriptor = null_mut();
    let result = unsafe {
        GetSecurityInfo(
            handle.0,
            SE_FILE_OBJECT,
            OWNER_SECURITY_INFORMATION | DACL_SECURITY_INFORMATION,
            &mut owner,
            null_mut(),
            &mut dacl,
            null_mut(),
            &mut descriptor,
        )
    };
    if result != ERROR_SUCCESS {
        return Err(io::Error::from_raw_os_error(result as i32));
    }
    let descriptor = OwnedDescriptor(descriptor);
    if owner.is_null() || dacl.is_null() || unsafe { EqualSid(owner, user.User.Sid) } == 0 {
        return Err(io::Error::new(
            io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied,
            format!("{} is not a private directory of this user", path.display()),
        ));
    }
    let mut control = 0;
    let mut revision = 0;
    if unsafe { GetSecurityDescriptorControl(descriptor.0, &mut control, &mut revision) } == 0
        || control & SE_DACL_PROTECTED == 0
    {
        return Err(io::Error::new(
            io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied,
            format!("{} is not a private directory of this user", path.display()),
        ));
    }
    let mut acl_info = ACL_SIZE_INFORMATION {
        AceCount: 0,
        AclBytesInUse: 0,
        AclBytesFree: 0,
    };
    if unsafe {
        GetAclInformation(
            dacl,
            (&mut acl_info as *mut ACL_SIZE_INFORMATION).cast(),
            std::mem::size_of::<ACL_SIZE_INFORMATION>() as u32,
            AclSizeInformation,
        )
    } == 0
    {
        return Err(error());
    }
    #[repr(align(4))]
    struct AlignedSid([u8; 12]);
    let mut system_sid = AlignedSid([1, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 5, 18, 0, 0, 0]);
    let system_sid_ptr = system_sid.0.as_mut_ptr().cast::<c_void>();
    // S-1-5-18 is the fixed LocalSystem SID representation.
    let system_is_user = unsafe { EqualSid(user.User.Sid, system_sid_ptr) } != 0;
    if acl_info.AceCount != if system_is_user { 1 } else { 2 } {
        return Err(io::Error::new(
            io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied,
            format!("{} is not a private directory of this user", path.display()),
        ));
    }
    let expected_flags = (OBJECT_INHERIT_ACE | CONTAINER_INHERIT_ACE) as u8;
    let mut user_found = false;
    let mut system_found = false;
    for index in 0..acl_info.AceCount {
        let mut ace = null_mut();
        if unsafe { GetAce(dacl, index, &mut ace) } == 0 {
            return Err(error());
        }
        let header = unsafe { &*(ace.cast::<ACE_HEADER>()) };
        if header.AceType != 0 || header.AceFlags != expected_flags {
            return Err(io::Error::new(
                io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied,
                format!("{} is not a private directory of this user", path.display()),
            ));
        }
        let allow = unsafe { &*(ace.cast::<ACCESS_ALLOWED_ACE>()) };
        if allow.Mask != FILE_ALL_ACCESS {
            return Err(io::Error::new(
                io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied,
                format!("{} is not a private directory of this user", path.display()),
            ));
        }
        let sid_ptr = (&allow.SidStart as *const u32).cast_mut().cast::<c_void>();
        if unsafe { EqualSid(sid_ptr, user.User.Sid) } != 0 {
            user_found = true;
        } else if unsafe { EqualSid(sid_ptr, system_sid_ptr) } != 0 {
            system_found = true;
        } else {
            return Err(io::Error::new(
                io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied,
                format!("{} is not a private directory of this user", path.display()),
            ));
        }
    }
    let _ = token;
    if !user_found || (!system_is_user && !system_found) {
        return Err(io::Error::new(
            io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied,
            format!("{} is not a private directory of this user", path.display()),
        ));
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn no_follow_path(path: &OsStr) -> Vec<u16> {
    let mut units: Vec<u16> = path.encode_wide().collect();
    loop {
        let len = units.len();
        if len > 3 && (units[len - 1] == b'\\' as u16 || units[len - 1] == b'/' as u16) {
            units.pop();
        } else if len > 3
            && units[len - 1] == b'.' as u16
            && (units[len - 2] == b'\\' as u16 || units[len - 2] == b'/' as u16)
        {
            units.truncate(len - 2);
        } else {
            break;
        }
    }
    units.push(0);
    units
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn fresh_panel_directory_has_the_required_owner_and_protected_acl() {
        let panel = crate::PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        validate(panel.path()).unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn inherited_file_and_missing_path_are_rejected() {
        let panel = crate::PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        let file = panel.path().join("ordinary-file");
        std::fs::write(&file, "x").unwrap();
        assert!(validate(&file).is_err());
        assert!(validate(&panel.path().join("missing")).is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn inherited_reparse_point_is_rejected_even_with_terminal_dot_or_separator() {
        let panel = crate::PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        let target = panel.path().with_extension("target");
        std::fs::create_dir(&target).unwrap();
        let alias = panel.path().with_extension("alias");
        let junction = format!("mklink /J \"{}\" \"{}\"", alias.display(), target.display());
        let status = std::process::Command::new("cmd.exe")
            .args(["/d", "/c", &junction])
            .status()
            .unwrap();
        assert!(
            status.success(),
            "could not create junction fixture: {junction}"
        );
        for suffix in ["", "\\", "\\.", "\\.\\.\\"] {
            let candidate = std::path::PathBuf::from(format!("{}{suffix}", alias.display()));
            assert!(
                validate(&candidate).is_err(),
                "accepted reparse path {}",
                candidate.display()
            );
        }
        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir(&alias);
        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir(&target);
    }

    #[test]
    fn null_and_broad_dacls_are_rejected() {
        let panel = crate::PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        set_test_dacl(panel.path(), None, true);
        assert!(validate(panel.path()).is_err(), "accepted a null DACL");
        let panel = crate::PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        set_test_dacl(panel.path(), Some("D:(A;;FA;;;WD)"), true);
        assert!(validate(panel.path()).is_err(), "accepted a broad DACL");
    }

    #[test]
    fn otherwise_exact_but_unprotected_directory_dacl_is_rejected() {
        let panel = crate::PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        let (_token, user) = token_user().unwrap();
        let user = unsafe { &*(user.as_ptr().cast::<TOKEN_USER>()) };
        let mut sid = null_mut();
        assert_ne!(
            unsafe { ConvertSidToStringSidW(user.User.Sid, &mut sid) },
            0
        );
        let mut length = 0;
        unsafe {
            while *sid.add(length) != 0 {
                length += 1;
            }
        }
        let user_sid = String::from_utf16_lossy(unsafe { std::slice::from_raw_parts(sid, length) });
        unsafe {
            LocalFree(sid.cast());
        }
        let system_ace = if user_sid == "S-1-5-18" {
            String::new()
        } else {
            "(A;OICI;FA;;;SY)".to_owned()
        };
        let sddl = format!("D:(A;OICI;FA;;;{user_sid}){system_ace}");
        set_test_dacl(panel.path(), Some(&sddl), false);
        assert!(
            validate(panel.path()).is_err(),
            "accepted the right allow ACEs on an unprotected DACL"
        );
    }

    fn set_test_dacl(path: &Path, sddl: Option<&str>, protected: bool) {
        let path_wide = wide(path.as_os_str());
        let handle = unsafe {
            CreateFileW(
                path_wide.as_ptr(),
                READ_CONTROL | WRITE_DAC,
                FILE_SHARE_READ | FILE_SHARE_WRITE | FILE_SHARE_DELETE,
                null(),
                OPEN_EXISTING,
                FILE_FLAG_BACKUP_SEMANTICS | FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT,
                null_mut(),
            )
        };
        assert_ne!(handle, INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE);
        let handle = OwnedHandle(handle);
        let descriptor = if let Some(sddl) = sddl {
            let descriptor_sddl: Vec<u16> = sddl.encode_utf16().chain(Some(0)).collect();
            let mut descriptor = null_mut();
            let mut size = 0;
            assert_ne!(
                unsafe {
                    ConvertStringSecurityDescriptorToSecurityDescriptorW(
                        descriptor_sddl.as_ptr(),
                        SDDL_REVISION_1,
                        &mut descriptor,
                        &mut size,
                    )
                },
                0
            );
            Some(OwnedDescriptor(descriptor))
        } else {
            None
        };
        let acl = if let Some(descriptor) = &descriptor {
            let mut present = 0;
            let mut dacl_defaulted = 0;
            let mut dacl = null_mut();
            assert_ne!(
                unsafe {
                    GetSecurityDescriptorDacl(
                        descriptor.0,
                        &mut present,
                        &mut dacl,
                        &mut dacl_defaulted,
                    )
                },
                0
            );
            assert_ne!(present, 0);
            dacl
        } else {
            null_mut()
        };
        let protection = if protected {
            PROTECTED_DACL_SECURITY_INFORMATION
        } else {
            UNPROTECTED_DACL_SECURITY_INFORMATION
        };
        let result = unsafe {
            SetSecurityInfo(
                handle.0,
                SE_FILE_OBJECT,
                DACL_SECURITY_INFORMATION | protection,
                null_mut(),
                null_mut(),
                acl,
                null_mut(),
            )
        };
        assert_eq!(result, ERROR_SUCCESS);
    }
}
```

### crates/panel-control/Cargo.toml

```
[package]
name = "panel-control"
version.workspace = true
edition.workspace = true
license.workspace = true

[dependencies]
# The document this channel carries **is** the panel document, so its parser, its ceiling and the
# endpoint's name all come from the contract rather than being written a second time here. A second
# parser is how two transports of one contract start disagreeing field by field.
panel-contract = { path = "../panel-contract" }

# The wire format is newline-delimited JSON, and it is read from a socket somebody else fills: the
# same reason the contract crate states for taking it.
#
# **`raw_value` is on so the document travels as the bytes the producer wrote.** Without it the
# envelope was parsed into a value and the document re-serialised compact, so the ceiling was checked
# against the compact form: a pretty-printed document over 256 KiB could compact under it and be
# accepted over the channel while the same file was refused. `RawValue` keeps the producer's own text
# and the parser measures what was sent.
serde_json = { version = "1", features = ["raw_value"] }

# **`serde`'s derive is what lets the request be read with a raw document field.** `serde_json`'s
# `Map` only deserialises values, so the only way to keep the producer's own text for one field is a
# struct with a `RawValue` member; the derive is that struct. `serde` is already in the tree under
# `serde_json`, so this adds no compiled crate the workspace did not have.
serde = { version = "1", features = ["derive"] }

# **One dependency that already knows how to be a Unix socket on POSIX and a named pipe on Windows**,
# behind one name-building API, in blocking mode. The three alternatives and why each was refused are
# in the delivery's design; the short of it is that the hand-rolled half is raw `CreateNamedPipeW` on
# a platform nobody in this project has run yet, `mio` is only here transitively through `notify` and
# offers the one shape this design does not want, and `tokio` is a runtime for a server whose whole
# concurrency is one blocking thread per pane.
#
# It is **not** in `Cargo.lock` or in this machine's registry cache before this delivery, so the first
# build of this branch fetches it. Worth knowing before running the delivery offline.
interprocess = "2.4"

# The Codex relay accepts only authenticated loopback WebSockets; protocol framing
# belongs to the RFC implementation, not a second handwritten parser.
tungstenite = { version = "0.30", default-features = false, features = ["handshake"] }
# Route cutover and a complete MCP operation share an operating-system file lock.
fs2 = "0.4"
getrandom = "0.3"

# Closing a channel wakes its blocking accept without opening another client connection. The Unix
# endpoint polls its listener beside a private stream pair; `libc` supplies that descriptor wait.
[target.'cfg(unix)'.dependencies]
libc = "0.2"

[target.'cfg(windows)'.dependencies]
windows-sys = { version = "0.61.2", features = [
    "Win32_Foundation",
    "Win32_Graphics_Gdi",
    "Win32_Security",
    "Win32_Security_Authorization",
    "Win32_Storage_FileSystem",
    "Win32_System_IO",
    "Win32_System_LibraryLoader",
    "Win32_System_Pipes",
    "Win32_System_Threading",
    "Win32_UI_WindowsAndMessaging",
] }

[[bin]]
name = "panel-mcp"
path = "src/bin/panel-mcp.rs"

[[bin]]
name = "ivy-session"
path = "src/bin/ivy-session.rs"

[[bin]]
name = "ivy-codex"
path = "src/bin/ivy-codex.rs"
```

### crates/panel-control/src/client.rs

```
//! The producer's side of the channel.
//!
//! **It is here and not in whoever speaks MCP**, because what a request looks like on the wire is
//! the contract's business and not the vocabulary's: the day the same channel is spoken over
//! something else, that vocabulary is the piece that goes and this is the piece that stays.

use std::io::{self, Read, Write};
use std::path::Path;

use crate::endpoint::Stream;

use crate::endpoint;
use crate::wire::{MAX_REQUEST, Side};

/// The pane identity a program inherited, **when it is whole**: `PANEL_ID` present, non-empty and
/// text, and `PANEL_DIR` present and an absolute path.
///
/// **The one rule the MCP bridge applies, to its handshake and to its tool calls alike**, because
/// two rules in one process gave two answers: the handshake said «no console» for an empty
/// identifier while the first tool call connected with it. Bringing the session helper under it was
/// left out of that decision (D88). Empty counts as absent — a client configuration that
/// interpolates `${PANEL_ID:-}` produces one, and on Windows an empty identifier names a single
/// machine-wide endpoint. An identifier that is not text cannot be one this terminal generated: the
/// identity is the pair (D7), and on Windows, where the identifier names the endpoint, converting
/// it lossily would connect to a mangled name. A relative directory cannot be one either:
/// it would resolve against the working directory and reach whatever socket sits there.
///
/// **It takes the values instead of reading them** so it can be tested without `set_var`, which
/// is undefined behaviour in a suite that launches processes in parallel.
pub fn whole_identity<'a>(
    id: Option<&'a std::ffi::OsStr>,
    dir: Option<&'a std::ffi::OsStr>,
) -> Option<(&'a str, &'a Path)> {
    let id = id?.to_str().filter(|id| !id.is_empty())?;
    let dir = Path::new(dir?);
    dir.is_absolute().then_some((id, dir))
}

/// Parses the app identity separately from the stable two-variable pane address.
pub fn server_pid(value: Option<&std::ffi::OsStr>) -> io::Result<u32> {
    value
        .and_then(std::ffi::OsStr::to_str)
        .filter(|text| !text.is_empty() && text.bytes().all(|byte| byte.is_ascii_digit()))
        .and_then(|text| text.parse::<u32>().ok())
        .filter(|pid| *pid != 0)
        .ok_or_else(|| io::Error::new(io::ErrorKind::InvalidInput,
            "PANEL_SERVER_PID must be the nonzero decimal PID inherited from Ivy; check how your client forwards it"))
}

#[cfg(windows)]
pub(crate) fn inherited_server_pid() -> io::Result<u32> {
    server_pid(std::env::var_os("PANEL_SERVER_PID").as_deref())
}

/// A connection to one pane's channel.
///
/// **The hold this delivery gives a source lasts as long as this value does**, which is why it is a
/// type rather than a function per request: publishing twice through two connections would claim the
/// pane twice and be refused the second time.
pub struct Producer {
    stream: Stream,
}

impl Producer {
    /// Opens the channel of the pane named by the two variables the program inherited.
    ///
    /// Windows also requires the inherited server PID, checked before writing request bytes.
    /// No identifier is sent on the wire. Which pane this reaches is decided by
    /// which endpoint was opened, so there is nothing in any message for anybody to trust.
    ///
    /// **A whole identity or nothing, and never a fallback** ([`whole_identity`]). Which of the two
    /// variables names the endpoint is the platform's business ([`endpoint::name_of`]), so half an
    /// identity is a missing one on one platform and harmless on the other — and defaulting the
    /// identifier to the empty string resolved every pane on Windows to a single machine-wide name.
    ///
    /// **Two identity refusals, because they have two causes** — a platform with no channel and a
    /// console that cannot be reached are refused apart, after them. Neither variable carrying
    /// anything — absent or empty, which is what a client interpolating `${PANEL_ID:-}` outside any
    /// pane hands over — is a program outside any pane. One carrying something but the identity not
    /// whole is a client that forwards them badly — a list of variables missing one, a value
    /// mangled on the way — and saying «not in a pane» there would send whoever reads it looking in
    /// the wrong place.
    pub fn of_this_pane() -> io::Result<Self> {
        let [id, dir] = panel_contract::ENV_NAMES.map(std::env::var_os);
        let unset =
            |value: &Option<std::ffi::OsString>| value.as_deref().is_none_or(|v| v.is_empty());
        let outside = unset(&id) && unset(&dir);
        let (id, dir) = whole_identity(id.as_deref(), dir.as_deref()).ok_or_else(|| {
            if outside {
                io::Error::new(
                    io::ErrorKind::NotFound,
                    "this program is not running inside a pane of this terminal",
                )
            } else {
                io::Error::new(
                    io::ErrorKind::InvalidInput,
                    "PANEL_ID or PANEL_DIR is set, but the two are not a whole pane identity (one \
                     is missing or empty, the identifier is not text, or the directory is not \
                     absolute): check how your client forwards them",
                )
            }
        })?;
        // Where no channel binds, a live console is unreachable by construction: say that, not
        // that the console went away.
        if !endpoint::BINDS {
            return Err(io::Error::new(
                io::ErrorKind::Unsupported,
                "this platform has no control channel yet: publish this console's panel as a file \
                 in PANEL_DIR",
            ));
        }

        // Any failure to reach a whole identity — most often the console closed and its directory
        // gone — is said naming the directory, which the bare system error does not. Its kind is
        // chosen to differ from «outside any pane»; the original error survives only as text.
        endpoint::private_to_this_user(dir)?;
        Self::of(dir, id).map_err(|failure| {
            io::Error::new(
                io::ErrorKind::NotConnected,
                format!(
                    "this console's panel could not be reached at {} ({failure})",
                    dir.display()
                ),
            )
        })
    }

    /// The same, for a pane named directly. **What the tests use**, and what a producer that manages
    /// several panes of its own would use. It takes the pair as given: the rule of
    /// [`whole_identity`] is the caller's to apply.
    pub fn of(dir: &Path, id: &str) -> io::Result<Self> {
        #[cfg(windows)]
        {
            Self::of_server(dir, id, inherited_server_pid()?)
        }
        #[cfg(not(windows))]
        Ok(Self {
            stream: endpoint::connect(dir, id)?,
        })
    }

    /// Opens a Windows pane with the expected app PID supplied by its launch environment.
    #[cfg(windows)]
    pub fn of_server(dir: &Path, id: &str, expected_pid: u32) -> io::Result<Self> {
        endpoint::private_to_this_user(dir)?;
        Ok(Self {
            stream: endpoint::connect_authenticated(dir, id, expected_pid)?,
        })
    }

    /// Publishes a document, and returns what the window answered.
    ///
    /// **The document is written back out on one line, and that is this side's job rather than the
    /// producer's.** The wire is framed by newlines, and the natural thing for somebody writing JSON
    /// by hand — or for any pretty-printer — is to produce several lines; interpolating that raw
    /// split the request across frames, so the pane read a first line that was not valid JSON and
    /// then a tail of nothing. Compacting here costs one parse on the producer's own thread and
    /// makes the framing impossible to break from outside.
    ///
    /// It also means a document that is not JSON at all is refused **here, to its author**, instead
    /// of travelling to the window to come back as an answer nobody can act on.
    pub fn publish(&mut self, source: &str, document: &str) -> io::Result<Answered> {
        let document: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(document).map_err(|failure| {
            io::Error::new(
                io::ErrorKind::InvalidInput,
                format!("the document is not JSON ({failure})"),
            )
        })?;
        // `Value`'s `Display` is the compact form: no newline can survive it, which is the property
        // the framing rests on.
        let escaped_source = serde_json::Value::String(source.to_owned());
        writeln!(
            self.stream,
            "{{\"tool\":\"panel_set\",\"source\":{escaped_source},\"document\":{document}}}"
        )?;
        self.stream.flush()?;

        self.read_reply()
    }

    /// Opens a console beside this pane's own, and returns the handle the window gave it.
    ///
    /// **The handle is what the rest of the verbs are named by**, and it is opaque on purpose: it
    /// belongs to this connection, and a second connection cannot use it even if it reads it.
    pub fn open_a_pane(
        &mut self,
        side: Side,
        program: Option<&str>,
        args: &[String],
    ) -> io::Result<Answered> {
        // **Built as a value and not interpolated**, so a program name with a quote in it cannot
        // break the request it travels in — the same hazard the reply's escaping answers.
        let mut request = serde_json::json!({"tool": "split_open", "side": side.name()});
        if let Some(program) = program {
            request["program"] = serde_json::Value::String(program.to_owned());
        }
        if !args.is_empty() {
            request["args"] = serde_json::json!(args);
        }
        self.ask(request)
    }

    /// Reads a console this connection opened: **the visible screen unless the scrollback is asked
    /// for**, which is the contract's default.
    pub fn read_a_pane(&mut self, pane: &str, scrollback: bool) -> io::Result<Answered> {
        self.ask(serde_json::json!({
            "tool": "split_read",
            "pane": pane,
            "scrollback": scrollback,
        }))
    }

    /// Asks for the document this pane's panel is showing.
    ///
    /// **It takes no hold and needs none**: it is for the producer that has *not* claimed the pane —
    /// one that restarted, one whose context was compacted away, a second tool taking the console
    /// over — and it answers with a document that arrived by file just as readily as with one this
    /// connection published.
    pub fn read_the_document(&mut self) -> io::Result<Answered> {
        self.ask(serde_json::json!({"tool": "panel_get"}))
    }

    /// Publishes a project plan document. The endpoint's pane determines the project; `repo` is
    /// carried for producer compatibility but is intentionally ignored by the receiver.
    pub fn publish_plan(&mut self, document: &str, repo: Option<&str>) -> io::Result<Answered> {
        let document: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(document).map_err(|failure| {
            io::Error::new(
                io::ErrorKind::InvalidInput,
                format!("the plan is not JSON ({failure})"),
            )
        })?;
        let mut request = serde_json::json!({"tool": "plan_set", "document": document});
        if let Some(repo) = repo {
            request["repo"] = serde_json::Value::String(repo.to_owned());
        }
        self.ask(request)
    }

    /// Reads the published project plan for this pane's project.
    pub fn read_plan(&mut self, repo: Option<&str>) -> io::Result<Answered> {
        let mut request = serde_json::json!({"tool": "plan_get"});
        if let Some(repo) = repo {
            request["repo"] = serde_json::Value::String(repo.to_owned());
        }
        self.ask(request)
    }

    /// Retires the published project plan for this pane's project.
    pub fn retire_plan(&mut self, repo: Option<&str>) -> io::Result<Answered> {
        let mut request = serde_json::json!({"tool": "plan_retire"});
        if let Some(repo) = repo {
            request["repo"] = serde_json::Value::String(repo.to_owned());
        }
        self.ask(request)
    }

    /// Changes part of the document this connection published, leaving the rest of it alone.
    ///
    /// **It needs the hold that a publication takes**, unlike [`Producer::read_the_document`]: there
    /// is no state where a connection holds a pane and there is nothing to patch, so *nothing has
    /// been published yet* and *this pane's panel is its file's* are one refusal, and it names the
    /// move — publish first.
    ///
    /// **What happens here is the framing, not the reading.** The ops are checked to be JSON and
    /// compacted, for the same two reasons a document is: a list that is not JSON at all is refused
    /// to its author instead of travelling to the window, and a compact line cannot carry the newline
    /// the framing rests on. **What each op says is decided at the other end** — by
    /// `panel_contract::parse_ops_noting`, on the connection's thread inside the application — so an op
    /// nobody serves or a field that is not patchable travels and comes back as a refusal.
    pub fn patch(&mut self, ops: &str) -> io::Result<Answered> {
        let ops: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(ops).map_err(|failure| {
            io::Error::new(
                io::ErrorKind::InvalidInput,
                format!("the ops are not JSON ({failure})"),
            )
        })?;
        self.ask(serde_json::json!({"tool": "panel_patch", "ops": ops}))
    }

    /// Intentionally ends the session currently holding this pane's panel.
    pub fn retire(&mut self) -> io::Result<Answered> {
        self.ask(serde_json::json!({"tool": "panel_retire"}))
    }

    /// Closes a console this connection opened.
    pub fn close_a_pane(&mut self, pane: &str) -> io::Result<Answered> {
        self.ask(serde_json::json!({"tool": "split_close", "pane": pane}))
    }

    /// Writes one request line and reads its answer, **the one place that talks to the socket**.
    ///
    /// `Value`'s `Display` is the compact form, so a request can never carry a newline and break the
    /// framing the wire rests on.
    fn ask(&mut self, request: serde_json::Value) -> io::Result<Answered> {
        writeln!(self.stream, "{request}")?;
        self.stream.flush()?;

        self.read_reply()
    }

    /// Reads one reply line, bounded the same way a request is.
    fn read_reply(&mut self) -> io::Result<Answered> {
        let mut line = Vec::new();
        let mut byte = [0_u8; 1];
        loop {
            match self.stream.read(&mut byte)? {
                0 => {
                    return Err(io::Error::new(
                        io::ErrorKind::UnexpectedEof,
                        "the pane closed the channel without answering",
                    ));
                }
                _ if byte[0] == b'\n' => break,
                _ => line.push(byte[0]),
            }
            if line.len() > MAX_REQUEST {
                return Err(io::Error::other("the answer goes over the ceiling"));
            }
        }

        // **The document is kept as the bytes that arrived, and every other field is not.** A
        // `serde_json::Value` cannot do it: without `preserve_order` its map is sorted, so a document
        // published as `{"updated_at":…,"phase":…,"lines":…}` came back
        // `{"lines":…,"phase":…,"updated_at":…}` — the same panel, and not the same bytes. That
        // defeats the one reason the reply carries the document unescaped, and it made this type's own
        // promise —«as the JSON text it arrived as»— false. Borrowing it as a [`RawValue`] is the same
        // answer the request side already gives for the same reason.
        let raw: Reply<'_> = serde_json::from_slice(&line)
            .map_err(|failure| io::Error::other(failure.to_string()))?;
        // **The field travels, not its name.** Looking one up by a string would put a `_ => None` arm
        // between a refusal and the caller: a typo would compile, every refusal would collapse into
```
