```
                json!({"id":inv["id"],"result":{"data":[],"nextCursor":null}}),
                &mut upstream,
                &mut client,
            )
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(client.0.pop(), Some(original));
        assert!(
            crate::codex_route::lock_route(&route)
                .unwrap()
                .identity
                .active
        );
        drop(state);
        std::fs::remove_dir_all(home).unwrap();
    }
    #[test]
    fn inventory_detects_legacy_name_and_complete_ivy_tool_set() {
        let inventory = |name: &str, tools: Value| json!({"result":{"data":[{"serverInfo":{"name":name},"runtimeStatus":"connected","tools":tools,"toolsError":null}],"nextCursor":null}});
        assert!(!inventory_excludes_ivy(&inventory(
            "agent-terminal panel",
            json!({})
        )));
        assert!(!inventory_excludes_ivy(&inventory(
            "renamed",
            json!({"panel_set":{},"panel_get":{},"split_open":{},"split_read":{},"split_close":{}})
        )));
        assert!(inventory_excludes_ivy(&inventory(
            "other",
            json!({"panel_get":{}})
        )));
    }

    #[test]
    fn exit_cancels_epoch_without_waiting_for_a_held_route() {
        let home = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("ivy-cancel-exit-{}", nonce().unwrap()));
        std::fs::create_dir(&home).unwrap();
        let registry = Registry::for_home(&home).unwrap();
        let route = registry.pending().unwrap();
        let epoch = nonce().unwrap();
        let thread = "01234567-89ab-cdef-0123-456789abcdef";
        registry
            .bind(thread, &route, "old", &home.join("pane"), &epoch)
            .unwrap();
        let held = crate::codex_route::lock_route(&route).unwrap();
        let start = Instant::now();
        registry.cancel_epoch(&epoch).unwrap();
        assert!(
            start.elapsed() < Duration::from_secs(1),
            "cancellation waited for the route"
        );
        drop(held);
        assert!(
            !crate::codex_route::lock_route(&route)
                .unwrap()
                .identity
                .active
        );
        assert!(
            registry
                .bind(thread, &route, "late", &home.join("pane"), &epoch)
                .is_err()
        );
        std::fs::remove_dir_all(home).unwrap();
    }
    #[test]
    fn closing_console_cannot_reactivate_route_after_relay_is_abandoned() {
        let home = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("ivy-exit-state-{}", nonce().unwrap()));
        std::fs::create_dir(&home).unwrap();
        let mut state = routing(&home);
        let route = state.registry.pending().unwrap();
        state.exit_route.lock().unwrap().closing = true;
        let mut peer = Peer::default();
        let original =
            json!({"id":9,"result":{"thread":{"id":"01234567-89ab-cdef-0123-456789abcdef"}}});
        state
            .finish(
                Attachment {
                    route: Some(route.clone()),
                    fresh: true,
                    servers: vec!["ivy".into()],
                    needs_inventory: false,
                },
                original,
                &mut peer,
            )
            .unwrap();
        assert!(peer.0[0].get("error").is_some());
        assert!(
            !crate::codex_route::lock_route(&route)
                .unwrap()
                .identity
                .active
        );
        drop(state);
        std::fs::remove_dir_all(home).unwrap();
    }
    #[test]
    fn unfinished_cleanup_thread_does_not_block_console() {
        let (tx, rx) = std::sync::mpsc::channel::<()>();
        let handle = std::thread::spawn(move || {
            let _ = rx.recv_timeout(Duration::from_secs(2));
        });
        let start = Instant::now();
        assert!(join_if_finished(handle, Duration::from_millis(20)).is_none());
        assert!(start.elapsed() < Duration::from_secs(1));
        drop(tx);
    }

    #[test]
    fn local_launches_require_shared_routing_on_linux_and_macos() {
        for platform in ["linux", "macos"] {
            for args in [vec![], vec!["resume", "--last"], vec!["fork", "--last"]] {
                let args: Vec<_> = args.into_iter().map(OsString::from).collect();
                assert!(supported_on(platform, &args), "{platform}: {args:?}");
            }
            assert!(!supported_on(platform, &["--no-daemon".into()]));
            assert!(!supported_on(
                platform,
                &["--remote".into(), "ws://host".into()]
            ));
        }
        assert!(!supported_on("windows", &[]));
        assert!(!supported_on("unknown", &[]));
        assert_eq!(
            supported(&[]),
            matches!(std::env::consts::OS, "linux" | "macos")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn remote_capability_probe_requires_both_exact_options() {
        assert!(remote_capabilities(
            "Options:\n --remote <ADDR>\n --remote-auth-token-env <ENV>"
        ));
        for help in [
            "",
            "--remote",
            "--remote-auth-token-env",
            "--remote-future --remote-auth-token-env",
        ] {
            assert!(!remote_capabilities(help));
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_local_peer_without_the_bearer_cannot_complete_the_handshake() {
        let listener = TcpListener::bind((std::net::Ipv4Addr::LOCALHOST, 0)).unwrap();
        let address = listener.local_addr().unwrap();
        let server = std::thread::spawn(move || {
            let (stream, _) = listener.accept().unwrap();
            authenticated(stream, "private-test-capability").is_err()
        });
        let stream = TcpStream::connect(address).unwrap();
        let result = tungstenite::client(format!("ws://{address}/"), stream);
        assert!(
            matches!(result,Err(tungstenite::HandshakeError::Failure(tungstenite::Error::Http(ref response))) if response.status()==401)
        );
        assert!(server.join().unwrap());
    }
    #[test]
    fn only_verified_invocations_use_the_shared_bridge() {
        for args in [
            vec![],
            vec!["resume", "--last"],
            vec!["fork", "--last", "-m", "model"],
            vec!["-C", "/tmp/work"],
        ] {
            assert!(supported_args(
                &args.into_iter().map(OsString::from).collect::<Vec<_>>()
            ));
        }
        for args in [
            vec!["--no-daemon"],
            vec!["--remote", "unix://"],
            vec!["-c", "mcp_servers.x.command=foo"],
            vec!["-s", "read-only"],
            vec!["--profile", "other"],
        ] {
            assert!(!supported_args(
                &args.into_iter().map(OsString::from).collect::<Vec<_>>()
            ));
        }
    }
    #[test]
    fn only_the_existing_local_ivy_bridge_receives_a_route() {
        assert!(ivy_server(
            &json!({"command":"/opt/ivy/panel-mcp","env":{"existing":"kept"}})
        ));
        for server in [
            json!({"command":"/bin/other"}),
            json!({"command":"panel-mcp","enabled":false}),
            json!({"url":"https://example.org/mcp"}),
        ] {
            assert!(!ivy_server(&server));
        }
    }
}
```

### crates/panel-control/src/diagnostic_peer.rs

```
//! Socket identity used only to label publications for diagnostics.
//! This witness is deliberately independent of session authorization.

use crate::endpoint::Stream;
use std::io;

#[cfg(unix)]
use std::os::fd::{AsFd, AsRawFd};

/// Kernel supplied process identity captured from an accepted local socket.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct DiagnosticPeer {
    pid: u32,
    #[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
    pidfd: Option<std::sync::Arc<std::os::fd::OwnedFd>>,
}

impl DiagnosticPeer {
    pub fn pid(&self) -> u32 {
        self.pid
    }

    /// A momentary kernel liveness check. On Linux it uses the exact peer pidfd when available.
    pub fn is_alive(&self) -> bool {
        #[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
        if let Some(pidfd) = &self.pidfd {
            let mut p = libc::pollfd {
                fd: pidfd.as_raw_fd(),
                events: libc::POLLIN,
                revents: 0,
            };
            // SAFETY: p and the owned descriptor remain valid for this nonblocking poll.
            return unsafe { libc::poll(&mut p, 1, 0) == 0 && p.revents == 0 };
        }
        #[cfg(unix)]
        {
            // SAFETY: kill(pid, 0) performs no memory access and sends no signal.
            unsafe {
                libc::kill(self.pid as libc::pid_t, 0) == 0
                    || io::Error::last_os_error().kind() == io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied
            }
        }
        #[cfg(not(unix))]
        {
            false
        }
    }

    pub(crate) fn capture(stream: &Stream) -> io::Result<Self> {
        #[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
        {
            let Stream::UdSocket(stream) = stream;
            let fd = stream.as_fd().as_raw_fd();
            let credentials: libc::ucred = option(fd, libc::SOL_SOCKET, libc::SO_PEERCRED)?;
            if credentials.pid <= 0 {
                return Err(io::Error::other("invalid diagnostic peer pid"));
            }
            let descriptor: libc::c_int = option(fd, libc::SOL_SOCKET, libc::SO_PEERPIDFD)
                .ok()
                .unwrap_or(-1);
            let pidfd = if descriptor >= 0 {
                // SAFETY: successful SO_PEERPIDFD returns a fresh owned descriptor.
                Some(std::sync::Arc::new(unsafe {
                    std::os::fd::OwnedFd::from_raw_fd(descriptor)
                }))
            } else {
                None
            };
            let peer = Self {
                pid: credentials.pid as u32,
                pidfd,
            };
            if peer.is_alive() {
                Ok(peer)
            } else {
                Err(io::Error::other("diagnostic peer is not alive"))
            }
        }
        #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
        {
            let Stream::UdSocket(stream) = stream;
            let pid: libc::pid_t =
                option(stream.as_fd().as_raw_fd(), 0, 2 /* LOCAL_PEERPID */)?;
            if pid <= 0 {
                return Err(io::Error::other("invalid diagnostic peer pid"));
            }
            let peer = Self { pid: pid as u32 };
            if peer.is_alive() {
                Ok(peer)
            } else {
                Err(io::Error::other("diagnostic peer is not alive"))
            }
        }
        #[cfg(not(any(target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos")))]
        {
            let _ = stream;
            Err(io::Error::new(
                io::ErrorKind::Unsupported,
                "diagnostic peers unavailable",
            ))
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
use std::os::fd::FromRawFd;

#[cfg(unix)]
fn option<T: Copy>(fd: libc::c_int, level: libc::c_int, name: libc::c_int) -> io::Result<T> {
    let mut value = std::mem::MaybeUninit::<T>::uninit();
    let mut length = std::mem::size_of::<T>() as libc::socklen_t;
    // SAFETY: getsockopt initializes value on success, with length checked below.
    if unsafe { libc::getsockopt(fd, level, name, value.as_mut_ptr().cast(), &mut length) } < 0 {
        return Err(io::Error::last_os_error());
    }
    if length as usize != std::mem::size_of::<T>() {
        return Err(io::Error::other("unexpected peer credential size"));
    }
    // SAFETY: successful getsockopt initialized the exact-sized value.
    Ok(unsafe { value.assume_init() })
}
```

### crates/panel-control/src/endpoint.rs

```
//! Where a pane's channel lives, and who is allowed to reach it.
//!
//! `PanelDir` creates a `0o700` directory. Inherited clients also verify its owner and
//! permissions before connecting: a stale identity may name a replacement planted after the
//! console closed. This pathname check does not authenticate the peer across a replacement race.

use std::io;
use std::path::Path;

#[cfg(unix)]
use std::os::fd::AsRawFd;
#[cfg(unix)]
use std::os::unix::net::UnixStream;

#[cfg(not(windows))]
use interprocess::local_socket::GenericFilePath;
#[cfg(windows)]
use interprocess::local_socket::GenericNamespaced;
#[cfg(unix)]
pub(crate) use interprocess::local_socket::{Listener, Stream, prelude::*};
#[cfg(unix)]
use interprocess::local_socket::{ListenerOptions, Name};
#[cfg(windows)]
use interprocess::local_socket::{Name, ToNsName};
#[cfg(windows)]
mod windows;
#[cfg(windows)]
pub use windows::{Listener, Stream};

/// The channel-owned end of a private wake pair.
///
/// Dropping it makes the acceptor's end readable with a hang-up. It needs neither the endpoint's
/// pathname nor room in its connection backlog, and closing a descriptor cannot wait.
pub(crate) struct AcceptorStopper {
    #[cfg(unix)]
    _signal: UnixStream,
    #[cfg(windows)]
    _signal: windows::StopSignal,
}

/// The listener and private wake descriptor owned by one acceptor thread.
pub(crate) struct Acceptor {
    #[cfg(unix)]
    listener: Listener,
    #[cfg(unix)]
    signal: UnixStream,
    #[cfg(windows)]
    inner: windows::Acceptor,
}

/// What woke the acceptor.
pub(crate) enum AcceptorReady {
    Connection,
    Stop,
}

/// Gives one bound listener the private descriptor pair that stops it.
pub(crate) fn acceptor(listener: Listener) -> io::Result<(AcceptorStopper, Acceptor)> {
    #[cfg(windows)]
    {
        let (signal, inner) = windows::acceptor(listener)?;
        Ok((AcceptorStopper { _signal: signal }, Acceptor { inner }))
    }
    #[cfg(unix)]
    {
        let (stopper, wake) = UnixStream::pair()?;
        Ok((
            AcceptorStopper { _signal: stopper },
            Acceptor {
                listener,
                signal: wake,
            },
        ))
    }
}

impl Acceptor {
    /// Sleeps until the endpoint has a connection or its owner disappears.
    pub(crate) fn wait(&self) -> io::Result<AcceptorReady> {
        #[cfg(windows)]
        {
            self.inner.wait()
        }
        #[cfg(unix)]
        {
            let Listener::UdSocket(listener) = &self.listener;
            let mut descriptors = [
                libc::pollfd {
                    fd: listener.inner().as_raw_fd(),
                    events: libc::POLLIN,
                    revents: 0,
                },
                libc::pollfd {
                    fd: self.signal.as_raw_fd(),
                    events: libc::POLLIN,
                    revents: 0,
                },
            ];
            // SAFETY: both descriptors remain owned for the call, the array has the declared
            // length, and `poll` only writes its `revents` fields.
            let ready = unsafe { libc::poll(descriptors.as_mut_ptr(), descriptors.len() as _, -1) };
            if ready < 0 {
                return Err(io::Error::last_os_error());
            }
            if descriptors[1].revents != 0 {
                Ok(AcceptorReady::Stop)
            } else {
                Ok(AcceptorReady::Connection)
            }
        }
    }

    /// Accepts the connection whose readiness [`Self::wait`] reported.
    pub(crate) fn accept(&self) -> io::Result<Stream> {
        #[cfg(windows)]
        {
            return self.inner.accept();
        }
        #[cfg(unix)]
        self.listener.accept()
    }

    #[cfg(windows)]
    pub(crate) fn wait_for_stop(&self, timeout: std::time::Duration) -> io::Result<bool> {
        self.inner.wait_for_stop(timeout)
    }
}

/// The name of a pane's endpoint.
///
/// **A name and not a path**, because on Windows there is no path: a pipe lives in the machine's
/// namespace. The two platforms differ here and only here, which is what keeps everything above
/// this function written once.
///
/// **Which of the two arguments does the separating is the platform's business too.** On POSIX the
/// socket is a file inside the pane's own directory, so the directory is what keeps two panes apart
/// and the identifier is not read at all; on Windows the name carries no directory, so the
/// identifier is the whole of it. That is why the producer refuses to start with either variable
/// missing instead of defaulting one — see [`crate::Producer::of_this_pane`].
pub fn name_of(dir: &Path, id: &str) -> io::Result<Name<'static>> {
    #[cfg(not(windows))]
    {
        let _ = id;
        dir.join(panel_contract::CONTROL_SOCKET)
            .to_fs_name::<GenericFilePath>()
    }
    #[cfg(windows)]
    {
        let _ = dir;
        format!("ivy-{id}").to_ns_name::<GenericNamespaced>()
    }
}

/// Whether [`bind`] is supported on this platform. Producers use this to decide whether they can
/// offer the control channel.
pub const BINDS: bool = cfg!(any(unix, windows));

/// Whether the Unix control socket currently has socket metadata at the contract path.
///
/// This is a presence hint for MCP instructions, not a liveness check: connecting here would
/// consume a listener resource and turn initialization into an endpoint probe.
#[cfg(unix)]
pub fn socket_present(dir: &Path) -> bool {
    use std::os::unix::fs::FileTypeExt;

    std::fs::symlink_metadata(dir.join(panel_contract::CONTROL_SOCKET))
        .is_ok_and(|metadata| metadata.file_type().is_socket())
}

/// Windows has no filesystem socket. This hint reports whether inherited identity and server PID
/// are complete; it does not probe the named pipe or claim that the server is reachable.
#[cfg(windows)]
pub fn socket_present(_dir: &Path) -> bool {
    std::env::var("PANEL_ID").is_ok_and(|id| !id.is_empty())
        && std::env::var("PANEL_SERVER_PID")
            .ok()
            .and_then(|pid| pid.parse::<u32>().ok())
            .is_some_and(|pid| pid != 0)
        && private_to_this_user(_dir).is_ok()
}

/// Binds a pane's endpoint, ready to accept. Windows creates a byte-mode overlapped pipe with a
/// protected user-and-SYSTEM DACL and rejects remote clients. Unix uses the pane directory's socket.
///
/// The acceptor waits on the listener and a private stop signal, so it does no periodic polling at
/// rest and does not need to open a synthetic client connection during shutdown. The Unix acceptor
/// uses `poll`; the Windows acceptor waits on overlapped connect and stop events. Both transports
/// provide bounded read and write operations to the listener (see [`crate::listener`]).
pub fn bind(dir: &Path, id: &str) -> io::Result<Listener> {
    // `BINDS` says the same thing to callers that must not promise a channel; the test
    // `binding_is_what_binds_says` ties the two together.
    #[cfg(windows)]
    {
        return windows::bind(dir, id);
    }
    #[cfg(not(windows))]
    {
        ListenerOptions::new().name(name_of(dir, id)?).create_sync()
    }
}

/// Refuses an inherited directory that another user could control.
pub fn private_to_this_user(dir: &Path) -> io::Result<()> {
    #[cfg(unix)]
    {
        // SAFETY: geteuid has no arguments or memory preconditions.
        private_to_uid(dir, unsafe { libc::geteuid() })
    }
    #[cfg(windows)]
    {
        windows::private_to_this_user(dir)
    }
    #[cfg(not(any(unix, windows)))]
    {
        let _ = dir;
        Ok(())
    }
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn private_to_uid(dir: &Path, uid: u32) -> io::Result<()> {
    use std::os::unix::{ffi::OsStrExt, fs::MetadataExt};
    // lstat("alias/.") follows alias. Strip only terminal slash/dot components;
    // normalising interior components (especially ..) would change symlink traversal.
    let mut bytes = dir.as_os_str().as_bytes();
    loop {
        if (bytes.len() > 1 && bytes.ends_with(b"/")) || bytes.ends_with(b"/.") {
            bytes = &bytes[..bytes.len() - 1];
        } else {
            break;
        }
    }
    let lookup = Path::new(std::ffi::OsStr::from_bytes(bytes));
    let metadata = std::fs::symlink_metadata(lookup).map_err(|failure| {
        let detail = if failure.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound {
            "directory is gone".to_owned()
        } else {
            failure.to_string()
        };
        io::Error::new(
            failure.kind(),
            format!(
                "this console's panel could not be reached at {} ({detail})",
                dir.display()
            ),
        )
    })?;
    if !metadata.is_dir() || metadata.uid() != uid || metadata.mode() & 0o077 != 0 {
        return Err(io::Error::new(
            io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied,
            format!("{} is not a private directory of this user", dir.display()),
        ));
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// Opens a connection to a pane's endpoint, from the producer's side.
/// Windows verifies the server against the separately inherited `PANEL_SERVER_PID` before returning.
pub fn connect(dir: &Path, id: &str) -> io::Result<Stream> {
    #[cfg(windows)]
    {
        return connect_authenticated(dir, id, crate::client::inherited_server_pid()?);
    }
    #[cfg(unix)]
    Stream::connect(name_of(dir, id)?)
}

/// Connects only after the pipe server process matches the inherited app PID.
#[cfg(windows)]
pub fn connect_authenticated(dir: &Path, id: &str, expected_pid: u32) -> io::Result<Stream> {
    let stream = windows::connect(dir, id)?;
    stream.verify_server_pid(expected_pid)?;
    Ok(stream)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use panel_contract::PanelDir;

    #[cfg(unix)]
    #[test]
    fn inherited_directory_requires_private_mode_type_and_effective_owner() {
        use std::os::unix::fs::{MetadataExt, PermissionsExt, symlink};
        let panel = PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        let dir = panel.path();
        private_to_this_user(dir).unwrap();
        let uid = std::fs::symlink_metadata(dir).unwrap().uid();
        assert!(private_to_uid(dir, uid ^ 1).is_err());
        for bit in [0o040, 0o020, 0o010, 0o004, 0o002, 0o001] {
            std::fs::set_permissions(dir, std::fs::Permissions::from_mode(0o700 | bit)).unwrap();
            let error = private_to_this_user(dir).unwrap_err().to_string();
            assert!(error.contains(&dir.display().to_string()), "{error}");
            assert!(
                error.contains("not a private directory of this user"),
                "{error}"
            );
        }
        std::fs::set_permissions(dir, std::fs::Permissions::from_mode(0o700)).unwrap();
        let alias = dir.join("alias");
        symlink(dir, &alias).unwrap();
        for suffix in ["", "/", "/.", "/././"] {
            let path = std::path::PathBuf::from(format!("{}{suffix}", alias.display()));
            let error = private_to_this_user(&path).unwrap_err().to_string();
            assert!(error.contains(&path.display().to_string()), "{error}");
            assert!(
                error.contains("not a private directory of this user"),
                "{error}"
            );
            private_to_this_user(Path::new(&format!("{}{suffix}", dir.display()))).unwrap();
        }
        let file = dir.join("file");
        std::fs::write(&file, "").unwrap();
        assert!(private_to_this_user(&file).is_err());
        let missing = dir.join("gone");
        let error = private_to_this_user(&missing).unwrap_err().to_string();
        assert!(error.contains("directory is gone"), "{error}");
        assert!(error.contains(&missing.display().to_string()), "{error}");
        assert!(!error.contains("not a private directory"), "{error}");
        let error = private_to_this_user(&file.join("child"))
            .unwrap_err()
            .to_string();
        assert!(error.contains("file/child"), "{error}");
        assert!(!error.contains("not a private directory"), "{error}");
    }

    /// **The endpoint is inside the pane's directory, which is where its permission comes from.**
    /// Asserting the name alone would pass with a socket in the shared temporary — the one place it
    /// must never be, because the whole access control is the directory's mode.
    #[cfg(not(windows))]
    #[test]
    fn the_endpoint_lives_inside_the_panes_own_directory() {
        let panel = PanelDir::create().expect("create the panel directory");

        let _listener = bind(panel.path(), panel.id()).expect("bind the endpoint");

        let socket = panel.path().join(panel_contract::CONTROL_SOCKET);
        assert!(socket.exists(), "nothing was bound at {}", socket.display());
    }

    /// The mode is the directory's and is inherited rather than set: this is what fails if somebody
    /// ever moves the endpoint out of the directory, which is the change that would quietly widen it.
    ///
    /// **The mode read is the bound socket's own parent**, and not the directory `PanelDir::create`
    /// makes: reading the latter left `bind` out of the test entirely, so moving the socket to `/tmp`
    /// while keeping the directory `0o700` stayed green against its own comment. The subject is where
    /// the socket actually landed.
    #[cfg(unix)]
    #[test]
    fn nothing_outside_this_user_can_reach_the_endpoint() {
        use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;

        let panel = PanelDir::create().expect("create the panel directory");
        let _listener = bind(panel.path(), panel.id()).expect("bind the endpoint");
        let socket = panel.path().join(panel_contract::CONTROL_SOCKET);

        let containing = socket
            .parent()
            .expect("the bound socket has a parent")
            .metadata()
            .expect("read the containing directory's permissions")
            .permissions()
            .mode()
            & 0o777;

        assert_eq!(containing, 0o700, "the endpoint's door is {containing:o}");
    }

    #[cfg(not(windows))]
    #[test]
    fn what_was_bound_is_what_gets_connected_to() {
        let panel = PanelDir::create().expect("create the panel directory");
        let listener = bind(panel.path(), panel.id()).expect("bind the endpoint");

        // **The client connects first and the accept is a single blocking call.** The earlier loop
        // retried on `WouldBlock` without any deadline, so a regression that stopped accepting hung
        // the test instead of failing it.
        let client = connect(panel.path(), panel.id()).expect("connect to the endpoint");
        let accepted = listener.accept().expect("accept the connection");

        drop(client);
        drop(accepted);
    }

    /// **`BINDS` is what `bind` does, on whatever platform this runs.** The constant is the one a
    /// producer reads to decide whether to promise a channel; a `bind` that started working on
    /// Windows with the constant left false would keep agents there without instructions.
    #[test]
    fn binding_is_what_binds_says() {
        let panel = PanelDir::create().expect("create the panel directory");

        let bound = bind(panel.path(), panel.id());
        let refusal = bound.as_ref().err().map(ToString::to_string);
        assert_eq!(
            bound.is_ok(),
            BINDS,
            "BINDS disagrees with binding: {refusal:?}"
        );
        if let Err(refusal) = bound {
            assert_eq!(refusal.kind(), io::ErrorKind::Unsupported, "{refusal}");
        }
    }

    /// Two panes are two channels, asserted by **connecting** rather than by comparing names: a
    /// name that differed while both resolved to one endpoint would pass a comparison and break the
    /// scoping the whole design rests on — «the pane is the endpoint you opened».
    #[cfg(not(windows))]
    #[test]
    fn a_pane_cannot_be_reached_through_another_panes_endpoint() {
        let first = PanelDir::create().expect("create the first");
        let second = PanelDir::create().expect("create the second");
        let _bound = bind(first.path(), first.id()).expect("bind the first");

        let wrong_door = connect(second.path(), second.id());

        assert!(
            wrong_door.is_err(),
            "the second pane's name reached the first pane's endpoint"
        );
    }
}
```

### crates/panel-control/src/endpoint/windows.rs

```
//! Native, overlapped named-pipe transport.
//!
//! Every operation keeps its `OVERLAPPED` and event alive through cancellation completion. The
//! only waits here are kernel waits; the caller's receive/send deadlines bound them.

use std::ffi::OsStr;
use std::io::{self, Read, Write};
use std::os::windows::ffi::OsStrExt;
use std::path::Path;
use std::ptr::{null, null_mut};
use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, AtomicUsize, Ordering};
use std::sync::{Arc, Mutex};
use std::time::Duration;

use windows_sys::Win32::Foundation::*;
use windows_sys::Win32::Security::Authorization::*;
use windows_sys::Win32::Security::*;
use windows_sys::Win32::Storage::FileSystem::*;
use windows_sys::Win32::System::IO::*;
use windows_sys::Win32::System::Pipes::*;
use windows_sys::Win32::System::Threading::*;

const PIPE_PATH_PREFIX: &str = r"\\.\pipe\";
const PIPE_MODE: NAMED_PIPE_MODE =
    PIPE_TYPE_BYTE | PIPE_READMODE_BYTE | PIPE_WAIT | PIPE_REJECT_REMOTE_CLIENTS;
const PIPE_OPEN: FILE_FLAGS_AND_ATTRIBUTES = FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED | FILE_FLAG_FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE;
const PIPE_OPEN_NEXT: FILE_FLAGS_AND_ATTRIBUTES = FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED;

fn winerr() -> io::Error {
    io::Error::last_os_error()
}
fn wide(s: &OsStr) -> Vec<u16> {
    s.encode_wide().chain(Some(0)).collect()
}

struct Handle(HANDLE);
unsafe impl Send for Handle {}
unsafe impl Sync for Handle {}
impl Handle {
    fn new(handle: HANDLE) -> io::Result<Self> {
        if handle == INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE || handle.is_null() {
            Err(winerr())
        } else {
            Ok(Self(handle))
        }
    }
}
impl Drop for Handle {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        unsafe {
            CloseHandle(self.0);
        }
    }
}

struct Security(*mut std::ffi::c_void);
impl Security {
    fn pipe() -> io::Result<Self> {
        descriptor(false)
    }
}
impl Drop for Security {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        unsafe {
            LocalFree(self.0);
        }
    }
}

fn descriptor(children: bool) -> io::Result<Security> {
    let sid = current_sid_string()?;
    let inherit = if children { "OICI" } else { "" };
    let system_ace = if sid == "S-1-5-18" {
        String::new()
    } else {
        format!("(A;{inherit};FA;;;SY)")
    };
    let sddl: Vec<u16> = format!("O:{sid}D:P(A;{inherit};FA;;;{sid}){system_ace}")
        .encode_utf16()
        .chain(Some(0))
        .collect();
    let mut raw = null_mut();
    let mut size = 0;
    // SAFETY: the SDDL is terminated and output pointers are valid.
    if unsafe {
        ConvertStringSecurityDescriptorToSecurityDescriptorW(
            sddl.as_ptr(),
            SDDL_REVISION_1,
            &mut raw,
            &mut size,
        )
    } == 0
    {
        return Err(winerr());
    }
    Ok(Security(raw))
}

fn current_sid_string() -> io::Result<String> {
    let mut token = null_mut();
    // SAFETY: process pseudo-handle and output pointer are valid.
    if unsafe { OpenProcessToken(GetCurrentProcess(), TOKEN_QUERY, &mut token) } == 0 {
        return Err(winerr());
    }
    let token = Handle(token);
    let mut needed = 0;
    // SAFETY: probing the required buffer size is the documented call pattern.
    unsafe {
        GetTokenInformation(token.0, TokenUser, null_mut(), 0, &mut needed);
    }
    let mut buffer = vec![0usize; (needed as usize).div_ceil(std::mem::size_of::<usize>())];
    // SAFETY: buffer has the reported size and is suitably aligned for TOKEN_USER.
    if unsafe {
        GetTokenInformation(
            token.0,
            TokenUser,
            buffer.as_mut_ptr().cast(),
            needed,
            &mut needed,
        )
    } == 0
    {
        return Err(winerr());
    }
    let user = unsafe { &*(buffer.as_ptr().cast::<TOKEN_USER>()) };
    let mut sid_string = null_mut();
    // SAFETY: token information owns a valid SID for the duration of this call.
    if unsafe { ConvertSidToStringSidW(user.User.Sid, &mut sid_string) } == 0 {
        return Err(winerr());
    }
    let mut len = 0;
    unsafe {
        while *sid_string.add(len) != 0 {
            len += 1;
        }
    }
    let result = String::from_utf16_lossy(unsafe { std::slice::from_raw_parts(sid_string, len) });
    unsafe {
        LocalFree(sid_string.cast());
    }
    Ok(result)
}

struct ListenerInner {
    name: Vec<u16>,
    security: Security,
    handle: Option<Handle>,
    first: bool,
}
pub struct Listener {
    inner: Arc<Mutex<ListenerInner>>,
}
unsafe impl Send for Listener {}
unsafe impl Sync for Listener {}

fn new_pipe(name: &[u16], security: &Security, first: bool) -> io::Result<Handle> {
    let attributes = SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES {
        nLength: std::mem::size_of::<SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES>() as u32,
        lpSecurityDescriptor: security.0,
        bInheritHandle: 0,
    };
    // SAFETY: all arguments point to live data, descriptor is explicit, and the resulting handle is overlapped.
    Handle::new(unsafe {
        CreateNamedPipeW(
            name.as_ptr(),
            PIPE_ACCESS_DUPLEX | if first { PIPE_OPEN } else { PIPE_OPEN_NEXT },
            PIPE_MODE,
            PIPE_UNLIMITED_INSTANCES,
            64 * 1024,
            64 * 1024,
            0,
            &attributes,
        )
    })
}

pub fn bind(_dir: &Path, id: &str) -> io::Result<Listener> {
    if id.is_empty() || !id.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_hexdigit()) {
        return Err(io::Error::new(
            io::ErrorKind::InvalidInput,
            "invalid pane identifier",
        ));
    }
    let security = Security::pipe()?;
    let name: Vec<u16> = format!("{PIPE_PATH_PREFIX}ivy-{id}")
        .encode_utf16()
        .chain(Some(0))
        .collect();
    let handle = new_pipe(&name, &security, true)?;
    Ok(Listener {
        inner: Arc::new(Mutex::new(ListenerInner {
            name,
            security,
            handle: Some(handle),
            first: false,
        })),
    })
}

pub struct StopSignal {
    handle: Arc<Handle>,
    armed: bool,
}
pub struct Acceptor {
    listener: Arc<Mutex<ListenerInner>>,
    stop: Arc<Handle>,
    pending: Mutex<Option<(HANDLE, Box<OVERLAPPED>, Handle)>>,
    pending_ready: AtomicBool,
    connected: Mutex<bool>,
}
unsafe impl Send for Acceptor {}
unsafe impl Sync for Acceptor {}

pub fn acceptor(listener: Listener) -> io::Result<(StopSignal, Acceptor)> {
    let stop = Arc::new(event()?);
    let acceptor = Acceptor {
        listener: Arc::clone(&listener.inner),
        stop: Arc::clone(&stop),
        pending: Mutex::new(None),
        pending_ready: AtomicBool::new(false),
        connected: Mutex::new(false),
    };
    Ok((
        StopSignal {
            handle: stop,
            armed: true,
        },
        acceptor,
    ))
}

fn event() -> io::Result<Handle> {
    // SAFETY: manual reset event, initially nonsignaled, and non-inheritable.
    Handle::new(unsafe { CreateEventW(null(), 1, 0, null()) })
}

impl Acceptor {
    pub fn wait_for_stop(&self, timeout: Duration) -> io::Result<bool> {
        let millis = timeout.as_millis().min((u32::MAX - 1) as u128) as u32;
        match unsafe { WaitForSingleObject(self.stop.0, millis) } {
            WAIT_OBJECT_0 => Ok(true),
            WAIT_TIMEOUT => Ok(false),
            _ => Err(winerr()),
        }
    }

    pub fn wait(&self) -> io::Result<super::AcceptorReady> {
        if *self.connected.lock().unwrap() {
            return Ok(super::AcceptorReady::Connection);
        }
        let mut pending = self.pending.lock().unwrap();
        if pending.is_none() {
            let listener = self.listener.lock().unwrap();
            let pipe = listener
                .handle
                .as_ref()
                .ok_or_else(|| io::Error::new(io::ErrorKind::BrokenPipe, "listener stopped"))?;
            let ev = event()?;
            let mut ov: Box<OVERLAPPED> = Box::new(unsafe { std::mem::zeroed() });
            ov.hEvent = ev.0;
            // SAFETY: OVERLAPPED and event stay in `pending` until completion is drained.
            let ok = unsafe { ConnectNamedPipe(pipe.0, &mut *ov) };
            if ok == 0 {
                let code = unsafe { GetLastError() };
                if code == ERROR_PIPE_CONNECTED || code == ERROR_NO_DATA {
                    *self.connected.lock().unwrap() = true;
                    return Ok(super::AcceptorReady::Connection);
                }
                if code != ERROR_IO_PENDING {
                    return Err(io::Error::from_raw_os_error(code as i32));
                }
            }
            *pending = Some((pipe.0, ov, ev));
            self.pending_ready.store(true, Ordering::Release);
        }
        let (_, _ov, ev) = pending.as_ref().unwrap();
        // Stop is first so a simultaneous connect completion is treated as cancellation.
        let handles = [self.stop.0, ev.0];
        // SAFETY: both handles and the overlapped operation remain owned throughout the wait.
        let result = unsafe { WaitForMultipleObjects(2, handles.as_ptr(), 0, INFINITE) };
        if result == WAIT_OBJECT_0 {
            let (pipe, ov, _) = pending.take().unwrap();
            self.pending_ready.store(false, Ordering::Release);
            unsafe {
                CancelIoEx(pipe, &*ov);
            }
            let mut transferred = 0;
            unsafe {
                GetOverlappedResult(pipe, &*ov, &mut transferred, 1);
            }
            return Ok(super::AcceptorReady::Stop);
        }
        if result != WAIT_OBJECT_0 + 1 {
            return Err(winerr());
        }
        let (pipe, ov, _) = pending.take().unwrap();
        self.pending_ready.store(false, Ordering::Release);
        let mut transferred = 0;
        if unsafe { GetOverlappedResult(pipe, &*ov, &mut transferred, 0) } == 0 {
            if unsafe { GetLastError() } == ERROR_NO_DATA {
                *self.connected.lock().unwrap() = true;
                return Ok(super::AcceptorReady::Connection);
            }
            return Err(winerr());
        }
        *self.connected.lock().unwrap() = true;
        Ok(super::AcceptorReady::Connection)
    }

    pub fn accept(&self) -> io::Result<Stream> {
        if !std::mem::replace(&mut *self.connected.lock().unwrap(), false) {
            return Err(io::Error::new(
                io::ErrorKind::WouldBlock,
                "no pipe connection is ready",
            ));
        }
        let mut listener = self.listener.lock().unwrap();
        let pipe = listener
            .handle
            .take()
            .ok_or_else(|| io::Error::new(io::ErrorKind::BrokenPipe, "listener stopped"))?;
        // Keep the pipe name owned while preparing its successor instance.
        listener.handle = Some(new_pipe(
            &listener.name,
            &listener.security,
            listener.first,
        )?);
        listener.first = false;
        Ok(Stream {
            handle: pipe,
            stop: Arc::clone(&self.stop),
            tracker: Arc::new(IoTracker::default()),
            read_timeout: Mutex::new(None),
            write_timeout: Mutex::new(None),
        })
    }
}
impl Drop for Acceptor {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        if let Ok(pending) = self.pending.get_mut() {
            if let Some((pipe, overlapped, _event)) = pending.take() {
                self.pending_ready.store(false, Ordering::Release);
                let mut transferred = 0;
                unsafe {
                    CancelIoEx(pipe, &*overlapped);
                    GetOverlappedResult(pipe, &*overlapped, &mut transferred, 1);
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

impl Drop for StopSignal {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        if self.armed {
            unsafe {
                SetEvent(self.handle.0);
            }
        }
    }
}

impl Listener {
    pub fn accept(&self) -> io::Result<Stream> {
        let (mut stop, acceptor) = acceptor(self.clone())?;
        match acceptor.wait()? {
            super::AcceptorReady::Connection => {
                let stream = acceptor.accept()?;
                stop.armed = false;
                Ok(stream)
            }
            super::AcceptorReady::Stop => Err(io::Error::new(
                io::ErrorKind::Interrupted,
                "listener stopped",
            )),
        }
    }
}
impl Clone for Listener {
    fn clone(&self) -> Self {
        Self {
            inner: Arc::clone(&self.inner),
        }
    }
}

pub struct Stream {
    handle: Handle,
    stop: Arc<Handle>,
    tracker: Arc<IoTracker>,
    read_timeout: Mutex<Option<Duration>>,
    write_timeout: Mutex<Option<Duration>>,
}
unsafe impl Send for Stream {}
unsafe impl Sync for Stream {}
#[derive(Default)]
struct IoTracker {
    in_flight: AtomicUsize,
    pending: AtomicUsize,
}
struct IoGuard(Arc<IoTracker>, bool);
impl IoGuard {
    fn begin(tracker: &Arc<IoTracker>) -> Self {
        tracker.in_flight.fetch_add(1, Ordering::AcqRel);
        Self(Arc::clone(tracker), false)
    }
    fn mark_pending(&mut self) {
        self.0.pending.fetch_add(1, Ordering::AcqRel);
        self.1 = true;
    }
}
impl Drop for IoGuard {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        if self.1 {
            self.0.pending.fetch_sub(1, Ordering::AcqRel);
        }
        self.0.in_flight.fetch_sub(1, Ordering::AcqRel);
    }
}
impl Stream {
    pub fn verify_server_pid(&self, expected: u32) -> io::Result<()> {
        if expected == 0 {
            return Err(io::Error::new(
                io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied,
                "expected server PID is missing",
            ));
        }
        let mut actual = 0;
        if unsafe { GetNamedPipeServerProcessId(self.handle.0, &mut actual) } == 0 {
            return Err(winerr());
        }
        if actual != expected {
            return Err(io::Error::new(
                io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied,
                format!("control pipe server PID {actual} did not match expected PID {expected}"),
            ));
        }
        Ok(())
    }
    pub fn try_clone(&self) -> io::Result<Self> {
        let mut duplicate = null_mut();
        // SAFETY: duplicate refers to an independently closed duplicate of the current-process handle.
        if unsafe {
            DuplicateHandle(
                GetCurrentProcess(),
                self.handle.0,
                GetCurrentProcess(),
                &mut duplicate,
                0,
                0,
                DUPLICATE_SAME_ACCESS,
            )
        } == 0
        {
            return Err(winerr());
        }
        Ok(Self {
            handle: Handle(duplicate),
            stop: Arc::clone(&self.stop),
            tracker: Arc::clone(&self.tracker),
            read_timeout: Mutex::new(*self.read_timeout.lock().unwrap()),
            write_timeout: Mutex::new(*self.write_timeout.lock().unwrap()),
        })
    }
    pub fn set_recv_timeout(&self, timeout: Option<Duration>) -> io::Result<()> {
        *self.read_timeout.lock().unwrap() = timeout;
        Ok(())
    }
    pub fn set_send_timeout(&self, timeout: Option<Duration>) -> io::Result<()> {
        *self.write_timeout.lock().unwrap() = timeout;
        Ok(())
    }
    pub fn raw_handle(&self) -> HANDLE {
        self.handle.0
    }
}

fn transfer(
    handle: HANDLE,
    stop: HANDLE,
    tracker: &Arc<IoTracker>,
    buffer: *mut u8,
    len: u32,
    timeout: Option<Duration>,
    write: bool,
) -> io::Result<usize> {
    let mut operation = IoGuard::begin(tracker);
    let ev = event()?;
    let mut ov: OVERLAPPED = unsafe { std::mem::zeroed() };
    ov.hEvent = ev.0;
    let mut transferred = 0;
    let ok = unsafe {
        if write {
            WriteFile(handle, buffer, len, &mut transferred, &mut ov)
        } else {
            ReadFile(handle, buffer, len, &mut transferred, &mut ov)
        }
    };
    if ok != 0 {
        return Ok(transferred as usize);
    }
    let code = unsafe { GetLastError() };
    if code != ERROR_IO_PENDING {
        if !write && code == ERROR_BROKEN_PIPE {
            return Ok(0);
        }
        return Err(io::Error::from_raw_os_error(code as i32));
    }
    operation.mark_pending();
    let millis = timeout
        .map(|d| d.as_millis().min((u32::MAX - 1) as u128) as u32)
        .unwrap_or(INFINITE);
    let handles = [stop, ev.0];
    let wait = unsafe { WaitForMultipleObjects(2, handles.as_ptr(), 0, millis) };
    if wait != WAIT_OBJECT_0 + 1 {
        unsafe {
            CancelIoEx(handle, &ov);
            GetOverlappedResult(handle, &ov, &mut transferred, 1);
        }
        return Err(if wait == WAIT_OBJECT_0 {
            io::Error::new(io::ErrorKind::Interrupted, "pane channel stopped")
        } else if wait == WAIT_TIMEOUT {
            io::Error::new(io::ErrorKind::TimedOut, "pipe operation timed out")
        } else {
            winerr()
        });
    }
    if unsafe { GetOverlappedResult(handle, &ov, &mut transferred, 0) } == 0 {
        let code = unsafe { GetLastError() };
        if !write && code == ERROR_BROKEN_PIPE {
            return Ok(0);
        }
        return Err(io::Error::from_raw_os_error(code as i32));
    }
    Ok(transferred as usize)
}
impl Read for Stream {
    fn read(&mut self, buf: &mut [u8]) -> io::Result<usize> {
        (&*self).read(buf)
    }
}
impl Write for Stream {
    fn write(&mut self, buf: &[u8]) -> io::Result<usize> {
        (&*self).write(buf)
    }
    fn flush(&mut self) -> io::Result<()> {
        Ok(())
    }
}
impl Read for &Stream {
    fn read(&mut self, buf: &mut [u8]) -> io::Result<usize> {
        if buf.is_empty() {
            return Ok(0);
        }
        transfer(
            self.handle.0,
            self.stop.0,
            &self.tracker,
            buf.as_mut_ptr(),
            buf.len().min(u32::MAX as usize) as u32,
            *self.read_timeout.lock().unwrap(),
            false,
        )
    }
}
impl Write for &Stream {
    fn write(&mut self, buf: &[u8]) -> io::Result<usize> {
        if buf.is_empty() {
            return Ok(0);
        }
        transfer(
            self.handle.0,
            self.stop.0,
            &self.tracker,
            buf.as_ptr() as *mut u8,
            buf.len().min(u32::MAX as usize) as u32,
            *self.write_timeout.lock().unwrap(),
            true,
        )
    }
    fn flush(&mut self) -> io::Result<()> {
        Ok(())
    }
}

pub fn connect(_dir: &Path, id: &str) -> io::Result<Stream> {
    if id.is_empty() || !id.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_hexdigit()) {
        return Err(io::Error::new(
            io::ErrorKind::InvalidInput,
            "invalid pane identifier",
        ));
    }
    let name = wide(OsStr::new(&format!("{PIPE_PATH_PREFIX}ivy-{id}")));
    let handle = unsafe {
        CreateFileW(
            name.as_ptr(),
            GENERIC_READ | GENERIC_WRITE,
            0,
            null(),
            OPEN_EXISTING,
            FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED,
            null_mut(),
        )
    };
    Ok(Stream {
        handle: Handle::new(handle)?,
        stop: Arc::new(event()?),
        tracker: Arc::new(IoTracker::default()),
        read_timeout: Mutex::new(None),
        write_timeout: Mutex::new(None),
    })
}

pub fn private_to_this_user(_dir: &Path) -> io::Result<()> {
    panel_contract::windows_directory::validate(_dir).map_err(|failure| {
        if failure.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound {
            io::Error::new(
                failure.kind(),
                format!(
                    "this console's panel could not be reached at {} (directory is gone)",
                    _dir.display()
                ),
            )
        } else if failure.kind() == io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied {
            io::Error::new(
                failure.kind(),
                format!("{} is not a private directory of this user", _dir.display()),
            )
        } else {
            io::Error::new(
                failure.kind(),
                format!(
                    "this console's panel could not be reached at {} ({failure})",
                    _dir.display()
                ),
            )
        }
    })
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use std::io::{Read, Write};
    use std::thread;
    use std::time::Instant;

    #[test]
    fn first_instance_refuses_a_duplicate_and_pipe_dacl_is_exact() {
        let panel = panel_contract::PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        let listener = bind(panel.path(), panel.id()).unwrap();
        assert!(
            bind(panel.path(), panel.id()).is_err(),
            "a second first instance captured the pipe name"
        );
        assert_pipe_acl(&listener);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_client_that_connects_before_the_accept_wait_is_served() {
        let panel = panel_contract::PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        let listener = bind(panel.path(), panel.id()).unwrap();
        let mut client = connect(panel.path(), panel.id()).unwrap();
        let mut server = listener.accept().unwrap();
        client.write_all(b"x").unwrap();
        let mut byte = [0];
        assert_eq!(server.read(&mut byte).unwrap(), 1);
        assert_eq!(byte[0], b'x');
    }

    #[test]
    fn pane_ids_bind_distinct_pipe_names() {
        let first = panel_contract::PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        let second = panel_contract::PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        let first_listener = bind(first.path(), first.id()).unwrap();
        let second_listener = bind(second.path(), second.id()).unwrap();
        let mut first_client = connect(first.path(), first.id()).unwrap();
        let mut second_client = connect(second.path(), second.id()).unwrap();
        let mut first_server = first_listener.accept().unwrap();
        let mut second_server = second_listener.accept().unwrap();
        first_client.write_all(b"a").unwrap();
        second_client.write_all(b"b").unwrap();
        let mut byte = [0];
        assert_eq!(first_server.read(&mut byte).unwrap(), 1);
        assert_eq!(byte[0], b'a');
        assert_eq!(second_server.read(&mut byte).unwrap(), 1);
        assert_eq!(byte[0], b'b');
    }

    #[test]
    fn dropping_a_channel_cancels_and_drains_a_pending_accept() {
        let panel = panel_contract::PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        let listener = bind(panel.path(), panel.id()).unwrap();
        let (stop, acceptor) = acceptor(listener).unwrap();
        let acceptor = Arc::new(acceptor);
        let waiting = Arc::clone(&acceptor);
        let worker = thread::spawn(move || waiting.wait());
        wait_for_accept_pending(&acceptor);
        drop(stop);
        assert!(matches!(
            worker.join().unwrap().unwrap(),
            super::super::AcceptorReady::Stop
        ));
        assert!(
            acceptor.pending.lock().unwrap().is_none(),
            "pending connect OVERLAPPED was retained after stop"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn stop_cancels_idle_and_partial_reads_after_completion_is_drained() {
        for partial in [false, true] {
            let panel = panel_contract::PanelDir::create().unwrap();
            let listener = bind(panel.path(), panel.id()).unwrap();
            let (stop, acceptor) = acceptor(listener).unwrap();
            let mut client = connect(panel.path(), panel.id()).unwrap();
            acceptor.wait().unwrap();
            let mut server = acceptor.accept().unwrap();
            if partial {
                client.write_all(b"x").unwrap();
            }
            let tracker = Arc::clone(&server.tracker);
            let worker = thread::spawn(move || {
                let mut bytes = [0; 2];
                server.read_exact(&mut bytes)
            });
            wait_for_io_pending(&tracker);
            drop(stop);
            let result = worker.join().unwrap();
            assert_eq!(result.unwrap_err().kind(), io::ErrorKind::Interrupted);
            assert_eq!(tracker.pending.load(Ordering::Acquire), 0);
            assert_eq!(tracker.in_flight.load(Ordering::Acquire), 0);
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn stop_cancels_a_write_to_a_peer_that_is_not_reading() {
        let panel = panel_contract::PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        let listener = bind(panel.path(), panel.id()).unwrap();
        let (stop, acceptor) = acceptor(listener).unwrap();
        let _client = connect(panel.path(), panel.id()).unwrap();
        acceptor.wait().unwrap();
        let mut server = acceptor.accept().unwrap();
        let tracker = Arc::clone(&server.tracker);
        let worker = thread::spawn(move || server.write_all(&vec![b'x'; 16 * 1024 * 1024]));
        wait_for_io_pending(&tracker);
        drop(stop);
        let result = worker.join().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(result.unwrap_err().kind(), io::ErrorKind::Interrupted);
        assert_eq!(tracker.pending.load(Ordering::Acquire), 0);
        assert_eq!(tracker.in_flight.load(Ordering::Acquire), 0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn connect_racing_with_stop_leaves_no_accept_operation_pending() {
        let panel = panel_contract::PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        let listener = bind(panel.path(), panel.id()).unwrap();
        let (stop, acceptor) = acceptor(listener).unwrap();
        let acceptor = Arc::new(acceptor);
        let waiting = Arc::clone(&acceptor);
        let worker = thread::spawn(move || waiting.wait());
        wait_for_accept_pending(&acceptor);
        let client = connect(panel.path(), panel.id()).unwrap();
        drop(stop);
        let outcome = worker.join().unwrap().unwrap();
        if matches!(outcome, super::super::AcceptorReady::Connection) {
            drop(acceptor.accept().unwrap());
        }
        drop(client);
        assert!(
            acceptor.pending.lock().unwrap().is_none(),
            "connect/stop race left a live OVERLAPPED"
        );
    }

    fn wait_for_accept_pending(acceptor: &Acceptor) {
        let deadline = Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(5);
        while !acceptor.pending_ready.load(Ordering::Acquire) {
            assert!(
                Instant::now() < deadline,
                "accept operation never reached pending state"
            );
            thread::yield_now();
        }
    }

    fn wait_for_io_pending(tracker: &IoTracker) {
        let deadline = Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(5);
        while tracker.pending.load(Ordering::Acquire) == 0 {
            assert!(
                Instant::now() < deadline,
                "I/O operation never reached pending state"
            );
            thread::yield_now();
        }
    }

    fn assert_pipe_acl(listener: &Listener) {
        let listener = listener.inner.lock().unwrap();
        let handle = listener.handle.as_ref().unwrap().0;
        let mut dacl = null_mut();
        let mut descriptor = null_mut();
        let result = unsafe {
            GetSecurityInfo(
                handle,
                SE_KERNEL_OBJECT,
                DACL_SECURITY_INFORMATION,
                null_mut(),
                null_mut(),
                &mut dacl,
                null_mut(),
                &mut descriptor,
            )
        };
        assert_eq!(result, ERROR_SUCCESS);
        let descriptor = Security(descriptor);
        let mut control = 0;
        let mut revision = 0;
        assert_ne!(
            unsafe { GetSecurityDescriptorControl(descriptor.0, &mut control, &mut revision) },
            0
        );
        assert_ne!(control & SE_DACL_PROTECTED, 0);
        let mut info = ACL_SIZE_INFORMATION {
            AceCount: 0,
            AclBytesInUse: 0,
            AclBytesFree: 0,
        };
        assert_ne!(
            unsafe {
                GetAclInformation(
                    dacl,
                    (&mut info as *mut ACL_SIZE_INFORMATION).cast(),
                    std::mem::size_of::<ACL_SIZE_INFORMATION>() as u32,
                    AclSizeInformation,
                )
            },
            0
        );
        let sid_text = current_sid_string().unwrap();
        let sid_wide: Vec<u16> = sid_text.encode_utf16().chain(Some(0)).collect();
        let mut user_sid = null_mut();
        assert_ne!(
            unsafe { ConvertStringSidToSidW(sid_wide.as_ptr(), &mut user_sid) },
            0
        );
        let user_sid = Security(user_sid);
        let mut system_sid = [0x00000101u32, 0x05000000, 18];
        let system_sid = system_sid.as_mut_ptr().cast::<std::ffi::c_void>();
        let system_is_user = unsafe { EqualSid(user_sid.0, system_sid) } != 0;
        assert_eq!(info.AceCount, if system_is_user { 1 } else { 2 });
        let mut user_found = false;
        let mut system_found = false;
        for i in 0..info.AceCount {
            let mut ace = null_mut();
            assert_ne!(unsafe { GetAce(dacl, i, &mut ace) }, 0);
            let header = unsafe { &*(ace.cast::<ACE_HEADER>()) };
            let allow = unsafe { &*(ace.cast::<ACCESS_ALLOWED_ACE>()) };
            assert_eq!(header.AceType, 0);
            assert_eq!(header.AceFlags, 0);
            assert_eq!(allow.Mask, FILE_ALL_ACCESS);
            let ace_sid = (&allow.SidStart as *const u32)
                .cast_mut()
                .cast::<std::ffi::c_void>();
            if unsafe { EqualSid(ace_sid, user_sid.0) } != 0 {
                user_found = true;
            } else if unsafe { EqualSid(ace_sid, system_sid) } != 0 {
                system_found = true;
            } else {
                panic!("pipe DACL contains an unexpected principal");
            }
        }
        assert!(user_found);
        assert!(system_is_user || system_found);
    }
}
```

### crates/panel-control/src/lib.rs

```
#![deny(rustdoc::broken_intra_doc_links)]
//! **A second transport for the panel document, and not a second contract.**
//!
//! Since August 2026 `panel.json` is *a* transport and not *the* file: the contract is the document
//! and the source of truth is the state the application holds per pane (**D17**). This crate is what
//! makes that sentence true instead of aspirational — a producer that would rather speak than write
//! connects to its pane's endpoint and publishes, and the document it sends goes through the same
//! parser, the same ceiling and the same tolerance as the one on disk.
//!
//! **The file stays the default, and nothing here changes that.** Its barrier to entry is zero: a
//! shell script with `printf` publishes a panel. What this adds is for the producer that already has
//! a process running inside the pane and would rather not own a file — an agent, mainly — and for
//! whom the file's one real weakness bites: **the file does not know who writes it**, so two
//! producers are settled by the operating system's scheduling. A connection does know, which is what
//! lets a source *hold* a pane and be the only writer while it does.
//!
//! # What it deliberately does not carry
//!
//! **No pane identifier, on any message.** The pane a call acts on is the endpoint the connection
//! opened. `PANEL_ID` is in every descendant's environment and any sibling of this user reads it out
//! of `/proc/<pid>/environ`, so it is a name and not a credential; a field carrying it would be a
//! field somebody starts trusting, and the check that validates it is what a later refactor drops.
//!
//! OpenCode may also send a strict `agent_signal` v1 snapshot on its pane endpoint.
//! This independent connection does not claim the panel and is not an MCP tool; its
//! connection identity and disconnect are delivered to the application for arbitration.
//!
//! # What this build serves
//!
//! The six verbs of the frozen wire format: `panel_set`, `panel_get`, `panel_patch`, and the three
//! `split_*` that let a connection **open a console of its own, read it and close it**. The three
//! panel verbs need nobody else — their work stays with the pane — and the three split verbs travel
//! to the window, which is the only party with a tree: a console is named by an opaque
//! handle scoped to the connection that opened it, and the person's first byte into it revokes the
//! handle (**D44**). All six are advertised, because all six are answered.

pub mod client;
pub mod codex_relay;
pub mod codex_route;
pub mod diagnostic_peer;
pub mod endpoint;
pub mod listener;
pub mod session;
#[cfg(windows)]
pub mod windows_services;
pub mod wire;

pub use client::{Answered, Producer};
pub use diagnostic_peer::DiagnosticPeer;
pub use listener::{
    Answer, Channel, Connection, Delivery, OverlayAuthority, PublicationInspector,
    PublicationOrigin, PublishHandler, ReadDocument, What,
};
#[cfg(windows)]
pub use windows_services::{
    DocumentPublisher, DocumentReader, DocumentSnapshot, PlanPublisher, PlanReader, PlanSnapshot,
    ReleaseRegistry, ReleaseToken, SnapshotReadMetrics, WindowsServices, WindowsWake,
};
pub use wire::{Activity, AgentSnapshot, MAX_REQUEST, Reply, Request, Side};
```

### crates/panel-control/src/session/helper.rs

```
//! Bounded CLI input translation and one request/reply exchange over the inherited endpoint.

use std::io::{self, Read, Write};
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

#[cfg(not(windows))]
use interprocess::local_socket::prelude::*;
use serde::Deserialize;

use super::{Client, MAX_HOOK_INPUT, MAX_REPLY, Request};

#[derive(Deserialize)]
struct HookInput {
    session_id: String,
    cwd: String,
    #[serde(alias = "event")]
    hook_event_name: String,
    source: Option<String>,
}

/// Official hook input can contain transcript and prompt fields. Only these four keys survive.
pub fn hook_request(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Request, String> {
    if bytes.len() > MAX_HOOK_INPUT {
        return Err("hook input goes over the ceiling".into());
    }
    let input: HookInput =
        serde_json::from_slice(bytes).map_err(|_| "invalid Codex hook metadata")?;
    let source = match (input.source, input.hook_event_name.as_str()) {
        (Some(source), _) => source,
        (None, "SessionEnd") => "exit".to_owned(),
        _ => return Err("Codex hook source is missing".into()),
    };
    let request = Request::Observe {
        client: Client::Codex,
        session_id: input.session_id,
        cwd: input.cwd,
        event: input.hook_event_name,
        source,
    };
    request.validate()?;
    Ok(request)
}

#[derive(Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct UpdateInput {
    sequence: u64,
    session_id: Option<String>,
    cwd: Option<String>,
    challenge: Option<String>,
}

pub fn update_request(generation: String, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Request, String> {
    if bytes.len() > super::MAX_REQUEST {
        return Err("session input goes over the ceiling".into());
    }
    let input: UpdateInput =
        serde_json::from_slice(bytes).map_err(|_| "invalid session update metadata")?;
    let request = Request::Update {
        generation,
        sequence: input.sequence,
        session_id: input.session_id,
        cwd: input.cwd,
        challenge: input.challenge,
    };
    request.validate()?;
    Ok(request)
}

/// Validates and compacts a hook panel document before any endpoint connection is attempted.
pub fn publish_document(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<String, String> {
    if bytes.len() as u64 > panel_contract::MAX_BYTES {
        return Err(format!(
            "publication input goes over {} bytes",
            panel_contract::MAX_BYTES
        ));
    }
    let value: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_slice(bytes)
        .map_err(|_| "publication input is not valid JSON".to_owned())?;
    if !value.is_object() {
        return Err("publication input must be a JSON object".to_owned());
    }
    let compact = serde_json::to_string(&value).map_err(|_| "cannot encode publication input")?;
    if compact.len() as u64 > panel_contract::MAX_BYTES {
        return Err(format!(
            "publication document goes over {} bytes",
            panel_contract::MAX_BYTES
        ));
    }
    Ok(compact)
}

/// Opens only the endpoint inherited by this pane. No arbitrary endpoint or pane selector exists.
pub fn exchange(request: &Request) -> io::Result<serde_json::Value> {
    #[cfg(not(windows))]
    {
        let endpoint = inherited_endpoint()?;
        exchange_at(&request.line().map_err(io::Error::other)?, endpoint)
    }
    #[cfg(windows)]
    {
        let stream = inherited_stream()?;
        exchange_stream(
            &request.line().map_err(io::Error::other)?,
            stream,
            Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(2),
        )
    }
}

/// Exchanges a prebuilt, bounded newline JSON request over this pane's inherited endpoint.
pub fn exchange_line(line: &[u8]) -> io::Result<serde_json::Value> {
    #[cfg(not(windows))]
    {
        exchange_at(line, inherited_endpoint()?)
    }
    #[cfg(windows)]
    {
        let deadline = Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(2);
        exchange_stream(line, inherited_stream()?, deadline)
    }
}

#[cfg(windows)]
fn inherited_stream() -> io::Result<crate::endpoint::Stream> {
    let [id, dir] = panel_contract::ENV_NAMES.map(std::env::var_os);
    let (id, dir) =
        crate::client::whole_identity(id.as_deref(), dir.as_deref()).ok_or_else(|| {
            io::Error::new(
                io::ErrorKind::NotFound,
                "this helper is not running inside an Ivy pane",
            )
        })?;
    crate::endpoint::private_to_this_user(dir)?;
    crate::endpoint::connect_authenticated(dir, id, crate::client::inherited_server_pid()?)
}

#[cfg(not(windows))]
fn inherited_endpoint() -> io::Result<interprocess::local_socket::Name<'static>> {
    let outside = || {
        io::Error::new(
            io::ErrorKind::NotFound,
            "this helper is not running inside an Ivy pane",
        )
    };
    let [id, dir] = panel_contract::ENV_NAMES.map(std::env::var_os);
    let (id, dir) =
        crate::client::whole_identity(id.as_deref(), dir.as_deref()).ok_or_else(outside)?;
    crate::endpoint::private_to_this_user(dir)?;
    crate::endpoint::name_of(dir, id)
}

#[cfg(not(windows))]
fn exchange_at(
    line: &[u8],
    endpoint: interprocess::local_socket::Name<'static>,
) -> io::Result<serde_json::Value> {
    let deadline = Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(2);
    let stream = interprocess::local_socket::ConnectOptions::new()
        .name(endpoint)
        .wait_mode(interprocess::ConnectWaitMode::Timeout(remaining(deadline)?))
        .connect_sync()?;
    exchange_stream(line, stream, deadline)
}

fn exchange_stream(
    line: &[u8],
    mut stream: crate::endpoint::Stream,
    deadline: Instant,
) -> io::Result<serde_json::Value> {
    let mut unwritten = line;
    while !unwritten.is_empty() {
        stream.set_send_timeout(Some(remaining(deadline)?))?;
        match stream.write(unwritten) {
            Ok(0) => {
                return Err(io::Error::new(
                    io::ErrorKind::WriteZero,
                    "session endpoint stopped reading",
                ));
            }
            Ok(written) => unwritten = &unwritten[written..],
            Err(failure) if failure.kind() == io::ErrorKind::Interrupted => continue,
            Err(failure) => return Err(failure),
        }
    }
    let mut bytes = Vec::new();
    let mut chunk = [0; 1024];
    loop {
        stream.set_recv_timeout(Some(remaining(deadline)?))?;
        let read = match stream.read(&mut chunk) {
            Ok(0) => {
                return Err(io::Error::new(
                    io::ErrorKind::UnexpectedEof,
                    "session endpoint closed without a reply",
                ));
            }
            Ok(read) => read,
            Err(failure) if failure.kind() == io::ErrorKind::Interrupted => continue,
            Err(failure) => return Err(failure),
        };
        let end = chunk[..read].iter().position(|byte| *byte == b'\n');
        let content = end.unwrap_or(read);
        if bytes.len() + content > MAX_REPLY {
            return Err(io::Error::other("session reply goes over the ceiling"));
        }
        bytes.extend_from_slice(&chunk[..content]);
        if end.is_some() {
            break;
        }
    }
    let reply: serde_json::Value =
        serde_json::from_slice(&bytes).map_err(|_| io::Error::other("invalid session reply"))?;
    if !reply.is_object() || !reply["ok"].is_boolean() {
        return Err(io::Error::other("invalid session reply"));
    }
    Ok(reply)
}

fn remaining(deadline: Instant) -> io::Result<Duration> {
    deadline
        .checked_duration_since(Instant::now())
        .filter(|duration| !duration.is_zero())
        .ok_or_else(|| io::Error::new(io::ErrorKind::TimedOut, "session exchange timed out"))
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod publish_tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn publish_input_requires_object_and_compacts_with_byte_ceilings() {
        assert_eq!(
            publish_document(b"{\n \"lines\": [\"a\"]\n}").unwrap(),
            r#"{"lines":["a"]}"#
        );
        assert_eq!(
            publish_document(b"[]").unwrap_err(),
            "publication input must be a JSON object"
        );
        assert_eq!(
            publish_document(b"{broken").unwrap_err(),
            "publication input is not valid JSON"
        );
        assert!(publish_document(&vec![b' '; panel_contract::MAX_BYTES as usize + 1]).is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn publish_silence_gate_requires_a_whole_identity_and_supported_endpoint() {
        use crate::client::whole_identity;
        use std::ffi::OsStr;
        assert!(whole_identity(None, None).is_none());
        assert!(whole_identity(Some(OsStr::new("pane")), None).is_none());
        assert!(whole_identity(Some(OsStr::new("pane")), Some(OsStr::new("relative"))).is_none());
        assert!(whole_identity(Some(OsStr::new("pane")), Some(OsStr::new("/tmp/panel"))).is_some());
    }
}
```

### crates/panel-control/src/session/peer.rs

```
//! Identity captured from the accepted socket, before any request bytes are interpreted.

use std::io;
#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
use std::os::fd::{AsFd, AsRawFd, FromRawFd, OwnedFd};
#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
use std::sync::Arc;
#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
use std::{
    os::fd::{AsFd, AsRawFd, FromRawFd, OwnedFd},
    sync::Arc,
};

use crate::endpoint::Stream;

/// An unforgeable connected-process witness. Payload fields can never construct one.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct Peer {
    pid: u32,
    birth: u64,
    #[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
    lifetime: Arc<OwnedFd>,
    #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
    lifetime: Arc<OwnedFd>,
}

impl Peer {
    pub fn pid(&self) -> u32 {
        self.pid
    }
    pub fn birth(&self) -> u64 {
        self.birth
    }

    /// The exact kernel pidfd acquired with SO_PEERPIDFD, rather than reopened from a numeric PID.
    #[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
    pub fn lifetime(&self) -> Arc<OwnedFd> {
        Arc::clone(&self.lifetime)
    }

    pub fn is_alive(&self) -> bool {
        #[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
        {
            let mut descriptor = libc::pollfd {
                fd: self.lifetime.as_raw_fd(),
                events: libc::POLLIN,
                revents: 0,
            };
            // SAFETY: the owned descriptor and writable one-element array live through this call.
            let ready = unsafe { libc::poll(&mut descriptor, 1, 0) };
            ready == 0 && descriptor.revents == 0
        }
        #[cfg(not(target_os = "linux"))]
        {
            #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
            {
                let mut p = libc::pollfd {
                    fd: self.lifetime.as_raw_fd(),
                    events: libc::POLLIN,
                    revents: 0,
                };
                unsafe { libc::poll(&mut p, 1, 0) == 0 && p.revents == 0 }
            }
            #[cfg(not(target_os = "macos"))]
            {
                false
            }
        }
    }

    pub(crate) fn capture(stream: &Stream) -> io::Result<Self> {
        #[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
        {
            let Stream::UdSocket(stream) = stream;
            let socket = stream.as_fd().as_raw_fd();
            let credentials: libc::ucred = socket_option(socket, libc::SO_PEERCRED)?;
            if credentials.pid <= 0 {
                return Err(io::Error::other("invalid socket peer identity"));
            }
            let descriptor: libc::c_int = socket_option(socket, libc::SO_PEERPIDFD)?;
            if descriptor < 0 {
                return Err(io::Error::other("missing socket peer lifetime"));
            }
            // SAFETY: SO_PEERPIDFD returns a newly owned close-on-exec descriptor on success.
            let lifetime = Arc::new(unsafe { OwnedFd::from_raw_fd(descriptor) });
            let mut peer = Self {
                pid: credentials.pid as u32,
                birth: 0,
                lifetime,
            };
            if !peer.is_alive() {
                return Err(io::Error::other("socket peer has already exited"));
            }
            peer.birth = birth_of(peer.pid)?;
            // If the peer exited while /proc was read, its numeric PID cannot identify a successor.
            if !peer.is_alive() {
                return Err(io::Error::other("socket peer exited during verification"));
            }
            Ok(peer)
        }
        #[cfg(not(target_os = "linux"))]
        {
            #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
            {
                return capture_macos(stream);
            }
            #[cfg(not(target_os = "macos"))]
            {
                let _ = stream;
                Err(io::Error::new(
                    io::ErrorKind::Unsupported,
                    "verified session peers require Linux",
                ))
            }
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
#[repr(C)]
#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
struct AuditToken {
    val: [u32; 8],
}
#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
fn capture_macos(stream: &Stream) -> io::Result<Peer> {
    let Stream::UdSocket(stream) = stream;
    let fd = stream.as_fd().as_raw_fd();
    let token: AuditToken = socket_option_macos(fd, 6 /* LOCAL_PEERTOKEN */)?;
    let pid = token.val[5];
    let version = token.val[7];
    if pid == 0 || version == 0 {
        return Err(io::Error::other("invalid local peer token"));
    }
    let birth = unique_identity(pid, version)?;
    let lifetime = Arc::new(macos_lifetime(pid, version, birth)?);
    let peer = Peer {
        pid,
        birth,
        lifetime,
    };
    if !peer.is_alive() || unique_identity(pid, version)? != birth || !peer.is_alive() {
        return Err(io::Error::other(
            "peer identity changed during verification",
        ));
    }
    Ok(peer)
}
#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
fn socket_option_macos<T: Copy>(fd: libc::c_int, option: libc::c_int) -> io::Result<T> {
    let mut value = std::mem::MaybeUninit::<T>::uninit();
    let mut len = std::mem::size_of::<T>() as libc::socklen_t;
    if unsafe { libc::getsockopt(fd, 0, option, value.as_mut_ptr().cast(), &mut len) } < 0 {
        return Err(io::Error::last_os_error());
    }
    if len as usize != std::mem::size_of::<T>() {
        return Err(io::Error::other("invalid local peer token size"));
    }
    Ok(unsafe { value.assume_init() })
}
#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
#[repr(C)]
struct UniqueIdentity {
    uuid: [u8; 16],
    unique: u64,
    parent_unique: u64,
    pidversion: i32,
    parent_pidversion: i32,
    reserve2: u64,
    reserve3: u64,
}
#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
#[link(name = "proc")]
unsafe extern "C" {
    fn proc_pidinfo(pid: i32, flavor: i32, arg: u64, buf: *mut libc::c_void, size: i32) -> i32;
    fn kqueue() -> i32;
    fn kevent(
        kq: i32,
        changelist: *const libc::kevent,
        nchanges: i32,
        eventlist: *mut libc::kevent,
        nevents: i32,
        timeout: *const libc::timespec,
    ) -> i32;
}
#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
fn unique_identity(pid: u32, version: u32) -> io::Result<u64> {
    let mut bsd = [0u8; 136];
    let n = unsafe { proc_pidinfo(pid as i32, 3, 0, bsd.as_mut_ptr().cast(), bsd.len() as i32) };
    if n != bsd.len() as i32 {
        return Err(io::Error::other("peer PID identity unavailable"));
    }
    let queried_pid = unsafe { std::ptr::read_unaligned(bsd.as_ptr().cast::<u32>().add(3)) };
    if queried_pid != pid {
        return Err(io::Error::other(
            "queried PID does not match local peer token",
        ));
    }
    let mut info = std::mem::MaybeUninit::<UniqueIdentity>::uninit();
    let size = std::mem::size_of::<UniqueIdentity>() as i32;
    let n = unsafe { proc_pidinfo(pid as i32, 17, 0, info.as_mut_ptr().cast(), size) };
    if n != size {
        return Err(io::Error::other("process unique identity unavailable"));
    };
    let i = unsafe { info.assume_init() };
    if i.pidversion < 0 || i.pidversion as u32 != version || i.unique == 0 {
        return Err(io::Error::other(
            "local peer token does not match process identity",
        ));
    };
    Ok(i.unique)
}
#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
fn macos_lifetime(pid: u32, version: u32, birth: u64) -> io::Result<OwnedFd> {
    let fd = unsafe { kqueue() };
    if fd < 0 {
        return Err(io::Error::last_os_error());
    };
    let q = unsafe { OwnedFd::from_raw_fd(fd) };
    let ev = libc::kevent {
        ident: pid as _,
        filter: -5,
        flags: 1 | 4,
        fflags: 0x80000000 | 0x20000000,
        data: 0,
        udata: std::ptr::null_mut(),
    };
    if unsafe { kevent(fd, &ev, 1, std::ptr::null_mut(), 0, std::ptr::null()) } < 0
        || unique_identity(pid, version)? != birth
    {
        return Err(io::Error::other("peer exited during lifetime registration"));
    };
    Ok(q)
}

#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
fn socket_option<T: Copy>(socket: libc::c_int, option: libc::c_int) -> io::Result<T> {
    let mut value = std::mem::MaybeUninit::<T>::uninit();
    let mut length = std::mem::size_of::<T>() as libc::socklen_t;
    // SAFETY: the uninitialized output has precisely the capacity supplied to getsockopt.
    let result = unsafe {
        libc::getsockopt(
            socket,
            libc::SOL_SOCKET,
            option,
            value.as_mut_ptr().cast(),
            &mut length,
        )
    };
    if result < 0 {
        return Err(io::Error::last_os_error());
    }
    if length as usize != std::mem::size_of::<T>() {
        return Err(io::Error::other("invalid socket credential size"));
    }
    // SAFETY: getsockopt succeeded and confirmed that it initialized exactly T's byte width.
    Ok(unsafe { value.assume_init() })
}

#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
fn birth_of(pid: u32) -> io::Result<u64> {
    use std::io::Read;
    let file = std::fs::File::open(format!("/proc/{pid}/stat"))?;
    let mut bytes = Vec::new();
    file.take(4097).read_to_end(&mut bytes)?;
    if bytes.len() > 4096 {
        return Err(io::Error::other("process identity goes over the ceiling"));
    }
    let text =
        std::str::from_utf8(&bytes).map_err(|_| io::Error::other("invalid process identity"))?;
    text.rsplit_once(')')
        .and_then(|(_, fields)| fields.split_whitespace().nth(19))
        .and_then(|birth| birth.parse().ok())
        .ok_or_else(|| io::Error::other("missing process birth identity"))
}
```
